# DESIGN.md — syntek-media

**Last Updated**: 24/10/2026 **Version**: 0.5.0 **Maintained By**: Syntek Studio
**Language**: British English (en_GB)

The build contract for `syntek-media`, a Copier template that adds audio and video production to a repository, in three brand kinds:
`business` (a business's social video, recorded talks and explainers), `author-fiction` (book trailers, audiobook and author socials for a novelist) and `author-nonfiction` (talks, podcast, audiobook and author socials for a non-fiction author).
It is built to be applied on top of a project generated by its sibling `syntek-author`, whose conventions it copies exactly, and it follows the house Copier idiom of `syntek-base`.

This file is the template repository's own design record. It sits outside `template/`, so it is never rendered.
Where this file and the convention maps disagree, **this file wins**. A decision syntek-media inherits unchanged cites syntek-author's number — "(syntek-author D23)" — and binds in the wording syntek-author gives it; a departure is marked **(overrides syntek-author Dn)** with its reason.

**Baseline.** "syntek-author" in this file means its working tree as read on 03/10/2026: commit `0ab33c5` (the v0.1.0 release) plus the uncommitted v0.2.0 work its own DESIGN.md records as D39–D44 — the project-owned `00-project.md` seed, the business document families, workflows cited by full folder name, and no tooling that reads what Git ignores — with 51 skills. Each release since names its own baseline in `CHANGELOG.md`: 0.2.0 and 0.2.1 were checked against `9a65902` (syntek-author's v0.3.0 release, read on 04/10/2026), from which `syntek-author-names.txt` was refreshed at 0.2.0; 0.2.2 against the same commit, read again on 05/10/2026; and the 0.3.0 work found syntek-author still at that commit, with a clean working tree, on 05/10/2026.
That tree is still being edited. Every builder re-reads syntek-author's DESIGN.md, and the file it is copying, at build time, and records any drift that changes this contract in `CHANGELOG.md`.
The names media must never take are frozen in `.github/scripts/syntek-author-names.txt` (D27), at the baseline its header names (now `21d7253`, syntek-author v0.8.0, read 28/10/2026, with 52 skills); the over-author renders and `coexist-test.sh` snapshot whatever tree `--author` names, and fail when its names differ from that file.

**Source material** (read-only; never modify):

| Abbreviation | Source |
|---|---|
| SA | `syntek-author` (`gh:Syntek-Dev/syntek-author`) — the sibling writing template whose conventions this one copies, and whose projects it is applied to |
| SB | `syntek-base` (`gh:Syntek-Dev/syntek-base`) — the house Copier idiom |

The convention maps, probes, orchestrator rulings (R1–R19) and design reviews written during the build are kept outside the repository. Every decision that settles a ruling or a review finding names it, with the evidence that motivated it.

**The 0.2.0 work** (released as 0.2.0 on 04/10/2026; 0.2.1, the same day, changed only the audits; 0.2.2, on 05/10/2026, fixed the toolkit, as D49 and Sections 4.2, 6.4 and 7 record, and `copier.yml`). D53–D63 add three platforms for the brand's own channels — `website`, `blog` and `newsletter` — and a podcast self-hosted on the brand's own site, delivered from its feed to Apple Podcasts and Spotify and uploaded to YouTube as video. Their evidence is the maintainer's approved change and requirement (04/10/2026) and three research notes of 04/10/2026, kept outside the repository like the build's maps: the web delivery specs (cited below as 'web-specs research'), the ownership boundary between media and the written side ('boundary research'), and podcast self-hosting ('podcast-hosting research'). Sections 2–11 carry the change where each owns it.

**The 0.3.0 work (released 06/10/2026).** D64–D72 add a pipeline for a piece whose picture is drawn as code over its generated voice, and tidy where every piece's output lands: per-piece output folders where Git ignores them, and two tracked folders for timing and scene files (D64, needing no migration, D65); a working copy first and a tracked file only through `-o` for every tool-written timing and scene file (D66); word timing with WhisperX and lip sync with Rhubarb, both local and spending no credits (D67, D68); small text captures as a scene's real sources (D69); a neutral scene kit and `scene.py`, which checks a scene in stills and renders it frame by frame, a master per aspect (D70, D71); and an opt-in memory cap for heavy renders (D72). M4 gains four dated sub-checks for a scene piece and the brief two fixed sections (D17, Section 6.1); the gated audiobook output folders take the same `/**` and `README.md` pair as every other (D19, Sections 3.5 and 10); and D19 and Section 4.2 record what 0.2.2 shipped without saying: every output folder excluded at any depth (`shipped-brands.sh` check 12) and `assemble`'s streamed stills. Their evidence is the maintainer's decisions on the per-video pipeline brief and that brief's verification (both 05/10/2026), kept outside the repository like the build's maps. Sections 3–11 carry the change where each owns it.

**The 0.5.0 work (D80).** Media adopts the rest of syntek-author's family-shared set from its `v0.5.0` tag, byte for byte: the seven working-practice skills, the two handoff hooks with their pair and the `handoffs/` pair (syntek-author D51); the Gemini CLI and OpenCode files and the client gates of `AGENT_CLIENTS` (syntek-author D47); the two MCP servers and their rules (syntek-author D54); the permissions script (syntek-author D49); the data-root resolver (syntek-author D50, D53), beside a map of media's own that claims no folder; and the shared questions and web-tools rules. Media's own `family-shared.json` records every vendored byte and its audits hold the tree to it. Its evidence is syntek-author's release package for media (22/10/2026) and the maintainer's decisions of 23/10/2026 (the D80 grilling round), kept outside the repository like the build's maps. Sections 2–11 carry the change where each owns it.

**The 0.6.0 work (D81, D84).** Media re-vendors every family file and block from syntek-author's `v0.8.0` tag, byte for byte, forty-three files and four blocks where it held twenty-eight and three (D81): the update script, `migrations/family-update.py`, with the unversioned entry of its 'update' block and its copy-only `baseline` task, so that every update reports what it did not deliver, refreshes the listed family-shared files the owner never changed and offers the listed settings entries, top-level values and MCP servers to media's copy-only settings and MCP files once each, media's own safety rules among them, which supersedes D11 for those paths (syntek-author D51, D52); the compaction follow-ups (syntek-author D29); and the 'questions' block, whose coding-agent defaults now read syntek-author's record too (syntek-author D47). Python 3.14 becomes media's base, as the vendored scripts require, with `toolkit/transcribe.py` the one exception among media's tools (D20, D67; media's own migration scripts keep to D85). The user guide, `how-to/`, ships with it (D84; syntek-author D56): a read-only, shared reference folder, shared file by file, whose thirteen family files, with the guide guard beside them in `.claude/hooks/`, are copy-only and refreshed and whose media files sit only under three `syntek-media/` folders, put back on every full update; a combined project needs syntek-author v0.6.0 or later, updated before media. Its evidence is syntek-author's `v0.6.0` release package and its answer to media's decisions (27/10/2026: no conflict, with conditions, each checked that day against a clean clone of that tag), syntek-author's `v0.7.0` of the same day, whose family files differ from `v0.6.0`'s only in the update script, which no longer offers a hook entry that names no script (syntek-author D51), syntek-author's `v0.8.0` of 28/10/2026, whose family files differ from `v0.7.0`'s only in the update script, and the maintainer's decisions of 25, 26, 27 and 28/10/2026, kept outside the repository like the build's maps. Sections 2–11 carry the change where each owns it.

---

## 1. Decisions

| # | Decision | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | **`_subdirectory: template`.** Everything that ships lives at its real path under `template/`. The repository root holds the template's own state: `copier.yml`, this file, the user guide, the development manual, the audits and the example answers. | syntek-author D1. |
| D2 | **House Copier idiom:** `_min_copier_version: "9.12.0"`, `_templates_suffix: ""`, `_preserve_symlinks: true`, `_envops` delimiters `<% %>` / `<: :>` / `<~ ~>` with `keep_trailing_newline: true`, anchored `_exclude` patterns, one templated `_exclude` line per gated path, UPPER_SNAKE questions, `>-` help. The floor is 9.12.0 since 0.4.5 (D85), and syntek-author D2's too since its `v0.6.0` (D81). It is at least 9.6.0 because the copy-only shared files (D11) and the seed-once examples read `_copier_operation`, which older Copier lacks: there the copy-only tasks hold shut and the update gates hold OPEN, so a shared file or an example the author deleted is recreated on the next update — a shared one inside syntek-author's project (probes b3, b4: `_skip_if_exists` still keeps an existing file, so nothing is re-rendered over the project's own). It is 9.12.0 because the earlier releases measured fail media's own update: on 9.6.0 every update fails as Copier replays a multiselect answer (`ValueError: Invalid choice`), and 9.10.3 and 9.11.0 refuse an `.agents` link outside the project (`ForbiddenPathError`) before D76's hold stage runs; from 9.12.0 Copier also passes each path of an update's diff that matches `_skip_if_exists` to `git apply` as `--exclude`, so a stopped update never puts `/.agents` back (D76). `multiselect` predates the floor (proven on 9.18.2), and so does `UNSET`, which the family 'questions' block reads (it first exists in Copier 9.11.0, syntek-author D2's floor at its `v0.5.0`; D80). Every text artefact media ships — HTML and CSS templates, ffmpeg filter strings, SRT, TOML, `@dsCard` comments — is scanned for `<%`, `<:` and `<~`; none may ship. | syntek-author D2, whose floor is 9.12.0 since its `v0.6.0` and at its `v0.7.0` (its `DESIGN.md`:31; D81). Copier-idioms map Section 1; probes a1–a7, b1–b4 (Copier 9.18.2). The floor's reason corrected after the build (04/10/2026): probes b3/b4 showed recreation, not re-rendering. The floor raised from 9.6.0 to 9.12.0 on 22/10/2026 (D85), on `update-test.sh` runs with `COPIER_CMD` pinned to each release. |
| D3 | **Answers file `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`**, rendered from `template/.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml` (`<% _copier_answers\|to_nice_yaml -%>` under syntek-author's header comment with the name swapped). **Every `copier update` passes `-a .copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`** (without it Copier stops and changes nothing); `copier copy` takes an absolute template path or `gh:Syntek-Dev/syntek-media` (a relative `_src_path` breaks every later update). **Every shown copy and update command runs Copier as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier`** (amended by D81; syntek-author D3), quoted, since unquoted the shell reads `>` as a redirection: `uvx` may otherwise run Copier on an older Python it already has, on which the family's update and outside-link scripts, run on the Python that runs Copier, do nothing but print their warning (D81, D85). | syntek-author D3, which at its `v0.7.0`, as since its `v0.6.0` (its `DESIGN.md`:32), also asks for the Python every shown command runs Copier on. BRIEF coexistence rule 1. Probes c2a, i1. |
| D4 | **syntek-media is applied on top of a syntek-author project** — the intended use for all three brands: syntek-author first (business → `business`, author-fiction → `fiction`, author-nonfiction → `theology`), then syntek-media. **It still renders and works on its own**, degraded: no writing companions (D10), no writing router, no project settings file. The family's working practice ships from media too, byte for byte as syntek-author ships it (D80): the seven family-shared skills, `handoff` among them, the PreCompact and context-threshold hooks with their pair, and the `handoffs/` pair. Every audit of a standalone render passes. Nothing media ships assumes syntek-author is present: skills test for a companion's `SKILL.md`, and rules for syntek-author's `00-project.md`, at run time, and the only render-time reads of syntek-author's answers file, by the family questions' defaults and validators and by `_message_after_update`, find nothing where it is absent and fall back to their own defaults (D14, D80). Since 0.6.0 a combined project needs syntek-author v0.6.0 or later, updated before media (amended by D84; syntek-author D56): a copy-only task warns where syntek-author's answers file stands without its guide files, and media's crossing report names syntek-author's guide only where that file exists, each checking files at run time, so neither assumes syntek-author is there. | Ruling R1. Claude-shape map Q1; root-docs map Q1; audits map Q1. |
| D5 | **Template instructions live in `.claude/rules/syntek-media/`**: eight template-owned files numbered `01`–`08` after syntek-author's (Section 4.1), each ≤ 300 lines and together about 1,000 (accepted at the build: the Requirement/Why pairs of about fifty sections and the required tables need it; with syntek-author's roughly 1,350, a combined project loads about 2,350), with no `paths` frontmatter and no pair in the folder. Claude Code loads every `.claude/rules/**/*.md` without `paths` at launch with the same priority as `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, so they load beside syntek-author's rules in a combined project. **`.claude/CLAUDE.md` is never media's to update.** What every session must know about the brand is rendered from the answers into `01-layout-and-routing.md` Section 1, which is template-owned, so a re-answered `BRAND_NAME` or platform list arrives with the next update **(overrides syntek-author D40, whose brief lives in the project-owned seed `00-project.md`)**. Media ships no settings file of its own. In a combined project syntek-author's `00-project.md` — read by Claude at run time, where present, never through Copier — outranks media's rules as it outranks syntek-author's: media follows its `## Paths` (project rules, handoffs), `## Memory headings`, `## Workflow aliases` (a row naming a media workflow by full folder path) and `## Overrides` (a row naming a media rules file and section). Standalone, project rules go in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, under the heading 'Project-specific rules'. Since 0.6.0 a departure from media's guide under `how-to/` is recorded in the same places, as an `## Overrides` or `## Workflow aliases` row where `00-project.md` is present, otherwise under that heading, because the guide itself is read-only (D84; syntek-author D56). Every rules file opens with syntek-author's current blockquote, adapted to say exactly this (Section 4.1). | Ruling R4 (the rendered brand section). syntek-author D4, D40, D41. Tools-research map 5a (Claude Code memory docs, fetched 03/10/2026); claude-shape map Q3. Conventions review (03/10/2026): the two templates' always-loaded rules must tell one story about where project values and overrides live. |
| D6 | **Skills only.** No `.claude/agents/`, no `.claude/commands/`; `context: fork` where a subagent is wanted. | syntek-author D5. |
| D7 | **Mode files `BUSINESS.md` · `FICTION.md` · `NONFICTION.md`**, gated `BRAND_KIND == 'business'` / `BRAND_KIND == 'author-fiction'` / `BRAND_KIND == 'author-nonfiction'`. A moded `SKILL.md` is byte-identical in every brand kind and carries syntek-author's mode paragraph verbatim with the three names swapped (Section 5); every mode file has the four H2s `## Paths and unit` · `## Additions to the steps` · `## Domain rules` · `## Examples`. A mode file applies only when its `SKILL.md` carries the Mode paragraph, so a project's own skill of the same name ignores it. Theology-specific practice (scripture translation permissions for audio, reading scripture aloud) lives as examples inside `NONFICTION.md`, never as a fourth kind. **In the five family-shared skill folders syntek-author writes mode files for** (`grill-with-docs`, `grilling`, `handoff`, `to-questionnaire`, `wayfinder`) **media's mode files are `MEDIA-BUSINESS.md` · `MEDIA-FICTION.md` · `MEDIA-NONFICTION.md`** (amended by D80; syntek-author D51's names, so neither template ships a mode file under the other's), with the same three gates, in the same generated block of `_exclude`, template-owned beside the copy-only `SKILL.md`. Each has the four H2s and is **routing only**: each section points to media's paths and unit for that skill and to the media procedure, guide or rules section that governs the work, and restates none of them. The shared `SKILL.md` carries the family's neutral Mode paragraph, never this one (Section 5.3): it reads the mode files the project's rules route the work to, and media's rules route media work to `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` (rules 02 Section 2), so syntek-author's own mode files beside them apply only to its work. `grill-me` and `wait-what` take none. | Ruling R7. syntek-author D6, D41. Skills map 8.2. |
| D8 | **Layers keep the user's names:** `brand/`, `scripts/`, `production/` and `publishing/` (production layers: `docs/`, `src/`, `workflows/`), and `toolkit/` (supporting, flat). None collides with a syntek-author path. The cost of `scripts/` is recorded: it reads like a folder of shell scripts and sits beside `.github/scripts/` in this repository, so every document says "the scripts layer" or names `scripts/src/pieces/`, never "the scripts". `brand/` never duplicates syntek-author's business `standards/brand/` seeds: the written voice and the brand guide's words stay there, cited "where present"; `brand/` holds the visual tokens and the spoken, on-screen voice. Nor does media duplicate syntek-author's social-media family (D48). **`how-to/` is neither a production layer nor a media layer name** (amended by D84): it is the family's shared reference folder, the user guide, read and never produced in, shared with syntek-author file by file, with `docs/`, `src/` and `workflows/` but no seed, no `docs/project/` and no `workflows/local/` (syntek-author D56). | Ruling R8. Root-docs map Q5; claude-shape map R16–R17. |
| D9 | **Media ships its own router, `run-media-workflow`** (unmoded). Its description is scoped to media work — video, audio, podcast, trailer, audiobook, captions, thumbnails, posting — and its boundaries name "the writing router of syntek-author, where present" and "the social-media-documents skill (syntek-author), where present" without assuming either exists. It checks syntek-author's `00-project.md` `## Workflow aliases` first, where present, then each layer's `workflows/local/`, then the template's workflows; it lists a layer's workflow folders directly when that layer's index has no "You want to… \| Procedure" table. Rules 01 Section 2 lists the layers it reads. | Ruling R3. syntek-author D41. Skills map 5.3: syntek-author's `run-workflow` reads only the layers listed in its own rules 01 Section 1, so it never sees media's. |
| D10 | **Companion skills are syntek-author's and never ship here.** Media files name them **unbackticked**, never in `skills:` frontmatter, in one fixed form — "the spelling skill (syntek-author), where present" — and a skill checks `.claude/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md` at run time before naming the step. Rules 02 Section 4 lists each companion and how it applies to media text (Section 5.2): spelling, grammar, comprehension, flow and fact-check on scripts, transcripts and post copy, as reports; improve-section and adapt-section on a script only as report-then-agreed-edit, **with no ledger entry** (the provenance ledger belongs to syntek-author's content layer); research as working practice; social-media-documents as the owner of the written social plan (D48). `doc-references.sh` check 5 enforces the form. **Four former companions ship from media since 0.5.0** (amended by D80): `grilling`, `grill-with-docs`, `handoff` and `wayfinder` are family-shared skills (syntek-author D51), vendored with `grill-me`, `wait-what` and `to-questionnaire` (Section 5.3). Media files name these seven backticked, by folder name, in the form '`grill-with-docs`, where present', never in `skills:` frontmatter, never in the companion form and never as syntek-author's: each `SKILL.md` is copy-only (D11), so an owner may delete it and a project made before 0.5.0 lacks it until D81's update writes it, and a step that runs one says what to do where it is absent. Rules 02 Section 4 gives each role they name a media path (the role table). | Ruling R2. Skills map 7.1–7.3; layers map Section 19 item 8. |
| D11 | **Fifty shared paths are copy-only (D73, D76, D80, D84)** **(overrides syntek-author D17)**, which makes them seed-if-missing: the nineteen of 0.4.x, `/README.md`, `/CONTEXT.md`, `/.gitignore`, `/.mcp.json`, `/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `/.claude/CONTEXT.md`, `/.claude/MEMORY.md`, `/.claude/settings.json`, `/.claude/skills/CONTEXT.md`, `/.claude/skills/CLAUDE.md`, `/AGENTS.md`, `/GEMINI.md`, `/.agents`, `/.codex/config.toml`, `/.codex/CONTEXT.md`, `/.codex/CLAUDE.md`, `/.claude/mcp_config.json`, `/.agents/skills`, `/.agents/mcp_config.json`; and, since 0.5.0, the seventeen family-shared paths syntek-author's `copier.yml` makes copy-only in the same way (syntek-author D47, D51), each line for line as it holds them: `/.claude/skills/grill-me/SKILL.md`, `/.claude/skills/grill-with-docs/SKILL.md`, `/.claude/skills/grilling/SKILL.md`, `/.claude/skills/handoff/SKILL.md`, `/.claude/skills/to-questionnaire/SKILL.md`, `/.claude/skills/wait-what/SKILL.md`, `/.claude/skills/wayfinder/SKILL.md` (file by file, never the folder, which also holds media's template-owned mode files, D7), `/.claude/hooks/pre-compact-handoff.sh`, `/.claude/hooks/context-threshold-handoff.sh`, `/.claude/hooks/CONTEXT.md`, `/.claude/hooks/CLAUDE.md`, `/handoffs/CONTEXT.md`, `/handoffs/CLAUDE.md`, `/.gemini/settings.json`, `/.gemini/CONTEXT.md`, `/.gemini/CLAUDE.md` and `/opencode.json`; and, since 0.6.0, D84's fourteen, as syntek-author's `copier.yml` holds them (syntek-author D56): `/.claude/hooks/how-to-read-only.sh` and the thirteen family paths of `how-to/`, `/how-to/CONTEXT.md`, `/how-to/CLAUDE.md`, `/how-to/docs/CONTEXT.md`, `/how-to/docs/CLAUDE.md`, `/how-to/src/CONTEXT.md`, `/how-to/src/CLAUDE.md`, `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/CONTEXT.md`, `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/CLAUDE.md`, `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/agent-clients-and-mcp.md`, `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/the-data-root.md`, `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/updating-and-recovery.md`, `/how-to/workflows/CONTEXT.md` and `/how-to/workflows/CLAUDE.md` (file by file, never a folder, since a folder line would also take media's own files under its three `syntek-media/` folders). Each has an anchored `_skip_if_exists` line **and** an `_exclude` line `"<: if _copier_operation == 'update' :>/path<: endif :>"`: written by a copy into an empty place, skipped (never overwritten) by a copy over an existing file, never rendered by an update, never recreated after deletion. **D81 supersedes this for `.claude/settings.json`, the five MCP files and the twenty-seven files media's `migrations/family-refresh.json` lists, and D84 for the thirteen under `how-to/`** (syntek-author D51, D52, D56): on Python 3.14 D81's update script refreshes a listed file where it still equals a revision of the project's render history and writes one only where the owner never had it (a deleted one stays deleted, an edited one or a sibling template's version is kept and reported); its copy-only `baseline` task, over an existing file, and every update offer the listed settings entries and top-level values to `.claude/settings.json`, and the two MCP servers to `.mcp.json` and to the MCP files of the clients the answers record, never `.claude/mcp_config.json`, each once, never removing one or adding back one it counts as offered (Section 10); and every update that runs it in full puts the guide under `how-to/` back (D84). No other shared path is refreshed, offered to or restored, the client entrypoints included. `/.claude/mcp_config.json` is retired (D76): its file and both lines stay, and a third, unconditional `_exclude` line keeps a copy from writing it, so a copy writes up to forty-nine, fewer where `AGENT_CLIENTS` leaves a client out (its seven gates, Section 3.5, D80). `/.codex` and `/.gemini` also take syntek-author's folder-level update gates, so an update never creates either folder empty; neither is a shared path or has a `_skip_if_exists` line (each file in it has its own). Media ships no `.claude/hooks/` or `handoffs/` content but those family files (the three hooks and the two pairs), nothing under `how-to/` but the thirteen family paths and its own files under three `syntek-media/` folders (D84), no `learning/`, `assets/` or `build/`, no `.github/` in `template/`, no root `Makefile` and no root `.gitattributes`. Media never mutates a shared file but by its stated exceptions, three since 0.6.0. The first two are D76's: the `v0.4.2` migration, which replaces a `.agents -> .claude` link with the D76 folder; and a second, transient one, the unversioned `agents-outside-link.py` pair, which renames an `.agents` link that leaves the project aside before an update's render and back after it, so the net change is zero and the link's target is never touched. The third is D81's update script, as above, which the family 'update' entry, last in `_migrations`, and the copy-only `baseline` task, last in `_tasks`, run through `"<% _copier_python %>"`. The copy-only `chmod +x .claude/hooks/*.sh` task (syntek-author's, D80) changes no byte: at copy time it sets the executable bit the family records for the hooks. A shared file's two `copier.yml` lines are never deleted, and the file is never removed or renamed in a later release (Section 10 gives the mechanics). | Ruling R5; BRIEF rule 3 ("render on copy only, never on update"), which is why this overrides SA D17. Probes b1–b4, bE1, bE2, c1, h1; mechanics review probe `gate.sh` (03/10/2026). The second exception approved by the maintainer on 14/10/2026. The third, and D81's refresh and offers in place of 'never touched by an update' for the paths they list: the maintainer's decisions of 25 and 27/10/2026 (D81; syntek-author D51, D52); the fourteen and the restore under `how-to/`: of 26/10/2026 (D84). |
| D12 | **Five ownership classes**, one mechanism each: template-owned; seed-if-missing (`_skip_if_exists`); seed-once examples (copy-only `_exclude` with `SEED_EXAMPLES`); author-owned pair-only folders; **copy-only shared** (D11). Section 3. | D17 | **The piece and its ladder.** A *piece* is one deliverable work — a short, an explainer, a talk, a trailer, a podcast episode, an audiobook, a standalone voiceover — and lives at `scripts/src/pieces/NNN-kebab-title/` (three digits, frozen once assigned, never reused; `000` is the seed-once example) with its own pair (D42). The gates are defined in the template-owned guide `scripts/docs/reference/the-piece-ladder.md`, but **rules 03 Section 3 owns the requirement**: the guide's gates bind every piece, and a same-named project guide (D33) may add a check to a gate but never remove or weaken one **(overrides syntek-author D11, whose gates live in a standard that no project guide can override)**. Every CHECKLIST's `> **See**` line names its guide and the gate in the form `M2 (briefed → scripted)`, never restating it; a procedure that moves no piece through a gate uses the fixed form ``> **See** `<guide>`. No gate in `scripts/docs/reference/the-piece-ladder.md` applies to this procedure.`` (Section 4.3 names which procedures). A gate that does not apply to the piece is recorded `n/a` with its reason, never skipped silently. | Ruling R19 (the guide holds the ladder). Conventions review: under syntek-author's guide rules a project guide replaces a reference guide entirely, and "a guide never outranks a standard", so the rules file owns the requirement. syntek-author D11 (V1–V6) is the model; the `M` prefix cannot be confused with it. Precedent for the gate-less See line: syntek-author `proposal/workflows/03-update-the-tracker/`. M4's sub-checks: the per-video pipeline decisions (05/10/2026): a scene timed from its voice needs four of the author's checks before its master exists, and gates for them would renumber M4–M7 in every brief, guide and See line, while dated sub-keys keep each check in the one map the router already reads. |

Sync with the brand's Claude Design design-system project is **author-started, incremental, one component at a time, never a wholesale replace**. The workflow names `/design-sync` (with a hint pointing it at `brand/src/design-system/` as a hand-authored HTML kit) as the route the user asked for, records that its converter targets React kits so the first sync's behaviour is `VERIFY`, and gives `/design import` and `/design export` as the fallback route. No React and no Node in the template: **a scene drawn as code** is composed in Python from the neutral scene kit `toolkit/media_scene.py`, beside these fallback layouts, and the only JavaScript is inside the page the kit writes (D70); the brand's look and any character art stay in `brand/`, the art as exports. Exports: small → `brand/src/exports/` (committed); large → `brand/src/exports/large/` (Git LFS through a nested `.gitattributes` that exempts itself and the pair); one row per design in `brand/src/design-register.md`. | Ruling R9. | D19 | **Big binaries never sit in plain Git.** Renders and generated audio are git-ignored by template-owned nested `.gitignore` files that ship in every profile. Output folders are named `generated/` (ElevenLabs or espeak-ng audio, and audiobook text chunks) and `renders/` (toolkit video, audio and PNG output, and the working copies of tool-written timing and scene files, a scene's page and its stills, D66, D70, D71); the ignore rules keep each folder's `README.md` tracked. **A piece's output sits in a folder named for it inside its output folder** (D64), which the same rules ignore whole, so no new pattern is needed. **Copier's `_exclude` takes everything inside each output folder and the footage mirror at any depth**, so a per-piece folder or stray output a toolkit run leaves in `template/` never ships: `/**` with the folder's own `README.md` negated back in, the gated audiobook folder's `generated/` and `renders/` included: every such negation is listed above every gated line, so a shut gate, matched last, still excludes the folder and its `README.md` (Section 3.5). **Source footage — every recorded or licensed source file — lives in external storage by decision**, listed in the committed `production/src/footage/manifest.toml` (file, sha256, bytes, duration, location label) with a git-ignored local mirror `production/src/footage/raw/`; `media.py footage verify` proves the mirror. Reason: footage is large and rarely diffed, it would exhaust LFS storage and bandwidth quotas, and checksums keep the edit decisions reproducible. Approved generated audio is archived the same way (D52). **Git LFS is used only for `brand/src/exports/large/`.** The LFS tripwire is a copy-only, non-fatal `_tasks` step that sets `filter.lfs.required` only when no LFS filter is configured at any scope — `git config --get filter.lfs.process >/dev/null 2>&1 \|\| git config --get filter.lfs.clean >/dev/null 2>&1 \|\| git config filter.lfs.required true \|\| echo '…'` — so `git add` of an LFS-marked file fails loudly (`clean filter 'lfs' failed`, exit 128) instead of committing a blob silently, whether git-lfs is absent or installed without `git lfs install`. It writes the repository's own `.git/config`, which `_message_after_copy` says (D13). `media.py check` is the second guard: LFS-marked paths stored as plain blobs; LFS-marked files present while no `lfs` filter is configured; files over 10 MB outside ignored and LFS folders. | Ruling R10, whose tripwire command is amended here to meet R10's own stated purpose: the mechanics review probe (03/10/2026) showed `git lfs version` passes when git-lfs is installed but no filter is configured, and the blob was committed whole. Tools-research map Section 4 (nested `.gitattributes` honoured; `filter.lfs.required` makes `git add` fail with exit 128). The any-depth exclusion: Copier 9.18.2 with pathspec 1.1.1 (05/10/2026) tests every path it walks without pruning, so a pattern on a folder's children (`renders/*.png`) missed a per-piece sub-folder and `production/src/renders/*.json` was never excluded, and it obeys the last pattern that matches, so a negation listed after a gate would reopen it. Listed above the gated lines instead, the audiobook folder's two negations left a render with that gate shut holding no audiobook path and one with it open holding only the two `README.md` files (the early fixes' scratch renders, 05/10/2026). |
| D20 | **The toolkit is the repository's own minimal code**, in the supporting folder `toolkit/`, never a root `Makefile` (syntek-author owns it). One standard-library CLI, `python3 toolkit/media.py <command>` (Python 3.14 or later since 0.6.0, the family's one minimum, syntek-author D18; amended by D81), drives `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe` and `rhubarb` (Rhubarb Lip Sync 1.14.0, for `lipsync`, D68) through `subprocess.run([...])` argument lists, and `systemd-run` only where the author sets a memory limit (D72); a driven binary that is absent stops only the commands that need it, each named with its install hint. Its helper modules `toolkit/media_*.py` have no CLI and no `--self-test` of their own, and `media.py --self-test` exercises them. **A heavy dependency lives in a PEP 723 script, pinned exactly, never in `media.py`**: `card.py` renders HTML and CSS to PNG with `playwright==1.63.0` (`assemble` calls it for card PNGs, D47); `transcribe.py` aligns known words to a voice with `whisperx==3.8.6` (D67); `scene.py`, with the same pin and `card.py`'s set-up, checks a scene written as code in stills and renders it frame by frame (D71); the scene kit it runs, `toolkit/media_scene.py`, is a helper module like the others, standard library only (D70). No other script is added without a decision here. **When `media.py` starts a script it names the interpreter**: `uv run --python` with the interpreter a user-scope `UV_PYTHON` names where it meets the script's `requires-python`, or else the one `uv python find --system` returns for that range, which skips every virtual environment, so an active virtual environment, its `bin` on `PATH` or a `.venv` in the working folder can never mislink the script's environment (a virtual environment made with `--copies` reports another Python as its base, and uv then records one version and links another, so the script's packages do not import); a `UV_PYTHON` outside a script's range is never used for it (amended by D81: `uv run --quiet` hides the mismatch, so with 3.14 the base an owner's `UV_PYTHON` would otherwise send `transcribe.py` to 3.14, D67); where no interpreter meets the range it exits 2, naming the fix, which for `transcribe.py` is `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON`. It never starts a script while another run of the same script is building its environment (a lock at user scope, never in the project), because two first runs at once race to install its packages and can leave the environment broken. `uv run toolkit/card.py` or `scene.py`, typed by the author or run by a skill, is safe where no virtual environment is active or `UV_PYTHON` is set, and `check --setup` reports when it is not (D49). `card.py` and `scene.py` declare `requires-python = ">=3.14"`, the base. `transcribe.py` is the one exception among media's tools **(overrides syntek-author D18 for this one script; amended by D81)**: WhisperX 3.8.6 needs Python 3.10 to 3.13, so it keeps `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.14"`, and it alone may run instead under an interpreter path the author names at user scope, `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON`, which `media.py` runs directly, never an environment the author activates where Claude Code or `media.py` runs, whose `python3` the hooks and family scripts need at 3.14 (D67). Every script offers `--self-test`, standard-library only where its dependency is absent. A PEP 723 script's self-test that cannot run a part — its declared dependency, or a browser or tool it drives, being absent — names it on a SKIP line with why and exits 2, because a skip is never a pass; `media.py --self-test` names a probe whose optional tool is absent (Rhubarb, `systemd-run`, pandoc, an image encoder) on a skip line and keeps its exit, as it does today, because such a tool is a note in `check --setup` (D49) and CI, which installs none of them, would otherwise never record a pass. **The toolkit's deliberate network steps are three**: `transcribe fetch`, which the author runs once (D67); `score run --approve-call`, one approved request to TypeSafe (D74); and `voice remix run --approve-call`, one approved request to ElevenLabs, its one direct ElevenLabs call (D82), the last two standard library only; otherwise it reaches the network only through uv's own install of a script's declared packages on its first run. **Author-edited data is TOML**, the brand's platform overrides included (D26); **tool-written timing and scene-index data is JSON** (D66), every number finite, because a scene's page reads it with `JSON.parse`, which refuses the `-Infinity` and `NaN` that Python's `json` writes. No heavy framework: stills, cards, a cross-fade and a slow push-in are ffmpeg's own filters (D47), and other motion in `assemble` is deferred; motion drawn as code is a scene's, captured frame by frame in Chromium (D70, D71). | syntek-author D18. The maintainer's decisions 4, 5, 8 and 11 and the memory-cap follow-up (05/10/2026). Per-video pipeline review (05/10/2026): with a `--copies` virtual environment active, its `bin` on `PATH`, or a `.venv` in the working folder, `uv run` built a script environment recorded as 3.14 but linked to a 3.12 interpreter, so Playwright would not import and a `media.py --self-test` case failed; `--python 3.12`, `UV_PYTHON` and an interpreter from `uv python find --system` each gave a consistent environment; `card.py --self-test` reported a pass with Playwright missing; two first runs at once raced to install Playwright and left its environment broken. Python 3.14 as the base, `transcribe.py` the exception: the maintainer's decisions of 25 and 27/10/2026 (D81; syntek-author D18 at its `v0.7.0`, its `DESIGN.md`:47). A `UV_PYTHON` outside a script's range: media's own check of 27/10/2026, in which `media_common.py`:988-990 returned a user-scope `UV_PYTHON` before it read a script's `requires-python`, and with uv 0.13.4 a `>=3.10,<3.14` script run with `--python` 3.14 under `--quiet` ran on 3.14.8 and exited 0. |
| D21 | **Audio is ElevenLabs, through the user-scope MCP server `elevenlabs`**: voiceover, AI narration and speech-to-text. Every ElevenLabs call goes through that server but one: the voice remix, which the server lacks, is sent by `media.py voice remix run` itself (D82). The template adds no ElevenLabs server to `.mcp.json` or to any client file: since 0.5.0 they declare only the family's two keyless servers, `code-review-graph` and `scrapling` (syntek-author D54; D80). Never generate unasked; state the characters (or audio minutes), the number of calls and the estimated credits, then wait for a yes: for a voiceover, `media.py speak plan <piece>` works out the characters and calls offline and states no credits, because the template carries no credit rate (D36; Section 4.2). Discover the model with `mcp__elevenlabs__list_models` (currently `eleven_v4`) and record it per use in `brand/src/voice/voice.md`. **One call at a time**, each passing an **absolute** `output_directory` inside a git-ignored `generated/` folder, resolved from `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`: for a voiceover the piece's `production/src/voiceover/generated/<piece>/takes/` (D64), which `media.py speak plan` creates before the first call, because the server refuses, before any spend, a folder whose parent is missing; for an audiobook its own `generated/` folder; then **`python3 toolkit/media.py take add`** renames the result to its Section 6.16 name at once (the server names files by the second and can overwrite one) and writes its register and credits-log rows, so no rename needs a shell `mv`. A voice trial, which no piece owns, goes to `production/src/voiceover/generated/voice-trials/<name>/` (D64), which `media.py speak plan --trial <name>` creates before the first call, as it creates a piece's `takes/`, because the server refuses a folder whose parent is missing; no skill makes it with `mkdir`. A trial is never a take, so it enters no register and takes a credits-log row with `—` for the piece (D44): a trial the server writes keeps the server's name and takes its row from the skill; a remix trial's previews are named `<name>.pNN.mp3`, beside `<name>.remix.json`, and logged by `voice remix run` itself (D82). Stop at the first error. Choose the output format for the deliverable and say when it needs a higher ElevenLabs tier (`mp3_44100_192` needs Creator or above, `pcm_44100` Pro or above). A `pcm_*` result is headerless 16-bit PCM in a file the server names `.mp3`: `take add` names it `.pcm`, and every toolkit reader takes the sample rate from the register's `output_format` and reads it as `s16le`, mono (`VERIFY` the raw layout on first use). Re-encoding a 128 kbps take up to 192 kbps meets the letter of ACX, not its intent, and the skill says so. The server's base path must contain the project (Section 8). **espeak-ng** is a scratch and timing track only, labelled approximate, offered after `command -v espeak-ng`. | Ruling R12. syntek-author D19. Tools-research map 1.2–1.4 (G1 Desktop default, G2 base path, G3 filename collisions, G4 extension and raw PCM, G9); platform-research map 5.3; one free `list_models` call, 03/10/2026. The maintainer's decision 3 (05/10/2026); elevenlabs-mcp 0.12.2 `utils.py` refuses an output folder whose parent is missing, before any spend (the per-video pipeline brief's verification, 05/10/2026). |
| D22 | **Caption timing has four routes**, because the MCP `speech_to_text` returns text only, with no timestamps. (1) **Generated voiceover is made one segment per spoken sentence or beat**, within the model's character limit, never one per caption cue (that would split a sentence over two calls with two final intonations, and the MCP passes no neighbouring text to smooth the join): `media.py captions from-segments` chunks each approved segment's text to the deliverable's caption limits and spreads the segment's probed duration across its cues by character share, so cue boundaries are exact at every segment join and close within one, at no cost. One segment per cue stays an option (`per_cue = true`) for kinetic-caption shorts. (2) **Recorded speech**: the words come from the script, or for a recorded piece from its approved transcript (D46), made by `speech_to_text` (credits; only when asked; needs the base path covering the project) or by hand; `media.py captions align` spreads cues over the speech intervals ffmpeg `silencedetect` finds — over a whole recording **beat by beat**, the transcript's beat anchors being hard sync points so drift never crosses a beat, and over one cut **on that cut's own audio and lines** — a heuristic the author checks by eye in a burned preview. `captions retime` carries recording timing through the edit decision list to the master, and master timing to each cut. (3) By hand. (4) **Word timing for a generated voice** (D67): once `transcribe` has aligned the approved segments' own words to the piece's joined voice, `captions from-words` chunks those words to the deliverable's caption limits as route 1 chunks a segment, and times each cue from its first word's start to its last word's end, so a cue inside a long segment lands on its words, not on a share of characters; the times are the aligner's estimates, checked by eye in a burned preview as route 2's are, and route 1 serves wherever no words file exists. A cut's captions may be made before the cut is rendered, so `cut --captions` burns them in the cutting pass. Burn-in writes an ASS file whose `PlayResX` and `PlayResY` equal the output size, and burns into the cut clip or cuts with `-copyts`. Direct-API SRT, ElevenLabs forced alignment and local speech-to-text of a recording stay deferred (Section 11). | The maintainer's decision 4 (05/10/2026), for route 4. Ruling R11 ("one segment per caption chunk/beat"). Tools-research map G2, G5 and G6 (elevenlabs-mcp 0.12.2 source: input files confined to the base path; no `previous_text`/`next_text`; only `transcription.text` returned) and 2d (burn-in on ffmpeg 6.1.1: without PlayRes the default 384×288 canvas made text 6.7× too large). Requirements review: whole-master alignment of a 45-minute talk drifts into every clip. |
| D23 | **The audiobook media kind has three routes, chosen per channel with the author.** (a) **Human** — the author records — mastered and checked by the toolkit to the targets of `[audiobook.acx]`: RMS −23 to −18 dB, peak ≤ −3 dB, noise floor ≤ −60 dB, 1–5 s room tone, 44.1 kHz, at least 192 kbps CBR MP3, mono by house choice (ACX asks only that every file has the same channels). (b) **AI through the MCP** — ElevenLabs text-to-speech, mastered by the toolkit to MP3 — only for channels that accept such a master (for example Spotify for Authors, with its digital-voice toggle); **never offered for ACX or Audible without the author's authorisation**. (c) **External** — a channel that takes only a package made by ElevenLabs' own product (INaudio accepts AI narration only as an unmodified LPF package): the author makes it in ElevenLabs' studio, outside this template; the chapter register records the export, where it is kept and its disclosure, and `narrate-audiobook` refuses to generate or master for that channel. **An audiobook has no script** **(build ruling C1, 04/10/2026)**: `narrate-audiobook` and `production/workflows/05-narrate-an-audiobook/` write its plan, the chapter register (Section 6.7: one row per chapter, plus the credit rows `ch00` for the opening credits and `ch99` for the closing credits, whose spoken words are written with the author in `<piece>.ch00.md` and `<piece>.ch99.md` beside it), and the author's approval of that register, every chapter's source `final` or provided, is its M2; `write-script` never touches an audiobook. **Chapter text comes from its source through `media.py audiobook text`**: syntek-author's section markers, comments, citation keys and Pandoc divs and spans stripped, a scene break turned into `{pause 2}`, footnotes handled as the mode file says, every word or reference with no spoken form listed before any call, and the result chunked at paragraph boundaries under the recorded model's limit into the git-ignored `generated/` folder — from a syntek-author unit whose status is `final` unless the author says otherwise, and never copied into a tracked file. **A chunk ends at every `{pause N}`** **(ruling T3)**: `audiobook text` records the pause after each chunk in the chapter's sidecar `<piece>.chNN.chunks.toml` in `generated/` (the chunk files in order, each with `pause_after` seconds), `audiobook master` reads the sidecar where present and puts exactly that much room tone between the parts, and the mark never reaches ElevenLabs as text. Distributor policies are dated `VERIFY` facts in `production/docs/reference/audiobook-narration.md`. Disclosure always. These ACX targets and D24's house values are the only platform numbers this file states (D26): both are rulings. | Ruling R13. Platform-research map Sections 3a ("all files the same channel format"), 3b ("only unmodified LPF packages … plain MP3 masters do not qualify"), 5.3; tools-research map 1.3 (no MCP tool makes an LPF), 1.5, 2e′–2f; syntek-author D9, D36 and its house Lua filter (the markup a promoted chapter carries). |
| D24 | **Loudness targets:** social video −14 LUFS integrated / −1 dBTP — **a house decision, recorded as such**, because no social platform publishes one; the same house target covers video for the brand's own sites (D62); podcast −16 LUFS / −1 dBTP (Apple Podcasts' published recommendation: around −16 LKFS, ±1 dB); audiobook the ACX route of D23 (RMS, peak and noise floor, not LUFS). Two-pass `loudnorm`, always with `-ar` on the output. | Ruling R14. Platform-research map Section 1 (`[platform.podcast.apple_rss_audio]`), Section 4 row 20 ("−14 LUFS for YouTube" is folklore, not in YouTube's help); tools-research map 2e (tested: −14.1 LUFS / −1.0 dBFS). |
| D25 | **AI disclosure: every platform's own rule for its label, the house rule for the words.** Every piece using a synthetic voice — the brand owner's own cloned voice included — or AI-generated visuals or music records it in its brief. Its `ai_visuals` reads `generated` for a picture an AI model made, `assisted` for a picture made with AI help from the author's own material, and `none` otherwise: **a scene Claude writes as code, animating the author's own drawings, is `assisted`**, never `generated`, because every picture in it is the author's; `assisted` is not `none`, so it takes the house line (2), while the toggle (1) still follows each platform's own rule. At publish: (1) set each platform's AI label or toggle **exactly when that platform's current rule requires it** (for example YouTube's "altered or synthetic content" setting only when YouTube's criteria are met — its help exempts an owner's own cloned voice used for a voiceover — while Meta and TikTok require a label for a realistic AI-generated voice); (2) **always** add a disclosure line to the description on every platform, whatever the toggle rule (house transparency); (3) for podcasts, disclose in the audio and in the episode and show metadata (Apple guideline 1.11). The rules, with each platform's criteria, sources and checked dates, live in `publishing/docs/reference/ai-disclosure.md`; the post package records, per deliverable, the toggle decision and the rule it rests on. | Ruling R15 as amended by the orchestrator (03/10/2026) after the requirements review (REQ-24): a label a platform does not ask for can mislead viewers about what is synthetic, so the toggle follows each platform's rule and the description line carries the house's stricter transparency. Platform-research map Section 2 (YouTube, TikTok, Meta, LinkedIn, Apple and Spotify policies, read 03/10/2026). `assisted` for a code-animated scene of the author's drawings: the per-video pipeline decisions (05/10/2026). |

The brand's own choices (handle, cadence, tone, call to action, hashtag sets) live in the seeds `brand/src/platforms/<platform>.md`; a confirmed difference from the data file is an `[[override]]` in the TOML seed `brand/src/platforms/overrides.toml` (Section 6.15), which the toolkit applies and prints. The over-author renders and `coexist-test.sh` need syntek-author: locally `--author DIR` defaults to `../syntek-author`; in CI they run only when `SYNTEK_AUTHOR_DIR` is available, and otherwise **skip with a clear message, exit 0, and are reported as SKIP, never as passed**. **The names media must never take are frozen** in `.github/scripts/syntek-author-names.txt` — syntek-author's top-level entries, its `.claude/` entries, every skill folder and every rules file at the baseline, plus `build` (its Makefile's output) — which `skill-conformance.sh` check 20 and `shipped-brands.sh` check 11 read on every run and never skip; `coexist-test.sh --refresh-names` rewrites it from `--author`, and its check 20 fails while the live tree differs. **The family-shared set is the one exception** (amended by D80 and D84): the seven family skill folders, `.claude/hooks`, `handoffs`, `.gemini`, `opencode.json` and, since 0.6.0, `how-to` stand in that file and ship from media too, byte for byte as syntek-author ships them and copy-only in both templates, so the second copy skips them, Copier's update never renders them, and each template's update script refreshes a listed one only where it still equals a render of that template's own history (D11, D81, Section 10); under `how-to` media ships only the thirteen family paths and its own files under three `syntek-media/` folders, and syntek-author nothing under a `syntek-media/` folder (D84; syntek-author D56). Never fabricate a statistic, quotation, testimonial, endorsement, platform rule or limit. | DESIGN-NOTES.
