| D44 | **One credits log.** Every credit-spending call — text-to-speech, speech-to-text, any later generation — gets a row in `production/src/credits-log.md` (date, piece, tool, calls, characters or minutes, voice, model, output), written by `media.py take add` for a take, by `media.py voice remix run` for a voice remix (Tool `voice remix`, D82) and by the skill otherwise, so spend is visible across skills. | Skills map 4.9. |
| D45 | **Scripts are written for the ear.** Spoken lines open with an upper-case speaker tag (`VO:`, `ON:`, `HOST:`, a character's name); cues use the reserved tags `TEXT:`, `SFX:`, `MUSIC:`, `NOTE:`; delivery directions sit in braces (`{softly}`, `{pause 0.6}`); timing is estimated at about 150 words a minute unless the brief sets its own. A braced direction becomes an ElevenLabs audio tag only when the recorded model supports tags, and never reaches captions. The source script retains those directions and its `SFX:` and `MUSIC:` cues; the cue index preserves them as events alongside the spoken-word timings (Section 6.3). | DESIGN-NOTES. Tools-research map 1.5 (v4 audio tags; keep them out of caption text). |
| D46 | **Recorded pieces.** A talk, interview or conversation that exists as a recording is a piece with `origin: recorded` and `source_media` (its footage IDs) in the brief. `production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/` logs the recording (`footage add`, which copies, never moves), extracts its audio, and makes `transcript.md` in the piece folder — through `speech_to_text` only when asked, or by hand — in the script format (Section 6.2) with a beat anchor `(at HH:MM:SS.mmm)` on the recording for every beat, then the recording-timed captions. For a recorded piece **M2 is the transcript approved**, **M3 is `n/a — recorded`**, and the transcript stands in for the script wherever a script is read (`captions check --script`, `captions align`, `repurpose`). The master is then cut from the recording like any other footage. | Requirements review (03/10/2026): the business and non-fiction brands make recorded talks, which a script-first ladder could pass only by three waivers. Tools-research map G6, G8, 2j. |
| D47 | **Stills, cards and fades in the edit decision list.** A `[[clip]]` is a video or audio source with `in` and `out`, a still or card with `seconds`, or a solid `colour` with `seconds`; it may enter with `transition = "fade"` (ffmpeg `xfade`), and a still or card may carry `motion = "push-in"` (ffmpeg `zoompan`). An audio master (`size = ""`) takes sound only from its clips, so a podcast is cut from a recorded talk. `[[audio]]` takes an optional source range (`in`, `out`) and `fade_in`/`fade_out` (ffmpeg `afade`). **Cards have a tracked home**, `production/src/cards/<piece>.<card>.html`, copied from the brand's card component (D18); `assemble` renders any card PNG that is missing or older than its HTML or `tokens.css` into the piece's `production/src/renders/<piece>/cards/` (D64; an overlay's transparent PNG named apart from a clip's, Section 6.16) with `uv run toolkit/card.py` under D20's interpreter (exit 2 naming uv or Chromium when it cannot) before compositing, so a master always rebuilds from tracked files and never waits on the publishing layer. **Scenes leave this route unchanged** (D71): a scene drawn as code is never a `[[clip]]` but is rendered to a master of its own by `scene.py render`, and stills, cards, fades and the push-in still serve trailers and every other piece. | Requirements review: a 45-second trailer of stills and cards, and a podcast trimmed from a talk under a faded music bed, could not be expressed; the overlay pointed at a git-ignored render made in a later layer. Tools-research map 2h–2l. |
| D48 | **syntek-author's social-media family owns the written social plan.** In a business project with that family (syntek-author D39, on by default), its documents own the social media plan, the content calendar, profile bios, the channel voice guide and text-only posts. Media's platform profile then holds only delivery facts (handle, deliverables used) and cites the plan in prose for cadence, tone, call to action and hashtags, **for each platform the plan covers** (D56); `publishing/src/schedule.md` lists only media deliverables, each citing its calendar entry; `prepare-post` reads the calendar entry and packages media deliverables only; and every media skill or workflow whose triggers come near names "the social-media-documents skill (syntek-author), where present" as its boundary. Standalone, or in a book project, the profile and schedule hold all of it. | Mechanics and requirements reviews: two routers, two calendars and two sets of bios for one request. syntek-author D39; its `social-media-documents` description ("a content calendar", "refresh our bios", "a batch of posts"). |
| D49 | **A setup check before the first spend.** `media.py check --setup` reports, changing nothing: ffmpeg and ffprobe with libass, x264 and mp3lame; Python 3.14 or later (D81); uv, and whether the interpreter a plain `uv run` would choose here is a virtual environment's whose base is another Python version (D20's mislink), with the fix (`UV_PYTHON` at user scope, or no virtual environment active); the pinned Playwright's Chromium, found through `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`, else the default cache, else (where uv is present) the browsers path uv gives its scripts, asked offline with no project and nothing built, so a system bundle a uv wrapper supplies is found; fontconfig's `fc-match`, optional, which `card.py --self-test`'s brand-font probe needs (a note, never a finding); `rhubarb` with `res/sphinx/cmudict-en-us.dict` beside its real path, since `rhubarb --version` passes on a copy without it (D68); the `transcribe` interpreter — the one the user-scope variable names, or uv's environment once `transcribe fetch` has built it — its Python, which must lie in `transcribe.py`'s range, `>=3.10,<3.14`, below media's base (D67, D81), and its WhisperX version, exactly 3.8.6, read without importing WhisperX; the three files `transcribe fetch` brings, the wav2vec2 weights, nltk's `punkt_tab` and the cross-check model (D67); `systemd-run` with a user service manager, for the memory cap (D72); git-lfs and the LFS filter where `brand/src/exports/large/` holds files; media's own D13 allow and ask entries and its two `Edit` denies in `.claude/settings.json` (not the family's eight MCP asks, its two denies, `mcp__code-review-graph__embed_graph_tool` and `Edit(/how-to/**)`, or its hooks, the guide guard among them, syntek-author D54's, D56's and D51's, D80, D84) (an entry kept in a local or user settings file is reported absent, with that caveat, because media never reads an ignored file: D50), the two `voice remix run` asks among them, each one missing a FAIL naming the line to add by hand, because without it a remix spends credits with no prompt, and an update offers it only once, never adding back one it counts as offered (Section 10), and not at all on a Python older than 3.14 (D81, D82); whether the user-scope ElevenLabs server's base path contains the repository, reading only that one key of `~/.claude.json` and printing no other value; and whether `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` is set in the shell, for `voice remix run`, a note giving its presence only, never its value. `brand/workflows/06-check-the-setup/` runs it, `_message_after_copy` points to it, and rules 03 Section 4 forbids a credit-spending batch until its ElevenLabs lines are clean. **A tool only some pieces need** — Rhubarb, WhisperX and its fetched files, `systemd-run`, espeak-ng, pandoc and the optional image encoders (D57) — **is a note naming the commands its absence blocks, never a FAIL**; one that is present but broken (Rhubarb without its dictionary, WhisperX at another version, a mislinked uv interpreter) is a FAIL with its fix. | Requirements review: in the combined project the D13 ask rules are a hand edit nothing verified, and each missing tool surfaced mid-journey. Per-video pipeline review (05/10/2026): a copy of Rhubarb with no `res/` folder passed `--version`, then exited 1 naming the dictionary; a mislinked uv environment failed only when Playwright was imported. |
| D50 | **No tooling reads what Git ignores.** `media.py flags` and the large-file scan of `media.py check` filter their file lists through `git check-ignore`; `footage verify`, `take add`, `audiobook`, `voice join` (each take by its register's `file`), `levels`, `transcribe` and `lipsync` (the joined voice by its one path in the piece's renders folder) and `scene.py` (the page and stills it wrote, the joined voice its edit decision list's `vo:<piece>` row stands for, and each footage image `<piece>.real.json` indexes, by its manifest path, D70) open an ignored media file only by the path a manifest, register or argument names, and print names and hashes, never content. Outside a Git work tree they fall back to the plain list. `media.py where <piece>` lists the piece's tracked files across `scripts/`, `production/` and `publishing/`, filtered the same way, and names its ignored per-piece folders (D64) by path, never listing what is inside them. The timing and scene commands print what they found, so the session reads a command's output and never opens its ignored working copy (D66). `scene.py stills` prints every finding and the path of every still it wrote, and the session opens each still by that path to look at it, the one kind of ignored file it opens to see rather than to probe or play (D71). Rules 06 Section 12 states it for Claude. The family's data-root resolver (D80) is syntek-author's tool, not media's toolkit: its `status` names ignored binaries only in the folders a map claims, and media's map claims none. Media's own report-only `v0.6.0` crossing migration reads only the files `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard` lists, `git check-ignore` and Git's objects at `HEAD`, never an ignored file (D84). | syntek-author D42 (v0.2.0). |
| D51 | **The voiceover skill ships always.** `voiceover`, `production/workflows/02-make-a-voiceover/`, `production/src/voiceover/` and `production/docs/reference/voiceover.md` ship in every render: every brand kind narrates explainers, trailers or episodes, and the user named the skill. The `voiceover` media kind gates only `scripts/docs/reference/standalone-voiceovers.md`, the guide to a piece that is a voiceover and nothing else. | Requirements review: the `[tiktok]/[trailer]` profile could not voice a trailer. Departs from the orchestrator's design notes, which gated the skill on the media kind; flagged with this reason. |
| D52 | **Approved generated audio is archived like source media.** A take cannot be made again: regeneration spends credits and gives a different take, because the MCP exposes no seed. When the author approves a segment or a mastered chapter, the skill offers to log it with `media.py footage add --kind generated` (copied into the local mirror, hashed, labelled with its external location), and M4 needs every generated take a master uses approved and archived. **For an audiobook the mastered chapter is what must be approved and archived** **(ruling T10)**: its chunk takes may be archived on the author's word but need not be, because a book's takes would double the archive and the master is the unrepeatable whole the author approved. The `generated/` READMEs say so. | Requirements review; tools-research map G5. |
| D53 | **Three platforms for the brand's own channels: `website`, `blog`, `newsletter`.** `PLATFORMS` gains three values (Section 2). `website` is the sites the brand publishes media to, its own and any it writes for: pages, self-hosted or embedded video, posters, share images, and the home of a self-hosted podcast feed (D58). `blog` is dated posts on such sites: the video, its transcript, its featured and share images. `newsletter` is email issues: a linked still or animated GIF, never video, because email clients do not play it reliably. **One `website` platform covers every such site**: a site is a row of the profile, named by a frozen slug (D61), never part of a deliverable key, so keys stay `<platform>.<format>`, `platforms.toml` and `overrides.toml` stay platform-wide, and one render serves every site that uses its key. Sites differ only in which keys they use; a per-site value is deferred (Section 11). **A profile site** is any site in the website or blog profile's `## Sites`, whoever owns it: the own-channel rules (M6's captions and published transcripts, D60) apply on every profile site, and 'own' decides only whether a site needs an agreement (D61). The three stay separate platforms so that each gates its own profile, guide and tables: `blog.og_image` repeats `website.og_image` because a project may choose `blog` without `website`. No web, blog or email source publishes a delivery spec, so every size and rate in their tables is a house choice inside browser, standards-body or email-vendor guidance, named in `chosen`, with every value taken from a third party or a search snippet in `verify` (D26). | The maintainer's approved change (04/10/2026): one business publishing to its own site and to a second site it writes for, to blog posts, and to email newsletters. Web-specs research Sections 0–4 (MDN, web.dev, WebKit, W3C WCAG 2.2, Apple's HLS authoring ladder, ogp.me, Facebook, LinkedIn, Google Search Central, Litmus, Email on Acid, caniemail, Mailchimp); boundary research 6c, 6d and Q1. Practicality review (04/10/2026): rules keyed to 'the brand's own site' read literally exempted the second site the brand writes for. |
| D54 | **The per-kind defaults do not change.** No kind defaults to `website`, `blog` or `newsletter`: a value joins a kind's default only where every project of that kind should have it, and none qualifies. A non-fiction author's default `podcast` may live on a podcast host or be served from any web server the brand controls (D58), so not every such project needs the website platform; not every business or novelist places media on its own pages (an embed of the YouTube upload needs no website file); and over syntek-author the written-side documents, not the template, decide which of the brand's channels carry media (D56). The `PLATFORMS` help recommends `website` beside `podcast` for a self-hosted feed with episode pages. **What a self-hosted podcast needs:** `PLATFORMS` `podcast` (and `website` for its pages); `MEDIA_KINDS` `podcast` only for an episode of its own (D58 route 2), since a talk published as an episode (route 1) needs only the talk's own kind, such as `long-video`. Between them the three example answers files turn every value on, and one audit render ships `blog` without `website` (Section 7). | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` recipe 7.8 step 1. Boundary research item 6j (recommends off: the plan owns the decision). Podcast-hosting research Section 1.4. Mechanics review (04/10/2026): D58 serves a feed without the website platform, so the reason first given for adding `website` to the `author-nonfiction` default did not hold by recipe 7.8's own test. |
| D55 | **Who owns what on the brand's own channels** (D48 applied to them). **Media owns** every file a viewer plays or sees as part of a piece — self-hosted video, a silent loop, posters, share, featured and preview images, a GIF preview, WebVTT captions, a podcast's episode audio and its feed — and only the words that live outside the written body: an embed's or a figure's title, alt text, the structured-data values, the disclosure line, the published transcript (D60), and a podcast episode's title, description and chapters, which live in its show register and feed (D59). **The written side owns** the writing the media sits in, every word of it, and its timing: a blog post's body, headings, standfirst, figure captions, link text, page title, slug, meta text and publish date; a website's page copy; a newsletter issue's body, blurbs, subject line, preheader, list, send time, consent and unsubscribe. Over syntek-author that is a unit of its library layer, drafted through its own loop and recorded in its provenance ledger (syntek-author D12), by the writing router or, where the project writes blog posts in the same workflow as its social posts, by the social-media-documents skill; media names both as its boundary, each 'of syntek-author, where present'. Standalone it is the author's, outside media. **Media never edits a website repository, never sends an email and never holds a subscriber list.** An embed or player snippet is given in chat on request as paste-ready text, never written into a site's files (build ruling C4). A post package names the written piece a placement sits in, read and never edited, and never records its status: M7 reads that status, and a piece that is not `final` is a warning, never a gate (D4). A placement's schedule row takes its date and time from that written piece and cites it (Section 6.13). | Boundary research Sections 1–5: syntek-author's social-media family has no blog or newsletter type, and its calendar 'describes the image or video, never produces it'; its email family is one-to-one correspondence; a website's page copy lives in the site's own repository. syntek-author D12: the provenance of written text stays apart from media's disclosure (D25). Practicality review (04/10/2026): a standfirst or blurb drafted by media and pasted into a unit lived in two files and entered the unit with no ledger entry, and a status copied into the package went stale; the target project's social-media operating procedure writes its blog posts in the same workflow as its social posts. |
| D56 | **The written side owns what it sets, platform by platform and document by document** (amends D48). Where syntek-author's social-media documents are present, any of them that sets a value for a platform — the social media plan, the content calendar or an operating procedure — owns that value: cadence, tone, call to action, hashtags. A document **covers** a platform when it names the platform together with a cadence for it. The profile cites each such document in prose, 'where present', and holds a value itself only where no document sets it; every profile holds its delivery facts. `prepare-post` reads the calendar entry where one exists and the profile otherwise, deliverable by deliverable. | Boundary research 6a: syntek-author's social-media standard names LinkedIn, Instagram, X, Facebook, TikTok and YouTube and no blog or newsletter. Practicality review (04/10/2026): in the target project every cadence, the blog's weekly one included, sits in its social-media operating procedure, and its plan treats the website only as an assessment, so a rule keyed to the plan alone left the blog's cadence with no owner, or copied it into the profile. |
| D57 | **Own-channel deliverables go through the toolkit, with the fewest new parts.** **Web video** is `encode` or `cut` to a `website.*` or `blog.*` key: H.264 and AAC in MP4 with its index at the front, captions as a WebVTT sidecar loaded by `<track>`, never burned (their tables' `caption_formats`). **A silent loop** is a cut whose table sets `audio_tracks = 0`: the toolkit writes no sound track, and its verification fails a file that has one; it carries no words, so the cut-down rules that a cut stands alone in whole sentences and is no near-copy of another do not bind it (its Lines are `—`). **Image deliverables** are laid out and rendered by `card.py` at the preset size, as thumbnails are (D18), and encoded by one new command, `media.py image`, into the table's format (`jpg`, `png`, `webp` or `avif`) from a PNG, a still or a frame of a video (`--at`), then checked for size, format, `max_size` and alpha. It replaces the hand-run ffmpeg conversion `thumbnail-brief` had. `card.py` marks `:root` with `data-deliverable` and `data-platform`, so one layout can show a play button (an element carrying `data-play-button`) on a newsletter preview and not on a share image. **An animated GIF is an image deliverable that `cut` makes**, and it belongs to the thumbnail brief, made with the piece's other images towards M7, never at M5, because its overlay comes from the piece's thumbnail layout: `card.py render <layout> --deliverable newsletter.preview_gif --transparent` draws the play button and the words, and `cut <master> --deliverable newsletter.preview_gif --in … --out … --overlay <png>` trims and reframes as for video, lays the overlay on every frame, the first included, and writes the GIF through ffmpeg `palettegen` and `paletteuse` at `colours_max` and at most `fps_max`. It plays `floor(max_seconds ÷ clip seconds)` times, written as the gif muxer's `-loop` of plays − 1 (`-1` for a single play: no loop extension), so it stops within `max_seconds`; a range longer than `max_seconds` renders nothing. Over `max_size`, it is made again at half the frame rate (`fps=fps/2`, every second frame kept, the duration unchanged), then at a quarter (Litmus's own advice), and otherwise fails, naming the size. `--overlay PNG` is the one new flag of `cut`, and works on video cuts too. **A deliverable with no speech to caption takes no captions**: a table with `kind = "image"` or `audio_tracks = 0` carries no `caption_formats` (so nothing reads it as 'burn'), M6 reads `n/a` for it, and no skill reports its captions missing; the words a GIF shows are chosen in its thumbnail brief. A WebM (AV1 or VP9) source offered before the MP4 is deferred (Section 11): the MP4 plays in every browser, and AV1 encoding is slow. | Web-specs research Section 5 items 1–3 (no `-an`, no GIF, WebP or AVIF output; `card.py` writes PNG only) and Section 4 (Litmus: 'keep every second frame, or one in four'; classic Outlook shows only a GIF's first frame). The ffmpeg 6.1.1 build used for the research has `gif`, `libwebp`, `libaom-av1`, `libsvtav1` and the `avif` muxer (checked 04/10/2026); ffmpeg's gif muxer documents `-loop -1` as no loop and `0` as endless. `thumbnail-brief` step 8 ran ffmpeg by hand, against rules 04 ('every render goes through the toolkit'). Practicality review (04/10/2026): a GIF cut at M5 needed an overlay only workflow 04 made; `caption_formats = []` read as 'burn' made a silent loop report missing captions; the loop count and the thinning were ambiguous. |
| D58 | **A self-hosted podcast: the feed is the delivery mechanism.** A self-hosted show is published from **one RSS 2.0 feed per show**, written by the toolkit (`media.py feed`, standard library only, D20) from the show's register (D59) and served as a static file, with the episode audio, art, captions and chapters, from one site the brand owns: a site of the website profile, named by its slug, where the project has that platform; otherwise any web server the brand controls, named in the podcast profile's `## Account`. **Apple Podcasts and Spotify ingest that feed**; neither takes an upload. A show kept on a podcast host instead delivers `podcast.apple_rss_audio` to that host as before and uses neither the register nor the feed workflow. **YouTube carries the episode as video, by upload** into the show's podcast playlist; YouTube's RSS ingestion (a still of the show art, audio that can never be updated) is for an audio-only show only, recorded in the register, and never used beside uploads for the same show. **Two routes make an episode.** (1) **A talk as an episode**: where the episode is a recorded or scripted talk's whole sound, it is one piece with one picture master and the deliverables `youtube.long` and `podcast.feed_audio`, both encoded from that master (an audio deliverable takes the master's sound only, levelled to the podcast target), so the video and the feed carry the same chapters; it needs no `podcast` media kind and no episode workflow. (2) **An episode of its own**: an audio-first episode, or one whose trims and beds differ from its video, is a `kind: podcast`, `picture: false` piece mastered by `production/workflows/04-master-a-podcast-episode/` (the `podcast` media kind). Its video is `still-video` of its own master under a card rendered at `youtube.long`'s size (never the square episode art), or, where it is cut from a talk another piece recorded, that parent's `youtube.long` upload, which its package names: its brief's `parent` is the talk, its `source_media` the talk's footage IDs (logged once; `footage add` refuses a duplicate hash, so `production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/` skips its logging step), and its `transcript.md` the parent's approved beats it keeps, copied with their anchors on the same recording and approved as its own M2 (a later correction to either reopens both); each master's chapters are timed on that master. The existing `podcast` tables stay as Apple's and Spotify's specs, and two join them: **`podcast.feed_audio`**, the one file the feed encloses and the website's player plays, an MP3 inside both directories' ranges, chosen against Apple's advice for AAC because AAC is missing from Chromium builds and the same file must play on the brand's pages; and **`podcast.id3_cover`**, the copy of the show's cover inside that MP3, encoded with `media.py image` from the cover's design export, as `podcast.cover` is (`brand/workflows/03-record-a-design-export/`: a cover belongs to a show, not a piece). `[platform.podcast]` holds the platform-wide limits: description and title lengths, the chapter rules and the feed-move period. **A show's feed URL is its own for life**, and a corrected file is published under a new URL with the same GUID, because Spotify downloads again only when a file's path changes and Apple ignores a repeated enclosure. **A static feed is a schedule only by its upload time**: the feed naming a new episode is written with `--as-of` that episode's `pub_date` and uploaded at that time, after every file it names. Media writes the files; the author uploads them and does each directory's one-off set-up and each episode's hand steps: Spotify takes a transcript only through its own upload (whether it reads `podcast:transcript` is `VERIFY`), and the YouTube upload joins the show's playlist by hand (D41). A site whose own code writes feeds may build the feed from the register instead; `feed check --feed` validates any saved feed offline, and the toolkit never fetches a feed: its deliberate network steps are `transcribe fetch`, which the author runs once (D67), and the approved `score run` and `voice remix run` calls (D74, D82), uv's first install of a script's packages aside (D20). | The maintainer's requirement (04/10/2026): podcasts go to Apple Podcasts, Spotify and YouTube, and are self-hosted on the brand's website or websites. Podcast-hosting research Sections 0–4, 6, 8 and 9: Apple's *A Podcaster's Guide to RSS* and support pages 823, 893, 5482 and 5316; Spotify's Podcast Delivery Specification v1.10 and its transcripts help; YouTube help on RSS delivery and podcast playlists; MDN's audio codec guide; a standard-library prototype whose feed the W3C Feed Validation Service passed with 0 errors. Practicality review (04/10/2026): the recorded talk had two incompatible episode models, a hosted show had lost its route, a cover had no home, and the per-episode hand steps were missing. |
| D59 | **The show register owns a feed's text and identity.** `publishing/src/podcast/<show>.toml` (Section 6.17), in a pair-only folder gated `'podcast' in PLATFORMS`, is the single source of every value in a show's feed: the show's details and one `[[episode]]` row per episode, never deleted. The post package's feed section cites it and never repeats it. **Identity is written once.** `feed new` writes the show's `podcast:guid` (UUIDv5 of its feed URL, as the Podcasting 2.0 namespace defines it) and `feed add` each episode's `guid`; neither is ever recomputed, except by `feed new --rekey`, which rewrites the feed URL and every GUID, and only while no episode is `published` and no tracked feed exists (a provisional URL can be corrected before anything is public, never after). `feed new` refuses a project whose podcast folder is absent (the platform not chosen), naming the update that adds it (since 0.6.0 in D81's form, Copier run as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier` with `-a .copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`, Section 4.2). **Encoding and tagging are separate, so the ladder holds.** At M5, `encode --deliverable podcast.feed_audio` makes the episode's MP3 like any audio deliverable, needing no register. At M7, once the episode's words and chapters are written in its row and approved, `feed tag` re-muxes that render without re-encoding (`-c:a copy`) with the row's ID3v2.3 title, the show's title and author, the episode number, the chapters (`CTOC`/`CHAP`) and the show's `id3_cover`; it refuses a row whose title or description is empty or whose chapters break the `[platform.podcast]` rules, and writes the render's name, bytes and seconds back to the row, as `take add` writes its register. A title corrected after the episode is `published` goes into the register and the feed only: the published file is retagged only as a corrected file under a new URL (D58). **The tracked feed is the record of what is live.** `feed write` takes `--as-of` always, so one register and one `--as-of` give one set of bytes, and before it writes anything it compares the new feed with the show's tracked copy `publishing/src/podcast/<show>.feed.xml`, where one exists: it writes nothing and exits 1 when the show's `podcast:guid` changed, when a GUID of the tracked copy has vanished while its row is not `withdrawn`, or when an enclosure's length changed while its URL stayed the same. At M7 the feed workflow writes the upload copy into `publishing/src/renders/`; once the author reports the feed live, `publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/` sets the row `published` and writes the tracked copy with the same `--as-of`, byte for byte the file uploaded, so every public change is a reviewed diff. `feed check` makes the same comparison without writing, against the tracked copy or `--previous`. **The register's skeleton lives in `toolkit/media_feed.py`**, which writes it and adds its flags; the folder's `CLAUDE.md` shows it with no live flag, and `toolkit-smoke.sh` holds the two the same. An episode's JSON chapters file is a render. The register is TOML because the toolkit reads it (D26). | Podcast-hosting research Sections 7, 10 (item 2) and 11 (Q2, Q4). The Podcasting 2.0 `podcast:guid` definition: Python's `uuid.uuid5` reproduced both of its published examples (04/10/2026). syntek-author D18 (a Markdown table read by a script). Mechanics and practicality reviews (04/10/2026): `encode` needed a register row that M5 could not have and wrote an empty title; `feed write -o` could not run a second time over a tracked file, and the check that followed it compared a feed with itself; a feed written 'now' left out the episode being scheduled; a provisional feed URL fixed the show's GUID for life. |
| D60 | **Accessibility on the brand's own channels: WCAG 2.2, Level A enforced.** M6 (Section 6.1) widens, and its trigger is a **profile site** (D53), whoever owns it. Every `website.*` or `blog.*` video deliverable with speech has a WebVTT sidecar that passes `captions check --script` (SC 1.2.2). **Every piece or cut with speech placed on a profile site has a published transcript of exactly what that page plays**, `publishing/src/captions/<piece>[--cNN].transcript.en-GB.md`, made by `captions transcript` from the script or transcript (`--lines` taking one cut's lines, as `captions align` does) and published on the page, or linked, right under the player: one per distinct cut placed, and the whole piece's for a full-length video or an embed of the whole upload. For audio-only media it is the transcript of SC 1.2.1; for video it is the media alternative of SC 1.2.3, descriptive (on-screen text kept, and every visual fact the words leave out added by hand). A podcast with a `podcast.feed_audio` deliverable is therefore no longer `n/a` for M6: its episode pages carry its transcript, and its feed carries the master-timed WebVTT, which Apple reads. A loop or a GIF carries no speech: a loop needs a pause control and a still for reduced motion on its page (SC 2.2.2, the site's code), nothing flashes more often than SC 2.3.1 allows, and a GIF plays for no longer than its `max_seconds`, because an email offers no pause control. Audio description (SC 1.2.5, Level AA) is deferred (Section 11); until then a script for a piece bound for a profile site says aloud what the picture shows. | Web-specs research Sections 2.2 and 3 (W3C WCAG 2.2, Recommendation 12/12/2024; W3C WAI 'Transcripts'). Podcast-hosting research Section 5 (MDN: an `<audio>` element cannot show captions as `<video>` can; W3C 'Understanding SC 1.2.1'). Boundary research 6f and 6i. Practicality review (04/10/2026): a 90-second cut on one page and the whole talk on another needed a transcript each, and 'the brand's own site' read literally exempted the second site. |
| D61 | **Sites, lists and placements.** The website and blog profiles hold `## Sites`, and the newsletter profile `## Lists`, in place of `## Account` (Section 6.15): one row per site or list, named by a kebab slug frozen once a schedule row uses it, with its domain or sender as the author gives it, its owner, its CMS or sending service, its player route and caption handling, and its agreement. A row never holds a subscriber, a count or an account ID (D36). Where `website` and `blog` are both chosen, the blog's rows use the website's slugs, and **a site's agreement is recorded once**, in its website row, which the blog row's Agreement cites; a blog row holds it only where the website platform is not chosen. **A placement** is one deliverable going to one page of a profile site or one issue of a list, written `<platform>:<slug>` in the schedule's and the publish log's Platform cell and as the qualifier of its post-package H2 (Sections 6.12 and 6.13): **the one file that page or issue plays or shows first** — the video, the embed, the GIF, or a still only where there is no GIF. The images that go with it (its poster, share, featured or fallback image) are bullets of that placement, never placements or schedule rows of their own, and M7 counts placements, never image keys. One render serves every placement of its key. **An embed of the brand's YouTube upload is a placement of `youtube.long`**, never a deliverable of its own: there is no render, the package cites that upload's publish-log URL, and the embed loads from YouTube's privacy-enhanced host behind a click-to-load facade, with consent the site's own concern (`VERIFY`). **A podcast episode page on any site but the feed's** is a placement of `podcast.feed_audio` (`## podcast.feed_audio — website:<slug>`, or `blog:<slug>` inside a post), with its own schedule row, publish-log row and agreement, playing the same enclosure URL; the register's `page` and the feed's `<link>` stay the feed site's page. **A site or list the brand does not own** needs its row's Agreement to name a dated record (over syntek-author, its approval record, named in prose) before any placement there is `ready` (M7). A placement that depends on another, such as an embed on its upload or a newsletter on its watch page, names it under **Links to** and is posted after it. An image a partner site wants in its own branding is a design export (`brand/workflows/03-record-a-design-export/`), not a second thumbnail brief, until per-site values arrive (Section 11). | Boundary research Sections 2 (the target project's second site belongs to a partner organisation, with four points still open), 4, 5, 6b, 6c, 6h, Q3 and Q6; web-specs research Section 2.3 (YouTube's privacy-enhanced mode; Lighthouse's facade guidance). The agreement lives in the new profile seeds, so no existing seed's vocabulary changes (D63). Mechanics and practicality reviews (04/10/2026): one placement, three images and three rows were all readable from the first wording; an episode page on a second site had no package, row or agreement; the same site's agreement in two profiles could drift. |

| D67 | **Word timing: WhisperX aligns the words the voice was asked to say** (amends D22 with its route 4; lifts Section 11's deferral of local Whisper word timing). `python3 toolkit/media.py transcribe <piece>` times every word of a generated voiceover: it aligns each approved segment's known words to the piece's joined voice (`voice join`), each segment at its own start and end in that voice, so the aligner never guesses a word. It runs `toolkit/transcribe.py` with **WhisperX 3.8.6**, under the interpreter the user-scope variable `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` names where it is set (an interpreter whose environment already holds WhisperX, which `media.py` runs directly by that path, so the PEP 723 header is ignored; no media text tells the author to activate that environment where Claude Code or `media.py` runs, because the hooks and the family scripts need that shell's `python3` at 3.14, D81), and otherwise with `uv run` on that header (`requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.14"`, `whisperx==3.8.6`) and D20's interpreter, except that a user-scope `UV_PYTHON` outside that range is never used for it: it takes D20's `uv python find --system` within the range instead, and where none qualifies exits 2 naming `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` (D81), because D20 otherwise gives `UV_PYTHON` precedence over a script's `requires-python`, and `uv run --quiet` hides the mismatch; so on media's base, Python 3.14 (D81), this one script needs a Python in its range kept beside it, as media's guide says, naming `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` (D84); either way the script checks that WhisperX is exactly 3.8.6 and exits 2 otherwise, and no interpreter path is ever written into the project. It imports torch before anything else that loads a GPU library, so the CUDA libraries torch's own wheels carry are found with no `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. **The aligner text** is the segment's `text` with each `voice.md` respelling in place of its word and every hyphen made a space, a respelling's parts timed back to its one word; no IPA and no audio tag reaches the aligner. A digit or a symbol in it is exit 1 before anything runs: the alignment model knows 29 labels and aligns any other character as a wildcard with an ordinary-looking score, so it cannot show that such a word was said, which is why a script spells numbers as spoken. `align()` keeps its default `interpolate_method="nearest"`, because `"ignore"` can silently drop a sentence that has no times, and the aligned word sequence is compared with the input: a segment that comes back with no words, or a sequence that differs, is exit 1. **The cross-check runs by default**: faster-whisper `large-v3-turbo`, from Hugging Face's default cache, transcribes the voice and the heard words are compared with the script's, since only a transcription shows that the voice said something else; `--no-cross-check` skips it, and the words check says so. It writes `<piece>.words.json` (each word's `word`, `start`, `end`, `score` and `segment`) and the words check `<piece>.words-check.md` beside it (segments without words, low scores, where `request` and `text` differ, where the heard words differ), as D66 says, and prints the check. **No network, except `transcribe fetch`**, which the author runs once, never a skill (D13's allow rule would let a session run it unprompted, so a skill gives the author the command): on the uv route it builds the script's environment, and it downloads the three things WhisperX would otherwise fetch on first use — the English wav2vec2 weights, nltk's `punkt_tab` and the cross-check model — into each library's default user cache, never the project. Every other run checks those files first (exit 2 naming `transcribe fetch` where one is missing), runs uv with `--offline` and sets `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`; every run sets `PYANNOTE_METRICS_ENABLED=0` before any import, because pyannote, which the cross-check's voice-activity model loads, sends telemetry by default. It spends no credits. ElevenLabs forced alignment and timestamps stay deferred: they need a direct API call, and D21 allows only one, the voice remix (D82). | The maintainer's decision 4 (05/10/2026). Per-video pipeline review (05/10/2026), on WhisperX 3.8.6 as installed: `alignment.py` maps any character outside the model's labels to a wildcard (a year and a price were timed and scored like words), returns a failed segment with no words, and under `"ignore"` drops a sentence with no times; a hyphen is the blank label; torch downloads the wav2vec2 weights whenever the file is missing, and `model_cache_only` does not apply on the English path; `align()` fetches `punkt_tab` when it is missing; pyannote 4.0.7 enables telemetry by default; torch 2.8.0 loads its own cuBLAS and cuDNN, and ctranslate2 4.8.2's cuBLAS loads once torch is imported (a library-load test, not a model load). A WhisperX environment takes several gigabytes, and uv builds one per script path. |

Its recogniser, pocketSphinx, is US English: its effect on a British voice is `UNCONFIRMED` beyond the takes already tried. **The dialogue file (Rhubarb's `-d`) is the segments' plain spoken `text`**, never a respelling: on the one clip tried, a hyphenated respelling broke recognition into fragments, while a plain unknown word was given a guessed pronunciation (`UNCONFIRMED` beyond that clip). Rhubarb records the input's absolute path in `metadata.soundFile`; `lipsync` makes it repository-relative before writing, so no home folder enters a tracked file. Rhubarb's exit 1, a fatal error, is the toolkit's exit 2, with Rhubarb's message. Rhubarb finds its resources beside its real path, through any link, and will not recognise speech without `res/sphinx/cmudict-en-us.dict` there, while `--version` succeeds without it, so `check --setup` looks for that file (D49); the author installs the release archive unzipped at user scope with `rhubarb` linked onto `PATH`, never the binary copied alone. Cue times are whole centiseconds, truncated; **whatever reads `mouth.json` takes a frame's shape at the frame's middle**, t = (n + 0.5) / fps, so the mouth shows what sounds during the frame, never half a frame early. | The maintainer's decision 5 (05/10/2026). Per-video pipeline review (05/10/2026): Rhubarb 1.14.0 runs on Ubuntu 24.04 (built on 20.04); pocketSphinx with extended shapes gave all nine shapes in 125 cues on a project's generated takes, one cue shorter than a frame at 30 fps; a copy with no `res/` folder passed `--version`, then exited 1 naming the dictionary; `soundFile` held the input's absolute path; 5.4799 s was reported as 5.47; the dialogue-file trial was one 2.9 s espeak-ng clip. |

| D70 | **A scene written as code, drawn by a neutral kit** (amends D18, D20 and Section 11). A piece whose picture is drawn rather than filmed (a scene piece, Section 6.3) keeps its scene as Python in `production/src/scenes/<piece>.scene.py`, tracked, which composes the **scene kit** `toolkit/media_scene.py`: one flat standard-library module, exercised by `media.py --self-test`, that writes the page `production/src/renders/<piece>/scene/index.html`, git-ignored, written afresh by every `scene.py` run (D71) and never edited by hand. **Python outside the page, JavaScript only inside it:** the kit works out in Python what every frame shows — each element's position, the character's pose, facing, mouth and blink, the typed line's visible characters — from the tracked timing files (`production/src/scenes/<piece>.cues.json`, `production/src/timing/<piece>.words.json` and `<piece>.mouth.json`) and the real sources `<piece>.real.json` indexes, so the self-test proves the timing without a browser; it writes that state, and the short drawing code that applies it, inline into the page, so no `.js` file ships and the page loads nothing but the brand's files and the images and text captures `<piece>.real.json` indexes, never a video, each by a relative path worked out when the page is written (an image archived as footage by the path its manifest row names, D50): recorded video inside a scene is deferred (Section 11), and a piece that shows it is a footage piece, whose master `assemble` builds (D47). A timing file the scene needs and the project lacks is exit 2, naming the command that makes it. The page keeps Section 6.11's layout contract (`tokens.css` by relative path, nothing over the network, `--frame-*` and `--safe-*` on `:root`). **A deterministic clock:** the scene file sets its frame rate (30 where it sets none) and its length in frames, and the page exposes one draw function of the frame number, which `scene.py` calls before every capture; nothing runs on a timer, a CSS transition or animation, or `requestAnimationFrame`, nothing reads the time or a random number, and every position falls on a whole design pixel (one pixel of the frame at device scale 1), so frame `n` is the same picture on every run. **What the kit draws:** layers in a fixed stacking order; the typed line (on-screen text revealed a character at a time, its pace a function of the frame number); the safe zone, inside which text and the character stay; and the brand's look only through the custom properties of `brand/src/design-system/tokens.css` and any stylesheet or art under `brand/` the scene file names. **The character's sprite slot** takes whole frames, one per pose and mouth shape (`<sprite>-<pose>-mouth-<shape>`), or a mouthless `<sprite>-<pose>` under a `<sprite>-mouth-<shape>` layer placed at that pose's mouth, drawn at a whole-number scale; the shapes are those `lipsync` writes, `A`–`H` and `X` (lower case in a file name), and a frame the scene needs and the brand lacks is exit 2, naming it. **The mouth** at frame `n` is the shape of the cue in `<piece>.mouth.json` whose `[start, end)` holds `t = (n + 0.5) / fps` minus the voice's offset (the `at` of the edit decision list's `vo:<piece>` row), and the rest shape `X` where no cue holds `t`: sampling the middle of a frame never shows a cue a frame early, and a cue shorter than a frame may drop out. **Movement, so a character never stands frozen:** position keyframes per beat, line or word, the path between them stepped (a position or pose holds for a whole number of frames the scene sets) and snapped to the design-pixel grid, with a walk cycle through the walk poses the scene names while it moves; turning to face by mirroring the sprite about its own box on the pixel grid; idle life between moves, a blink (a `<sprite>-<pose>-blink` layer the brand draws, laid over the frame; a sprite without one does not blink) and a breathing bob of one sprite pixel, both functions of the frame number; and a change of pose on a beat, line or word boundary (`<piece>.cues.json`, `<piece>.words.json`), the walk cycle's poses stepping as the scene sets. **Interaction:** any element of the scene — a typed line, a graphic, a real source's capture — may carry a name and anchor points, and a keyframe may name an anchor in place of a position, so the character walks to, faces and points at that element (in a pointing pose the scene names), timed to its words (their starts in `<piece>.words.json`) or to its board or line (`<piece>.cues.json`); the element may answer on the frame the character reaches it (a highlight, a nudge, a reveal), worked out from the frame number like everything else. **The look stays the brand's:** the kit is neutral, and the brand's style and any real character art live in the project's `brand/`, the art as design exports (D18), never in the template (D36; the template ships no binary, Section 10). A scene Claude writes as code animating the author's own drawings is `ai_visuals: assisted` in its brief (Section 6.1), disclosed by D25. | The maintainer's decisions on the per-video pipeline (05/10/2026) — 7 (movement, interaction and the sprite slot), 10 (a neutral kit; the look in `brand/`), 11 (Python outside the page) and 5 (the frame's middle), with the follow-up that a scene animating the author's drawings is `assisted` — and the verified ground of the same date, both kept outside the repository like the build's maps: a prior engine already sampled the middle of each frame; the lip-sync tool truncates its times to whole centiseconds, so a cue can be shorter than a frame, and its rest shape is `X` only while its extended shapes include it; a `<piece>.scene.py` cannot be imported by name; a page five folders below the root needs five `../` to reach `brand/`. syntek-author D18 (a standard-library toolkit). |
| D71 | **`scene.py` checks a scene in stills and renders it frame by frame** (amends D20 and D47). `uv run toolkit/scene.py` is a PEP 723 script pinned to `playwright==1.63.0`, as `card.py` is, and reuses `card.py`'s set-up — Chromium at device scale 1, every http(s) request aborted and reported, `document.fonts.ready` awaited, every image checked — with two differences, because a scene is thousands of captures where a card is one: **one browser for the whole run**, and the frame size and safe-zone variables set before the page's first draw, where `card.py` sets them after the page loads. Every run first runs `production/src/scenes/<piece>.scene.py` **by its path** (`runpy.run_path`, never an import by name, so no `__pycache__` lands in `scenes/`), which writes the page afresh (D70). A scene piece's edit decision list has no `[[clip]]` or `[[overlay]]` rows, which are `assemble`'s, and `render` refuses one that has any (exit 2). It probes the master and fails it (exit 1) when its frame count is not the scene's or its sound is longer or shorter than its picture. **A master per aspect:** at `[edit]`'s `size` the master is `<piece>.master.mp4`; every other aspect is rendered natively at `--size`, laid out by the scene for that frame and never cropped or padded from another master, as `<piece>.master.<W>x<H>.mp4`, both in `production/src/renders/<piece>/`, and a vertical cut is taken from the vertical master with its Frame `native` (Sections 6.3 and 6.10). **The memory cap is `assemble`'s** (D72): `render` takes the same `--memory-max` flag and user-scope setting, and runs uncapped, saying so, where neither is set or `systemd-run` is absent. **Proved without a second example** (D43 unchanged): `scene.py --self-test` and `toolkit-smoke.sh` build their fixtures at run time — a placeholder sprite drawn in CSS, a voice made with espeak-ng where it is present and a tone where it is not, a two-board cue file and its mouth cues — and a browser case that cannot run is a SKIP by name, never a pass. **The stills route stays** (D47): `assemble`'s stills, cards, fades and push-in serve trailers and every project as before, and a scene is a second route to a master, never a clip. | The maintainer's decisions on the per-video pipeline (05/10/2026) — 6 (the stills route kept for everyone; fixtures, no second worked example, no D43 change), 11 (`scene.py` beside `card.py`; no `thumbnail.py`), the opt-in memory cap and the second aspect (rendered natively, never cropped) — and the verified ground of the same date: `card.py` set its variables after the page loaded and launched Chromium for every PNG; the toolkit had no streaming subprocess helper, and the `[[audio]]` mix was written inline in `assemble`; the loudness targets are data, and the edit's `loudness` key chooses one; the worked example is plan-only and cannot render (D43); render speed is still unmeasured. |
| D72 | **Heavy renders run under a memory cap where the author sets one.** `assemble` (and `scene.py render`, D71) take `--memory-max SIZE`, or read the user-scope variable `MEDIA_MEMORY_MAX`; where a limit is set and `systemd-run` is present with a user service manager, the command runs itself again under `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=SIZE -p MemorySwapMax=0`, so a render that outgrows the limit is stopped (exit 2, naming the limit) instead of exhausting the machine. With no limit set, or no `systemd-run` (macOS, a CI runner), it runs uncapped and says so in one line. The cap is the author's to set, because a sensible limit depends on the machine and one the toolkit guessed could stop a render that would have finished; `check --setup` reports `systemd-run` as a note (D49). Streaming the stills (Section 4.2, `assemble`) is the fix for the memory a stills master used; the cap guards against the next such cause. | The maintainer's memory-cap decision (05/10/2026). On 05/10/2026 a stills master held one input per still, about 190 MB each at 1080p (each a looped input that held a decoder and its frames for the whole render, `CHANGELOG.md` 0.2.2): 32 stills peaked at 6 GB, and 213 in one list exhausted 31 GB and stopped the machine; streamed, 213 mixed stills peak at about 0.8 GB (0.2.2). |

| D82 | **Voice remix is the one ElevenLabs call made directly; a character's voice is designed from the author's own** (amends D13, D20, D21, D44, D49, D58, D64, D67 and Sections 3.5, 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, 5.1, 6.13, 6.14, 6.16, 7, 8, 10 and 11; D77–D81 are reserved for queued work). The ElevenLabs MCP server has no remix tool, so `media.py voice remix plan NAME --from VOICE --describe TEXT [--text TEXT]` builds the request offline: `NAME` is a voice-trial folder name, with the rule of `speak plan --trial`; `VOICE` is a voice the account owns (ElevenLabs refuses a Voice Library voice); the description is 5–1000 characters and the optional preview text 100–1000 (`VERIFY`, checked 15/10/2026). It prints the request, one call and its characters, and no credit rate (D36). `voice remix run … --approve-call` sends that one request to ElevenLabs' `text-to-voice/{voice_id}/remix` endpoint, standard library only, with no redirect, a 30-second timeout and no retry, asking for generated preview text when none is given and for the previews in the response; the key comes only from a user-scope `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` in the shell, sent as `xi-api-key`, never written or printed. It decodes each returned preview into a staging folder and moves the set whole into `production/src/voiceover/generated/voice-trials/<name>/`, as `<name>.pNN.mp3`, beside `<name>.remix.json` (the date, the source voice, the description, the text, and each preview's `generated_voice_id`, media type and duration), refuses before any call when either already exists, and appends the trial's credits-log row itself (piece `—`, Tool `voice remix`, counting the generated preview text's characters when no `--text` is given). Saving the chosen preview, the Voice Changer and every other ElevenLabs call stay on MCP: `mcp__elevenlabs__create_voice_from_preview` takes the `generated_voice_id` (`VERIFY` on first use that it accepts a remix's). Because D13's allow lets any `toolkit/*.py` command run unprompted, a flag alone guards nothing (D66): D13 gains two ask entries, `Bash(python3 toolkit/media.py voice remix run *)` and `Bash(uv run toolkit/media.py voice remix run *)`, which reached new copies only (D11) until 0.6.0, when D81's refresh data began offering both to an existing `.claude/settings.json`, once each, never adding back one it counts as offered (amended by D81; Section 10); `check --setup` reports either one missing as a FAIL with the line to add (D49), and `_message_before_update`, which gave both by hand from 0.4.4, no longer does. Jev's `score run` keeps none (D74). `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/` ships always and runs through the `voiceover` skill (no new skill): it drafts each character's description from the manuscript or script, remixes the author's own cloned voice, has the author listen and choose, saves the voice through MCP, and records a `character:<name>` row in `brand/src/voice/voice.md` whose consent is the source clone's. A guide, `production/docs/reference/character-voices.md`, explains it. A remix starts only from a voice the author owns and has cleared consent for (rules 03 Section 6). No test calls ElevenLabs; fixtures and transport stubs are created at run time. Multi-voice takes in one piece (a voice per segment, and Voice Changer takes made from a recording) are deferred (Section 11). | The maintainer approved the remix route, its ask rule, the character-voice workflow and release 0.4.4 on 15/10/2026, and chose voices only, with multi-voice takes deferred. ElevenLabs API reference (text-to-voice remix, design and create; the voice remixing guide), read through Context7 on 15/10/2026: remix accepts only voices the account created and the default voices. The elevenlabs-mcp tool list, read the same day: `text_to_voice` and `create_voice_from_preview`, no remix. |
| D83 | **Community files live at the repository root and never ship** (amends D27; Sections 7, 9 and 11). The root holds `LICENSE`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, and `.github/` holds `CODEOWNERS`, `PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` and `ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` (`bug_report.yml`, `template_improvement.yml`, `config.yml`). `_subdirectory: template` keeps every one of them out of every generated project (D1), so none needs an `_exclude` line. None is placed under `template/`: there it would be either a template-owned file that collides with a project's own (Section 10) or a copy-only shared path that can never be removed (D11), and a generated project's licence is its owner's choice. `LICENSE` is the MIT Licence alone, 'Copyright (c) 2026 Sam Bailey t/a Syntek', byte-identical to syntek-base commit `969ab58` (sha256 `62d902ac…`, which `community-files.sh` pins), so GitHub detects it. The family's two licence paragraphs (syntek-author D55) carry the rest, word for word but for the template's name and two substitutions: 'the owner's own work' for 'the author's own writing', because media's owner is a brand or an author, and no file named after 'A file that carries its own licence keeps it.', because `template/` holds no file under a licence of its own. Media adds no sentence of its own. `README.md` ('Licence') carries the first paragraph: the licence, and its permission for generated projects. `CONTRIBUTING.md`, the front door for changes to the template, carries both in its 'Licence' section; the second takes contributions under the same terms, the generated-project permission included (inbound = outbound), with no contributor licence agreement and no copyright to assign. There is no DCO sign-off: the pull-request template asks the contributor to confirm those terms in a checkbox. `SECURITY.md` supports the latest release alone and takes reports only through GitHub's private vulnerability reporting, giving no address. `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` is the Contributor Covenant 2.1, kept over 3.0 as the family's choice, verbatim but for its contact (it is quoted text, so its US spelling and its Attribution section stay), and the only file that names an enforcement contact: one email address, written once in its Enforcement section, the only real one in the repository (every other address in it is invented). `CODEOWNERS` requests the maintainer's review on every path. `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` holds GitHub's own YAML files only and carries no pair, a house rule rather than a GitHub requirement: GitHub lists a Markdown file there as an issue template once it gains `name` and `about` front matter, so the house keeps Markdown out of the folder, and `.github/CONTEXT.md` describes it instead. Its `config.yml` turns blank issues off and links this repository's private vulnerability reporting, `https://github.com/Syntek-Dev/syntek-media/security/advisories/new`. No community file carries a metadata header, and none is family-shared: each family repository writes its own, naming its own rules, paths and audits. `SM_NEVER` (`_common.sh`) lists the four root files beside `.github`, so `shipped-brands.sh` check 8 proves on every standalone render that none ships, and `community-files.sh` checks 1–6 guard them at the root (Section 7). The floor measured on 22/10/2026 with `update-test.sh` and `COPIER_CMD` pinned to each release: 9.6.0 fails every update (`ValueError: Invalid choice`, 9.6.0 `user_data.py`:478-503, a multiselect answer replayed); 9.10.3 and 9.11.0 refuse the outside link (`ForbiddenPathError: ".agents" is forbidden`, 9.11.0 `_main.py`:726-727) before the hold; with this release's audits, `update-test.sh`'s real run passes on 9.12.0 and 9.19.1 (all three brand kinds), and its `--self-test` on each of the nineteen releases from 9.12.0 to 9.19.1. The script parsed with `ast` and passed its `--self-test` (21 probes) on Python 3.10.22, the oldest series Copier 9.12.0 supports (`Requires-Python: >=3.10`), on 22/10/2026. The maintainer's decisions of 22/10/2026: D85 in a 0.4.5 patch rather than waiting for D80, because the unsafe fix is live; a pointer, not the fix, in `_message_before_update`; paragraph B kept word for word, with syntek-author asked for a sentence true of both templates; check 21 tightened with its guard, check 23 over both states, the self-test in `run-all.sh` and in CI's existing loop step, and the byte pins as `shipped-seeds.sh` checks 21 and 22; the floor raised to 9.12.0. Approved by the maintainer on 22/10/2026. |

---

## 2. Copier questions

House style: UPPER_SNAKE keys, `>-` help written to the person answering, validators as `<: if bad :>message<: endif :>`. Sections in `copier.yml`, in this order: Identity · Variant · The brief and the audience · The owner · Platforms and media · Options · Policy and locale · Meta · Family-shared questions (D80) · Migrations · Before an update · After a copy · After an update · Post-generation.

| Key | Type | Shown when | Default | Help / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BRAND_NAME` | str | always | — | The brand's name exactly as it appears on screen, in captions and in credits: a trading name or a pen name. Validators: not empty; no `"` or `\` (the toolkit writes it into double-quoted metadata such as ID3 tags). |
| `BRAND_SLUG` | str | always | kebab of `BRAND_NAME` | `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]+$`, written as character-set tests (syntek-author's idiom: no regular expressions without an extension). |
| `BRAND_KIND` | str | always | **none** | `choices: [business, author-fiction, author-nonfiction]`. Decides the mode files and the defaults below. An update refuses a change (D14). `--defaults` without it stops with "Question "BRAND_KIND" is required". |
| `BRAND_DESCRIPTION` | str | always | — | What the channel is for and for whom, in one or two full sentences. Validator: at least 40 characters ("a tagline is not a brief"). |
| `AUDIENCE_TEST` | str | always | per kind (below) | One sentence naming the viewer or listener every piece must work for. The comprehension skill (syntek-author), where present, reads as this person. Validator (syntek-author's `READER_TEST`): at least 20 characters, 'a sentence, not a label'. |
| `OWNER_NAME` | str | always | `BRAND_NAME` | The person or business who maintains the project, exactly as written: "Maintained By" in every metadata header. Not empty; no `"` or `\`. |
| `OWNER_FIRST_NAME` | str | always | first word of `OWNER_NAME` | How skills address the owner in chat. |
| `PLATFORMS` | str, `multiselect: true` | always | per kind (below) | Labelled choices in syntek-author's `"label": value` form (its `BUSINESS_FAMILIES`): `"youtube — long videos, Shorts and thumbnails": youtube`, `"tiktok — vertical video": tiktok`, `"instagram — reels, stories and covers": instagram`, `"linkedin — landscape and vertical video posts": linkedin`, `"facebook — reels and feed video": facebook`, `"podcast — Apple Podcasts and Spotify, from your own feed or a podcast host": podcast`, `"website — sites you publish to: self-hosted or embedded video, posters and share images": website`, `"blog — posts on those sites: video, transcripts, featured and share images": blog`, `"newsletter — email issues: a linked preview image or GIF, never video": newsletter` (D53, D58). Validator refuses an empty list. The help names what podcast adds besides its profile and guide (the show-register folder and the feed workflow), recommends `website` beside it where the feed is self-hosted with episode pages (D54), and ends: "Removing one later deletes every file it generated, including the platform profile you filled in." |
| `MEDIA_KINDS` | str, `multiselect: true` | always | per kind (below) | Labelled the same way: `"short-video — vertical shorts and clips": short-video`, `"long-video — explainers and talks": long-video`, `"podcast — episodes": podcast`, `"audiobook — narrated or recorded books": audiobook`, `"trailer — book and launch trailers": trailer`, `"voiceover — pieces that are only a voiceover (the voiceover skill ships whatever you choose)": voiceover`. Validator refuses an empty list. Same deletion warning. |
| `SEED_EXAMPLES` | bool | always | `true` | The worked example piece `000-example-piece` (D43). Copy only; once deleted it stays deleted. |
| `MODEL_MECHANICAL` | str | always | business `opus`, otherwise `sonnet` | `choices: [sonnet, opus]`. Never Haiku (D38). |
| `TIMEZONE` | str | always | `Europe/London` | Validator syntek-author's, verbatim (D29): refused unless it contains `/` or is `UTC`. |
| `DATE` | str | always | — | The baseline date in every metadata header, DD/MM/YYYY (syntek-author's validator). Answered once and kept, so updates never churn headers. |

Per-kind defaults, each written as one dictionary lookup on `BRAND_KIND` (`"<% {…}[BRAND_KIND] | tojson %>"` for the lists; the lists hold values, never labels):

| `BRAND_KIND` | `PLATFORMS` | `MEDIA_KINDS` | `AUDIENCE_TEST` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `business` | youtube, linkedin, instagram | short-video, long-video, voiceover | 'A small-business owner watching on a phone between meetings, with the sound off.' |
| `author-fiction` | youtube, tiktok, instagram, facebook | short-video, trailer, audiobook, voiceover | 'A reader who has never heard of the book, scrolling with the sound off.' |
| `author-nonfiction` | youtube, podcast, instagram, facebook | long-video, short-video, podcast, audiobook, voiceover | 'A thoughtful listener with no specialist training, hearing it on a commute.' |

No kind defaults to `website`, `blog` or `newsletter`, and the 0.1.0 defaults are unchanged (D54). Had a default changed, it would reach new projects only: an existing project's answers are recorded, so an update never adds a platform to it.

**Family-shared questions (D80).** After `DATE`, between the marker lines `# BEGIN family-shared questions (D48)` and `# END family-shared questions (D48)` (syntek-author's text, so their D48 is its decision, as D85 keeps the migrations block's), media carries syntek-author's 'questions' block byte for byte from its `v0.7.0` tag (D81; syntek-author D47, D50 and D53; 33,486 bytes) and never edits it. The data-root keys' defaults read the project's previous answers, then syntek-author's recorded ones through `_external_data.peer` (D14), so one project keeps one store across both templates. Since 0.6.0 the client and model keys read the same two records in the same order, so one project also keeps one set of client files and one model table (syntek-author D47 and its Section 9 rule 7; D81): `AGENT_CLIENTS` keeps the project's recorded list, else takes syntek-author's, an empty one included, so the template applied second writes no client file the first left out; each model key takes a recorded value only where its question would accept it, and the OpenCode address, model and context only from a record whose `OPENCODE_PROVIDER` equals this answer. Over syntek-author a first copy therefore takes syntek-author's clients and models (Section 8):

| Key | Type | Shown when | Default | Help / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AGENT_CLIENTS` | str, `multiselect: true` | always | the recorded answer (its values that are still choices), then syntek-author's recorded list (likewise, an empty one included); else `[]` where the project already has its own record (the update that first asks it); else all four (a fresh copy, alone or over a syntek-author record from before the question) | `codex`, `gemini-cli`, `antigravity`, `opencode` (Claude Code always ships). May be empty. Decides which client files a copy writes (Section 3.5), the rows of rules 05's model table (D38) and which existing client files D81's update script offers the two MCP servers to (Section 3.6); an update never adds or recreates a client file, and changes one only by adding those servers, once each. |
| `CODEX_MODEL` | str | `'codex' in AGENT_CLIENTS` | the recorded answer, then syntek-author's, each only where the validator accepts it; else blank: Codex's own default | Written into `.codex/config.toml` once. Validator: letters, digits and `. _ : / @ + -` only. |
| `GEMINI_MODEL` | str | `'gemini-cli' in AGENT_CLIENTS` | as `CODEX_MODEL`'s; else blank: Gemini CLI's own default | Written into `.gemini/settings.json` once; the same validator. |
| `OPENCODE_PROVIDER` | str | `'opencode' in AGENT_CLIENTS` | the recorded answer, then syntek-author's, each only where it is one of the choices; else `user` | `user` (no model or provider written), `named` (a `provider/model`), or a local server: `llama-cpp`, `ollama`, `vllm`, `lmstudio`, `openai-compatible`. |
| `OPENCODE_BASE_URL` | str | `'opencode' in AGENT_CLIENTS and OPENCODE_PROVIDER not in ['user', 'named']` | the recorded address, then syntek-author's, each only from a record whose `OPENCODE_PROVIDER` equals this answer and only where the validator accepts it; else the server's usual local port | An `http://` or `https://` address ending `/v1`, checked without a regular expression. |
| `OPENCODE_MODEL` | str | `'opencode' in AGENT_CLIENTS and OPENCODE_PROVIDER != 'user'` | as `OPENCODE_BASE_URL`'s (a `named` model only with its `/`); else blank, so it must be answered | The model's ID; `provider/model` for `named`. |
| `OPENCODE_CONTEXT` | int | as `OPENCODE_BASE_URL` | as `OPENCODE_BASE_URL`'s (only 1,024 to 10,000,000); else per server | The context the server loads, 1,024 to 10,000,000 tokens, never more than the server's own setting. |
| `DATA_STORE` | str | always | the recorded answer, then syntek-author's (each only `external` or `repo`), then `repo` where the project already exists, else `external` | `external` (binary files under each machine's data root, named by `SYNTEK_DATA_ROOT`) or `repo`. A first answer must equal syntek-author's. Media files nothing under either: its map is empty (D80). |
| `DATA_AREA` | str | `DATA_STORE == 'external'` | recorded, syntek-author's, then `business` | The first folder under the data root, case kept; Windows-safe, at most 64 characters; a first answer equals syntek-author's exactly. |
| `DATA_SLUG` | str | `DATA_STORE == 'external'` | recorded, syntek-author's, then the project folder's name in kebab case, cut to 64; none (`UNSET`) where nothing is left or a Windows device name is | The project's folder in each kind folder, in kebab case; never changed once files are filed. |
| `DATA_KIND_DOCS` · `DATA_KIND_PHOTOS` · `DATA_KIND_AUDIO` · `DATA_KIND_VIDEOS` | str | `DATA_STORE == 'external'` | recorded, syntek-author's, then `docs`, `photos`, `audio`, `videos` | The four kind folders, each Windows-safe and different, ignoring case, from those before it. |
| `DATA_PROVIDER` | str | always | recorded; syntek-author's retired `INCLUDE_DRIVE_SYNC`, inert here; syntek-author's; `none` | `none`, `google-drive`, `onedrive`, `dropbox`, `other-cloud`, `nas`. No validator. It runs nothing: it only tailors the resolver's checks and advice. |

Inside the block a delimiter reads only those fifteen keys, `_external_data`, `_folder_name` and Copier's `UNSET` (hence Copier 9.11.0 or later, D2), and the retired `INCLUDE_DRIVE_SYNC` only inside two string literals; the peer key sits outside it (D14). Every gate, `when:` and content block that reads one of these answers repeats its question's `when:` test, because Copier renders a hidden question's default without recording it, and every test of `DATA_STORE` is an equality with a value, one positive block per value (syntek-author D50). The first update that asks them records `AGENT_CLIENTS: []`, or syntek-author's recorded clients where its answers file holds a list (D81), and `DATA_STORE: repo`, or syntek-author's recorded store; Copier writes no client file on that update, and D81's update script only adds the two MCP servers, once each, to the existing files of the clients it records (Section 3.6). `README.md` has a row for each, and all three `examples/*.answers.yml` carry the keys.

There are **no computed values** at 0.1.0. A later one is `when: false`, derived only from answers, never from `_external_data` (D14).

**Token discipline.** Variant tokens (`BRAND_KIND`, `PLATFORMS`, `MEDIA_KINDS`, `BRAND_DESCRIPTION`, `AUDIENCE_TEST`, `SEED_EXAMPLES`, `MODEL_MECHANICAL`, and any later answer) and `<: if … :>` blocks may appear only in the **spine set**:

- the root spine: `.claude/rules/syntek-media/*.md` and `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`;
- the fifty copy-only shared paths (Section 3.6), every seed (3.1) and every seed-once example (3.2), because each renders once and is never merged; the family-shared ones among them are syntek-author's bytes, whose delimiters read only the client and model keys of the family block (syntek-author D48), and every other vendored file holds none (D80);
- **index files**: every `CONTEXT.md` or `CLAUDE.md` whose folder is an ancestor of a gated path, as syntek-author's audit computes them. Such a file wraps each row, tree line or cross-reference that names a gated path in `<: if <gate> :>…<: endif :>`, using exactly the gate in Section 3.5, or names that path only in prose; it never gates a row on `SEED_EXAMPLES` and never names an example path (D43).

Every other shipped file is **shared** and byte-identical in every render that ships it; differences live in gated files and mode files. Identity and locale tokens (`BRAND_NAME`, `BRAND_SLUG`, `OWNER_NAME`, `OWNER_FIRST_NAME`, `DATE`, `TIMEZONE`) may appear anywhere but in media's chapters under `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/`, which hold no delimiter and no header, so each is the same bytes in every render (D84).
A list is rendered with `<% PLATFORMS | join(', ') %>`, never a `for` loop variable inside `<% %>` (the token audit rejects lower-case names), or one gated line per item.
A shared or mode file never backticks a path or skill gated by `PLATFORMS` or `MEDIA_KINDS`; it names it in prose ("the audiobook folder, where the project makes audiobooks"), or the line carries `<!-- doc-references: variant-only -->`.
Never put a token inside `_…_` emphasis.

---

## 3. Ownership classes and gating

Subsections keep syntek-author's numbers (3.1 seeds … 3.5 gating table), so its `copier.yml` comments, root `CONTEXT.md` and recipes transfer; media's extra class is 3.6.

| Class | Mechanism | Copy, path absent | Copy, path exists | Update | Author deleted it | Template stops shipping it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| seed-if-missing | `_skip_if_exists` | written | skipped, exit 0 | untouched | **recreated** | **deleted, even if filled** (probe h2) |
| seed-once example | `_exclude` `_copier_operation == 'update' or not SEED_EXAMPLES` | written if `SEED_EXAMPLES` | never happens (`SEED_EXAMPLES=false` when applying over a project) | untouched | stays deleted | kept |
| author-owned pair-only | only the pair ships (the pair is template-owned or a seed) | pair written | — | pair merged | — | — |
| template-owned | none | written | **copy: exit 1, "Interactive session required"** (a collision: a template bug); **update: overwritten with conflict markers** (Section 10) | three-way merged (under `how-to/`, then restored: below) | stays deleted (under `how-to/`, restored: below) | deleted |
| copy-only shared | `_skip_if_exists` + update-gated `_exclude` | written | skipped, exit 0 | never rendered (D81's lanes: below) | stays deleted (under `how-to/`, restored: below) | kept (probe `gate.sh`; Section 10) |

Since 0.6.0 the family update script acts after Copier wherever Copier runs on Python 3.14 or later: last in `_migrations` on every update, and as the copy's last task (Section 3.5; D81). It refreshes some copy-only shared files and offers settings entries and MCP servers to others, once each (Section 3.6, superseding D11 for those paths), and its **restore** (D84) puts the new render's bytes back on every file the new render holds under `how-to/`, template-owned or copy-only, whose copy differs (an edit, a merge, conflict markers or a deletion), never over another template's version of a shared one and never on a path the render lacks. A copy restores nothing, and neither does an update under `SYNTEK_UPDATE_REPORT=off`, under `--pretend`, after a failed render or on an older Python; the next update that runs in full does (syntek-author D56). Every other path keeps its row.

### 3.1 Seed-if-missing (`_skip_if_exists`)

brand: `brand/src/design-register.md` · `brand/src/design-system/tokens.css` · `brand/src/design-system/previews/{colors,type,spacing,brand,captions,thumbnail,card}.html` · `brand/src/voice/voice.md` · `brand/src/platforms/overrides.toml` ·
platforms (each also gated, Section 3.5): `brand/src/platforms/{youtube,tiktok,instagram,linkedin,facebook,podcast,website,blog,newsletter}.md` ·
scripts: `scripts/src/piece-register.md` ·
production: `production/src/rights-register.md` · `production/src/credits-log.md` · `production/src/footage/manifest.toml` ·
publishing: `publishing/src/schedule.md` · `publishing/src/publish-log.md` ·
author-filled index pairs: the `CONTEXT.md` + `CLAUDE.md` of every `docs/project/` and `workflows/local/` in the four layers (sixteen files).

Twenty-six seeds and sixteen index-pair files; with the fifty shared paths (3.6; D84 adds fourteen), `_skip_if_exists` holds 92 lines. Every line is anchored, listed unconditionally (a pattern for a path absent from this render is a no-op), explicit (no globs), and added in the change that adds its file. Seeds ship **empty of entries**: headings, writing rules and the "seeded stub" banner in Markdown; header comments only in TOML; neutral values with `/* AUTHOR TO CONFIRM: … */` slots in `tokens.css`; neutral layouts in the preview cards. The brand seeds (`tokens.css`, `previews/thumbnail.html`, `previews/card.html`, the platform profiles, `voice.md`) must carry `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM` slots. Their emptiness is audited, not trusted. A seed is never removed from the template without a migration that preserves it first (probe h2). No seed ships under `how-to/` (D84).

### 3.2 Seed-once examples (copy only; stay deleted)

`<: if _copier_operation == 'update' or not SEED_EXAMPLES :>…<: endif :>`, one `_exclude` line per path (eight):
`/scripts/src/pieces/000-example-piece` (folder: its pair, `brief.md`, `script.md`, `storyboard.md`, `shot-list.md`) · `/production/src/edits/000-example-piece.toml` · `/production/src/cards/000-example-piece.title.html` · `/publishing/src/cut-downs/000-example-piece.md` · `/publishing/src/captions/000-example-piece.en-GB.srt` · `/publishing/src/thumbnails/000-example-piece.md` · `/publishing/src/thumbnails/000-example-piece.html` · `/publishing/src/posts/000-example-piece.md`.
They are spine files: the content follows `BRAND_KIND`, and the cut-down plan and post package hold one gated section per selected platform, or, where the vertical example has nothing for a platform (a podcast feed, the brand's own sites, a newsletter), one gated sentence saying why. The example is never entered in the piece register, the schedule or the publish log, and no template-owned file names it (D43).

### 3.3 Author-owned, pair-only folders
