The template ships only the pair (plus the declared seed, nested ignore or attributes file, README-only sub-folder or example) in: every `docs/project/` and `workflows/local/`; `brand/src/design-system/fonts/`; `brand/src/exports/`; `brand/src/exports/large/` (+ `.gitattributes`); `brand/src/voice/` (+ `voice.md`); `brand/src/platforms/` (+ the platform seeds and `overrides.toml`); `scripts/src/pieces/` (each piece folder then carries its own pair, D42); `production/src/footage/` (+ `manifest.toml`, `raw/README.md`); `production/src/assets/` (small images and text captures, D69); `production/src/edits/`; `production/src/cards/`; `production/src/voiceover/` (+ `generated/README.md`); `production/src/timing/` and `production/src/scenes/` (a piece's tracked timing and scene files, flat under `<piece>.` names, D64; `SM_PAIR_ONLY_GLOBS` lists both); `production/src/audiobook/` (+ `generated/README.md`, `renders/README.md`); `publishing/src/cut-downs/`; `publishing/src/captions/`; `publishing/src/thumbnails/`; `publishing/src/posts/`; `publishing/src/podcast/` (gated `'podcast' in PLATFORMS`: the show registers and the feeds as last published are the author's, and stay when the platform is removed, D59; `SM_PAIR_ONLY_GLOBS` lists it); and, since 0.5.0, `handoffs/`, whose pair is the family's copy-only file rather than a template-owned one (D80): the handoffs in it are the author's, and `SM_PAIR_ONLY_GLOBS` lists it, so a handoff a session leaves there inside `template/` never ships.
The entry format of each lives in its pair (fenced in `CLAUDE.md` Output & naming, or in `CONTEXT.md` What's here), following Section 6.

### 3.4 Template-owned

Everything else: every other governance pair; `docs/reference/**`; `workflows/NN-*/**`; `.claude/rules/syntek-media/**`; `.claude/skills/<skill>/**` for each media skill, and the `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` mode files in the family-shared skill folders (D7; the `SKILL.md` beside them is copy-only shared); the bridge `.claude/syntek-media-agents.md` (D73); since 0.5.0, ungated, the family's resolver and permissions script under media's names, `.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.py` and `.claude/syntek-media-permissions.py`, and media's own data-root map, `.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.json` (D80); `brand/src/design-system/` and `previews/` pairs; `toolkit/**`; since 0.6.0, media's own files under `how-to/docs/syntek-media/`, `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/` and `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/` (D84: read-only, and restored by every full update, Section 3; the rest of `how-to/` is copy-only shared, Section 3.6); every nested `.gitignore` and `.gitattributes`; the `README.md` of every `generated/`, `renders/` and `raw/`; `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml` (Copier's).

### 3.5 Gating table

**Answer gates** (`_exclude` `"<: if not (GATE) :>/path<: endif :>"`, one line per path, 39 lines, the seven client lines syntek-author's, verbatim (D80); index rows use `<: if GATE :>` with the same string, never for a client path (below)):

| Path | Ships when |
|---|---|
| `brand/src/platforms/youtube.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/youtube.md` | `'youtube' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/tiktok.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/tiktok.md` | `'tiktok' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/instagram.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/instagram.md` | `'instagram' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/linkedin.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/linkedin.md` | `'linkedin' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/facebook.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/facebook.md` | `'facebook' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/podcast.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/podcast.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/podcast-feed.md`, `publishing/src/podcast` (folder), `publishing/workflows/08-publish-the-podcast-feed` | `'podcast' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/website.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/website.md` | `'website' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/blog.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/blog.md` | `'blog' in PLATFORMS` |
| `brand/src/platforms/newsletter.md`, `publishing/docs/reference/newsletter.md` | `'newsletter' in PLATFORMS` |
| `scripts/docs/reference/short-video.md` | `'short-video' in MEDIA_KINDS` |
| `scripts/docs/reference/long-video.md` | `'long-video' in MEDIA_KINDS` |
| `scripts/docs/reference/podcast-episodes.md`, `production/workflows/04-master-a-podcast-episode` | `'podcast' in MEDIA_KINDS` |
| `scripts/docs/reference/trailers.md` | `'trailer' in MEDIA_KINDS` |
| `scripts/docs/reference/standalone-voiceovers.md` | `'voiceover' in MEDIA_KINDS` |
| `production/src/audiobook` (folder), `production/workflows/05-narrate-an-audiobook`, `production/workflows/06-master-an-audiobook`, `production/docs/reference/audiobook-narration.md`, `.claude/skills/narrate-audiobook` | `'audiobook' in MEDIA_KINDS` |
| `AGENTS.md` | `'codex' in AGENT_CLIENTS or 'opencode' in AGENT_CLIENTS` |
| `GEMINI.md` | `'gemini-cli' in AGENT_CLIENTS or 'antigravity' in AGENT_CLIENTS` |
| `.agents` (folder: `skills`, `mcp_config.json`) | `AGENT_CLIENTS \| length > 0` |
| `.agents/mcp_config.json` | `'antigravity' in AGENT_CLIENTS` |
| `.codex` (folder) | `'codex' in AGENT_CLIENTS` |
| `.gemini` (folder) | `'gemini-cli' in AGENT_CLIENTS` |
| `opencode.json` | `'opencode' in AGENT_CLIENTS` |
| `.claude/skills/<skill>/BUSINESS.md` for every moded skill, and `MEDIA-BUSINESS.md` in each of the five family-shared skill folders of D7 | `BRAND_KIND == 'business'` |
| `.claude/skills/<skill>/FICTION.md` for every moded skill, and `MEDIA-FICTION.md` in each of the five | `BRAND_KIND == 'author-fiction'` |
| `.claude/skills/<skill>/NONFICTION.md` for every moded skill, and `MEDIA-NONFICTION.md` in each of the five | `BRAND_KIND == 'author-nonfiction'` |

**Seed-once examples** (`_exclude` `"<: if _copier_operation == 'update' or not SEED_EXAMPLES :>/path<: endif :>"`): the eight paths of Section 3.2 ship on copy, when `SEED_EXAMPLES` is true.
**Copy-only shared** (`_exclude` `"<: if _copier_operation == 'update' :>/path<: endif :>"`): the fifty paths of Section 3.6 ship on copy only, except `/.claude/mcp_config.json`, which a third, unconditional `_exclude` line keeps from shipping at all (D76), and the twelve a client gate above names ship only for the clients `AGENT_CLIENTS` ticks; `/.codex` and `/.gemini` also take syntek-author's folder-level update gates (D80). An update renders none of them; since 0.6.0 D81's update script refreshes, offers to or, under `how-to/`, restores some of them after the render (Sections 3 and 3.6).
**Client gates** (D80): they gate copy-only shared paths alone, so they decide what a copy writes and, since 0.6.0, which of a project's existing client files D81's update script offers the two MCP servers to, once each (Section 3.6), and nothing else. An update renders none of those paths and the script never creates one, so a project keeps every client file it has, whatever `AGENT_CLIENTS` records (the first update that asks it takes syntek-author's recorded clients, or else records `[]`, Section 2). So no template-owned file gates a line on `AGENT_CLIENTS` to name one of those paths, which would drop the line, at the next update, from a project that has the file: it names the path in prose, or on a line that carries `<!-- doc-references: variant-only -->`. A copy-only file, rendered once with the copy, may gate such a line, as the template's `README.md` does; and a content block may test the answer (rules 05's model table, D38), repeating each hidden question's `when:` test (Section 2).

Everything else ships always — the voiceover skill, workflow, folder and guide included (D51), and so do `production/src/timing/`, `production/src/scenes/`, `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`, `production/workflows/10-animate-a-scene/` and the guide `production/docs/reference/scenes-as-code.md` (D64, D17, D70), and `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/` and the guide `production/docs/reference/character-voices.md` (D82), ungated as D51 ships voiceover: every brand kind voices and times pieces, no one gate atom names every media kind with a picture, a business can voice a persona as an author voices a character, and outside the client gates the audits combine gate atoms only with 'and', never with 'or'. The family's resolver and permissions script and media's map ship always too (D80), and so, since 0.6.0, do the family's thirteen `how-to/` paths and its guide guard and `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/01-overview.md`, which `_message_after_copy` names, so it exists in every render (D84); no other media path under `how-to/` is gated in 0.6.0, and a media file there names a gated path or a client file in prose (Section 2; above). The per-piece output folders are made by the toolkit at run time and never ship. `toolkit/data/platforms.toml` ships whole in every render; the nested `.gitignore` files cover folders that may be absent, so removing a media kind never removes an ignore rule while author files remain. Bare paths name whole directories; nothing under a gated directory is negated back in, except the `README.md` of a README-only output folder (D42), whose negation is listed above every gated line, so a shut gate, matched last, still wins (D19; `shipped-seeds.sh` check 16).
The mode-file block of `_exclude` (three lines per moded skill and per family-shared skill media writes mode files for, 36 lines, `"<: if BRAND_KIND != 'author-fiction' :>/.claude/skills/<skill>/FICTION.md<: endif :>"`, and `MEDIA-FICTION.md` in a family folder, D7) is **generated** by `.github/scripts/gen-mode-excludes.sh` between `# BEGIN generated mode excludes` and `# END generated mode excludes`; `--check` fails on drift; never type a line there.
**Index files** (Section 2): rules 01 and 02, and the pairs of `brand/`, `brand/src/`, `brand/src/platforms/`, `scripts/`, `scripts/docs/`, `scripts/docs/reference/`, `production/`, `production/docs/`, `production/docs/reference/`, `production/src/`, `production/workflows/`, `publishing/`, `publishing/docs/`, `publishing/docs/reference/`, `publishing/src/` and `publishing/workflows/` (the last two since 0.2.0, as ancestors of the podcast feed's folder and workflow) (and the copy-only `.claude/skills/` pair and root `CONTEXT.md`, spine files in any case). Each either gates the rows that name a gated path or names it only in prose. The ancestors of the examples (`scripts/src/pieces/`, `production/src/edits/`, `production/src/cards/`, `publishing/src/{cut-downs,captions,thumbnails,posts}/`) count as index files to the audit, but name no example (D43).

`_message_before_update` (static; Copier renders it before any answer is loaded) reads:

```text
Before you answer: removing a platform or a media kind deletes EVERY file it generated,
including the seeds you have filled in (git history keeps the last committed copy). Copy out
and commit anything you need first. Your own files are never deleted.

  PLATFORMS without <platform>     brand/src/platforms/<platform>.md (your profile) and
                                   publishing/docs/reference/<platform>.md — for each of
                                   youtube, tiktok, instagram, linkedin, facebook, podcast,
                                   website, blog, newsletter
  PLATFORMS without podcast        also publishing/src/podcast/ (its pair; your show
                                   registers and saved feeds stay),
                                   publishing/workflows/08-publish-the-podcast-feed/ and
                                   publishing/docs/reference/podcast-feed.md
  MEDIA_KINDS without audiobook    the narrate-audiobook skill; production/src/audiobook/ (its
                                   pair and the generated/ and renders/ READMEs; your chapter
                                   registers stay); production/workflows/05-narrate-an-audiobook/,
                                   production/workflows/06-master-an-audiobook/ and
                                   production/docs/reference/audiobook-narration.md
  MEDIA_KINDS without podcast      scripts/docs/reference/podcast-episodes.md and
                                   production/workflows/04-master-a-podcast-episode/
  MEDIA_KINDS without trailer      scripts/docs/reference/trailers.md
  MEDIA_KINDS without short-video  scripts/docs/reference/short-video.md
  MEDIA_KINDS without long-video   scripts/docs/reference/long-video.md
  MEDIA_KINDS without voiceover    scripts/docs/reference/standalone-voiceovers.md (the
                                   voiceover skill and its workflow stay)

A list answer replaces the whole list: --data 'PLATFORMS=[youtube, podcast]' removes every
platform not named.

Crossing 0.4.2, one migration runs after the update (D76): where .agents is a symbolic link
to .claude, it replaces the link with a real .agents/ folder, links .agents/skills to
../.claude/skills and moves .claude/mcp_config.json to .agents/mcp_config.json if that path
is free. A real .agents folder, a link anywhere else or no .agents at all is left as it is
and reported. It deletes nothing you wrote; review its report and commit the change.

Where .agents is a symbolic link to a folder outside this project, every update renames it
to .agents.syntek-held before the render and back afterwards; its target is never
touched. If the update stops before it ends, put the link back in the order the update
printed, or as README.md 'Updating a project' (Migrations), at the template's source,
says. Never move the held link back while .agents still stands.

Updating to 0.4.4: two "ask" entries in .claude/settings.json keep voice remix run, which
spends ElevenLabs credits, from running without a prompt. From 0.6.0 the update adds both
once, where Copier runs on Python 3.14 (never one you removed after committing it). Until
then, python3 toolkit/media.py check --setup reports each missing one as a FAIL with the
line to add. Seeds are never updated, so edit by hand the Consent rule of
brand/src/voice/voice.md (a voice remixed from a clone cites the source clone's consent
ID) and the rules of production/src/credits-log.md (Tool voice remix, the row voice remix
run writes itself).

Updating to 0.5.0: fifteen questions shared with syntek-author are asked once (the coding
agents you use besides Claude Code, their models, and where binary files are kept); their
defaults keep the project as it is, or take what syntek-author's answers file records, and
no client file is written.

Updating to 0.6.0: run Copier on Python 3.14 or later, as README.md 'Updating a project',
at the template's source, shows. On an older one a message framed in lines of = says that
family-update did NOT run, and nothing is reported, refreshed, added or put back; where
.agents links outside the project the update then stops with ForbiddenPathError. Over
syntek-author, update syntek-author to v0.6.0 or later first. This release adds the user
guide, how-to/, read-only: every full update puts its files back as the template wrote
them (Git keeps your version) and never touches a file there that no template ships.
Commit or move any untracked file under how-to/ first, and if how-to is a file or a link
rather than a folder, rename it: the update stops on a file (FileExistsError or
NotADirectoryError) or a link outside the project (ForbiddenPathError), and writes through
a link inside it. If it stops, never commit what it left: README.md 'Updating a project'
gives the recovery.

BRAND_KIND never changes: the update refuses it, because a different brand kind is a new
project. After a platform or media-kind change, edit by hand the files that describe your
options: README.md, CONTEXT.md and .claude/CLAUDE.md Section 1 in a standalone project (over
syntek-author they are syntek-author's), and any rows for a removed platform in
brand/src/platforms/overrides.toml. The brand section of the media rules updates itself.

If this update stops with OSError: [Errno 39] Directory not empty, never commit what it
left: README.md 'Updating a project', at the template's source, gives the recovery and the
command to use.
```

`_message_after_update` (since 0.5.0, D80; it renders answers, and reads `_external_data.peer`, D14) prints, in order: (1) since 0.6.0, in place of 0.5.0's known-limitation line, syntek-author's static lines on its update report with media's names (syntek-author D52; D81): where no '▸ syntek-media update report' line appears above, Copier ran no migration (it could not read a template version), so the owner runs `git status` and looks for `.rej` files and conflict markers; where a message framed in lines of `=` says `family-update` did NOT run, Copier ran on a Python older than 3.14 and nothing was reported, refreshed, added or put back, so the owner upgrades to Python 3.14, looks for `.rej` files and conflict markers, commits, and runs the same update again as `README.md` 'Updating a project', at the template's source, gives it; how to print the report again, `git log -1 --format=%B syntek-media/template`; and that its records live in `.git` under `refs/syntek-media/`, which `git push` leaves behind and `git push --mirror` publishes, that `syntek-media/yours` keeps the owner's `HEAD` at the update reachable, and that both are deleted with `git update-ref -d` before a history rewrite that removes a secret; and that a settings file seeded before the data-root guard shipped gains the guard's `PreToolUse` entry only by hand, as `README.md` 'Updating a project' gives it, because no update of either template offers it (syntek-author D17, D50; D80); (2) only where one differs, each difference between this project's `DATA_STORE`, its layout while both records are `external` (`DATA_AREA`, `DATA_SLUG` and the four kind folders) or its `DATA_PROVIDER` and syntek-author's recorded answers, what it stops (a store or layout difference makes every data command of both templates refuse until the two agree; a provider difference is only reported), and how to agree, each command shown as `python3 .claude/syntek-media-dataroot.py …` (media has no Makefile, D20). It never compares with `_external_data.prev`, which Copier re-reads after writing the new answers, and every test of `DATA_STORE` is an equality with a value, one positive block per value (syntek-author D50). It says nothing of a store media files nothing under (its map is empty).

`_tasks` (every task non-fatal where git is installed — without git the first fails after the files are written, as syntek-author's does, and the toolkit needs git in any case; no `mv` seed tasks): (1) `[ -d .git ] || git init --initial-branch=main`, `when: "<% _copier_operation == 'copy' %>"`; (2) since 0.5.0, syntek-author's `chmod +x .claude/hooks/*.sh 2>/dev/null || true`, verbatim, with the same copy-only `when`, because a checkout of the template does not always carry the hooks' executable bit and an update must never change it (syntek-author D51; D80); (3) the LFS tripwire of D19, `git config --get filter.lfs.process >/dev/null 2>&1 || git config --get filter.lfs.clean >/dev/null 2>&1 || git config filter.lfs.required true || echo '…'`, with the same copy-only `when`; (4) since 0.6.0 (D84), a check that warns, and never fails, where `.copier-answers.syntek-author.yml` stands without syntek-author's guide (its `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-author/01-overview.md`), so syntek-author is older than v0.6.0, which a combined project must not keep: it reads files at run time, never `_external_data` (D84 adds no read to D14's three), with the same copy-only `when`; (5) last, since 0.6.0, syntek-author's copy-only `baseline` task, verbatim (D81; syntek-author D52), `command: '"<% _copier_python %>" "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>/migrations/family-update.py" baseline --src "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>" --to <% _copier_conf.vcs_ref_hash %> --answers "<% _copier_conf.answers_file %>"'`, with the same copy-only `when`, which records the copy's render under `refs/syntek-media/`, offers the listed settings entries and MCP servers to the files the copy kept, refreshes a listed shared file that equals a render recorded before (Section 3.6), needs the repository task (1) makes, and always exits 0; on a Python older than 3.14 it records nothing and says so in a framed message, so the first update renders the old version itself. No manifest pins a task, so a media audit holds `baseline` last, line for line (D81), and no task runs the permissions script (its `--write` is the owner's, D13). `_migrations` with syntek-author's comment block and, since 0.6.0, five entries (D76, D81, D84), in this order: first the unversioned outside-link pair, since 0.4.5 syntek-author's family-shared 'migrations' block, byte for byte, between the marker lines `# BEGIN family-shared migrations (D48)` and `# END family-shared migrations (D48)`, which keep syntek-author's text, so their D48 is syntek-author's number, as a media comment above the BEGIN marker says (D85): `command: '"<% _copier_python %>" "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>/migrations/agents-outside-link.py" hold'` with `when: "<% _stage == 'before' %>"` and the same command with `restore` and `when: "<% _stage == 'after' %>"`; then `version: v0.4.2`, `command: python3 "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>/migrations/v0.4.2-agents-folder.py"`, `when: "<% _stage == 'after' %>"`; then, since 0.6.0, media's own report-only crossing check (D84), `version: v0.6.0`, `command: '"<% _copier_python %>" "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>/migrations/v0.6.0-how-to.py"'`, `when: "<% _stage == 'after' %>"`, which runs once per project, on the update that crosses 0.6.0, writes nothing and always exits 0; and last, since 0.6.0, syntek-author's family-shared 'update' block, byte for byte, between the marker lines `# BEGIN family-shared update (D48)` and `# END family-shared update (D48)`, whose D48 is likewise syntek-author's, as a media comment above that BEGIN marker says (D81): one entry, `command: '"<% _copier_python %>" "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>/migrations/family-update.py" update --src "<% _copier_conf.src_path %>" --to <% _copier_conf.vcs_ref_hash %> --answers "<% _copier_conf.answers_file %>"'` with `when: "<% _stage == 'after' %>"`, unversioned, so that it runs after every other entry on every update and sees what they left. The outside-link pair and the 'update' entry are the only entries not keyed to a release, the two exceptions `.claude/CLAUDE.md` Section 7.7 allows (D76, D81), and a media audit holds the order: the pair first, media's `v0.6.0` entry after `v0.4.2`'s, the 'update' block last (D81, D84). The scripts live in `migrations/` at this repository's root, never shipped, with media's own `migrations/family-refresh.json` beside them (D81); plus the sentence "a release that stops shipping a seed ships a migration that preserves it first".

### 3.6 Copy-only shared files (copy only; never rendered by an update)

`/README.md` · `/CONTEXT.md` · `/.gitignore` · `/.mcp.json` · `/.claude/CLAUDE.md` · `/.claude/CONTEXT.md` · `/.claude/MEMORY.md` · `/.claude/settings.json` · `/.claude/skills/CONTEXT.md` · `/.claude/skills/CLAUDE.md` · `/AGENTS.md` · `/GEMINI.md` · `/.agents` · `/.codex/config.toml` · `/.codex/CONTEXT.md` · `/.codex/CLAUDE.md` · `/.claude/mcp_config.json` (retired, D76) · `/.agents/skills` · `/.agents/mcp_config.json` · and, since 0.5.0 (D80), `/.claude/skills/grill-me/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/skills/grill-with-docs/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/skills/grilling/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/skills/handoff/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/skills/to-questionnaire/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/skills/wait-what/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/skills/wayfinder/SKILL.md` · `/.claude/hooks/pre-compact-handoff.sh` · `/.claude/hooks/context-threshold-handoff.sh` · `/.claude/hooks/CONTEXT.md` · `/.claude/hooks/CLAUDE.md` · `/handoffs/CONTEXT.md` · `/handoffs/CLAUDE.md` · `/.gemini/settings.json` · `/.gemini/CONTEXT.md` · `/.gemini/CLAUDE.md` · `/opencode.json` · and, since 0.6.0 (D84), `/how-to/CONTEXT.md` · `/how-to/CLAUDE.md` · `/how-to/docs/CONTEXT.md` · `/how-to/docs/CLAUDE.md` · `/how-to/src/CONTEXT.md` · `/how-to/src/CLAUDE.md` · `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/CONTEXT.md` · `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/CLAUDE.md` · `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/agent-clients-and-mcp.md` · `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/the-data-root.md` · `/how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/updating-and-recovery.md` · `/how-to/workflows/CONTEXT.md` · `/how-to/workflows/CLAUDE.md` · `/.claude/hooks/how-to-read-only.sh`.
Each has two lines in `copier.yml`: anchored in `_skip_if_exists` (so a copy over an existing file skips rather than aborts, and syntek-author's coexist test classes it as a seed) and `- "<: if _copier_operation == 'update' :>/path<: endif :>"` in `_exclude` (so an update never renders it, and Copier never recreates a file the author deleted: D81's script writes a missing listed file only where the owner never had it, and D84's restore puts back one deleted under `how-to/`, below). They are spine files: they may carry variant tokens, because they render once. **Thirty-nine of them are syntek-author's family-shared bytes** (D80, D84): `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.agents/skills`, `.agents/mcp_config.json`, the three `.codex/` files, the seventeen of 0.5.0 and the fourteen of 0.6.0 above, each byte for byte from syntek-author's `v0.7.0` tag (D81) and never edited here; the other eleven are media's own, `.claude/settings.json` among them. 'Family-shared' names a file of syntek-author's manifest (its resolver and permissions script among them, template-owned here, Section 3.4), 'copy-only' this class (D11). Twelve ship only for the clients `AGENT_CLIENTS` ticks (Section 3.5). In a combined project they are whichever template copied first, the same bytes where both are at one tag, and since 0.6.0 either template's update refreshes a listed family-shared one only from its own render history, keeping and reporting one that holds the other's version (syntek-author D51), and nothing media needs lives only in them (D5): a media rule that routes to a family skill keeps a fallback for its absence (D10, D35).
`/.agents` is a folder path: its `_skip_if_exists` line matches every path beneath it, and its update gate keeps an update out of the whole folder, so an update never writes a folder over a 0.4.1 `.agents -> .claude` link; `/.agents/skills` and `/.agents/mcp_config.json` carry their own lines so each is checked path by path. `/.claude/mcp_config.json` keeps its file and both lines and gains an unconditional `_exclude` line, so no copy writes it (D76). `/.codex` and `/.gemini` take syntek-author's folder-level update gates, an `_exclude` line each and no `_skip_if_exists` line (D80), so an update never creates an empty client folder in a project that never had one. The fourteen of D84 take their lines file by file, as syntek-author's `copier.yml` writes them, never a line naming `/how-to` or a folder under it, because each of those folders also holds media's own template-owned files under its `syntek-media/` folder.

**After the render, since 0.6.0** (D81, which supersedes D11 for the paths it names; syntek-author D51, D52): where Copier runs on Python 3.14 or later, the family update script, run by the 'update' block and by the copy's `baseline` task (Section 3.5), acts on these paths as media's own `migrations/family-refresh.json` lists them. It **refreshes** the twenty-seven family-shared files of its `refresh` list (the seven `SKILL.md`, the three hooks with the `.claude/hooks/` pair, the `handoffs/` pair and the thirteen `how-to/` files): one is replaced only where it equals a revision of the project's render history and differs from the new render, and written only where it is absent and the owner never had it; one the owner deleted stays deleted outside `how-to/`, an edited one, or one holding a sibling template's version, is kept and reported, and a hook it writes gets mode 755. It **offers** `.claude/settings.json` each settings entry and top-level value the list names (D13, D81), and the two MCP servers to `.mcp.json` and, where the answers record their client, to `.agents/mcp_config.json`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.gemini/settings.json` and `opencode.json` (Section 3.7), never to `.claude/mcp_config.json` (D76): each once, into the owner's own text, only where the new render holds it, never removing, reordering or weakening an entry and never adding back one it counts as offered. 'Once' is counted per template, from media's own record or, where the clone holds none, from the committed history of the file, which also decides for a top-level value or MCP server the record does not list (Section 10), so the lists stay stable from 0.6.0 on; and the data-root guard is never listed, so it reaches new copies only (D81; syntek-author D17, D50). It **restores** every file the new render holds under `how-to/`, the thirteen included (D84; Section 3). Its **report** lists, each with a compare and a take command, every one of these paths the new version changed whose copy still differs. The other seventeen (`/README.md`, `/CONTEXT.md`, `/.gitignore`, `/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `/.claude/CONTEXT.md`, `/.claude/MEMORY.md`, the `.claude/skills/` pair, `/AGENTS.md`, `/GEMINI.md`, `/.agents`, `/.agents/skills`, the `.codex/` and `.gemini/` pairs and `/.claude/mcp_config.json`) are never written after the copy.

---

### 3.7 Agent support (D73)

- **Copy-only shared:** `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, the `.agents/` folder with `.agents/skills` and `.agents/mcp_config.json`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.codex/CONTEXT.md` and `.codex/CLAUDE.md`; since 0.5.0 `.gemini/settings.json`, `.gemini/CONTEXT.md`, `.gemini/CLAUDE.md` and `opencode.json`, every client file now syntek-author's bytes, written by a copy only for the clients `AGENT_CLIENTS` ticks (D80); `.claude/mcp_config.json` keeps its lines but no longer ships (D76). Each uses both protections of Section 3.6, including the link itself. A pre-existing `.agents` other than the 0.4.1 link, or a `.agents/skills` that is a real directory or a differently targeted link, is preserved and reported for manual setup, never replaced or merged by force.
- **Existing directory copies:** Copier can fail before its skip check when a symbolic link would land on a real directory. Before a copy, where `.agents` exists but is not a real folder (a file, or a link, the 0.4.1 `.agents -> .claude` included), preserve it and pass `--exclude /.agents`; where `.agents/skills` is a real directory, preserve it and pass `--exclude /.agents/skills`. Updates already exclude the folder. Report that agent integration requires manual setup; never rename or remove either to make the copy succeed, except an `.agents` link that leaves the project, which Copier refuses before `_exclude` is read: its copy recipe moves the link aside and back by hand (`mv .agents .agents.syntek-held`, copy with `--exclude /.agents`, `mv .agents.syntek-held .agents`), and updates do the same through D76's unversioned hold/restore pair, on Python 3.14 or later: below it the pair holds nothing and says so in a framed message, and the update stops with `ForbiddenPathError` until it is run again on 3.14 (D81, D85).
- **One skill tree:** `.claude/skills/` is the only skill tree; `.agents/skills` is a relative link to `../.claude/skills`, rendered with Copier’s `_preserve_symlinks: true`. `.agents` itself is never a link: Codex mounts it read-only and its sandbox refuses a read-only path that links to a writable folder, and a whole-folder link exposed `.claude/rules/` to Antigravity (D76). Where Git checks the link out as a text file (`core.symlinks=false`), the bridge says how to recreate it. Test link preservation on generation, coexistence and updates, and prove that platform and media-kind gates expose the same skills through the link.
- **Entrypoints:** the root `AGENTS.md` and `GEMINI.md` read every `.claude/syntek-*-agents.md` present (a new copy only: both are copy-only). The template-owned `.claude/syntek-media-agents.md` ships always and reads every other `.claude/syntek-*-agents.md` present (D76). It reads the active rules recursively, `.claude/MEMORY.md`, the applicable folder pair, local overrides and project rules, including the companion’s project settings where present. Since 0.5.0 it also carries syntek-author's guidance on the two MCP servers (rules 06 Section 13; the warm-up and browser-download commands, which the owner runs and a session never does) and on the permissions script, and the session-boundary thresholds for clients that run no hook (D80). Since 0.6.0 it also says that the user guide, `how-to/`, is read-only in every client, and that of `opencode.json`'s four edit denies three name another template's paths and are inert here, while the fourth, `how-to/*`, is the guide's (D84). Do not depend on Claude’s automatic imports or rule discovery in another client.
- **Settings:** the client files are syntek-author's family bytes (D80; syntek-author D47, D54). `.codex/config.toml` enables `CLAUDE.md` as a fallback instruction filename and writes `CODEX_MODEL` where it is answered; `.gemini/settings.json` writes `GEMINI_MODEL`; `opencode.json` writes the provider the `OPENCODE_*` answers name; none sets a trust level, a permission bypass, a default approval mode or an allow rule. Antigravity’s MCP configuration lives at `.agents/mcp_config.json` (D76), byte-identical to `.mcp.json`; Codex’s uses its own TOML format. Each declares the family's two pinned, keyless servers, `code-review-graph` and `scrapling`, with the ask forms the permissions script derives from syntek-author's seed rules; media's own seed rules reach them through `python3 .claude/syntek-media-permissions.py --write` (D13). Since 0.6.0 D81's update script, and the copy's `baseline` task, add the two servers, once each, to each of these files that exists for a recorded client, at `mcpServers`, at `mcp_servers` in `.codex/config.toml` and at `mcp.servers` in `opencode.json`; to `opencode.json` and `.gemini/settings.json` only where the file already holds every ask form the permissions script derives, and otherwise the report gives that `--write` and the next update adds them (syntek-author D51). No credential, ElevenLabs server or paid call ships. Document local authentication and mapping an available tool by capability when its client name differs.
- **Existing projects:** new copy-only entrypoints and client files never arrive through an update; since 0.6.0 the family-shared files `migrations/family-refresh.json` lists do, through D81's update script on Python 3.14 (Section 3.6). Document an explicit setup for missing files, and how to route an existing entrypoint to the bridge without overwriting project instructions. Keep the bridge template-owned so updates maintain the production contract. The exceptions are D76's, the `v0.4.2` migration, which replaces a `.agents -> .claude` link with the folder, and the transient hold/restore pair for an `.agents` link that leaves the project, and D81's: its update script adds the two MCP servers once to a recorded client's MCP file that exists, and never creates or otherwise changes a client file. The bridge's enabling-an-existing-project section gives the steps by hand for every other case, and media's procedure for enabling a client, under `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/`, follows it step by step with media's exact command (D84).
- **Development:** add root development `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, a real `.agents/` folder (its pair only; `skills -> ../.claude/skills` only if the development `.claude/skills/` exists; never a link, never an MCP file, never a path into `template/`, D76) and `.codex/config.toml`, directing agents to the development manual and DESIGN.md. Keep product skills and product instruction files out of development work. Codex honours skill rules only in user configuration (0.160.1), so the project `.codex/config.toml` carries none: development Codex sessions start at the repository root, the user-level `SKILL.md` rule is documented in `.codex/CLAUDE.md`, and the audit refuses an inert project rule. The one development MCP server is `code-review-graph` (root `.mcp.json` and `.codex/config.toml`, pinned, its graph in the ignored `/.code-review-graph/`; launched with `--tools` omitting `embed_graph_tool` and with the cloud-embedding keys blanked in its `env`, so no client is offered it and the server holds no key; `embed_graph_tool` also denied, and `ask` rules on `apply_refactor_tool` and `generate_wiki_tool`; its `install` command is never run here). Development configuration is never shipped.
- **Verification:** extend existing audits with link, configuration, entrypoint, isolation, overwrite-protection and migration cases (a `.agents -> .claude` or `.claude/` link, a real folder, a link elsewhere, a missing `.agents`, an existing `.agents/mcp_config.json`, and a second run); retain the full coexistence and update audits. The client configuration does not translate Claude permissions into another client’s native enforcement; explicit author requests and the spending rules govern tool use in every entrypoint.

---

## 4. The generated tree

### 4.1 Root and `.claude/`

```text
<project>/
├── .copier-answers.syntek-media.yml   ← Copier answers (do not hand-edit); beside syntek-author's own
├── README.md · CONTEXT.md · .gitignore · .mcp.json   ← copy-only shared (written only where absent)
├── AGENTS.md · GEMINI.md · opencode.json · .agents/ · .codex/ · .gemini/   ← copy-only shared, for the clients AGENT_CLIENTS ticks (D73, D76, D80)
├── handoffs/                 ← the family's pair, copy-only shared (D80); the handoffs themselves are the author's
├── how-to/                   ← the user guide (D84), read-only and put back by every full update: the family's five pairs and three shared chapters, copy-only shared; media's guides, chapters and procedures under its three syntek-media/ folders, template-owned (Section 4.4)
├── .claude/
│   ├── CLAUDE.md · CONTEXT.md · MEMORY.md · settings.json   ← copy-only shared
│   ├── syntek-media-agents.md · syntek-media-dataroot.py · syntek-media-permissions.py · syntek-media-dataroot.json   ← template-owned: the bridge (D73), the family's resolver and permissions script, media's empty map (D80)
│   ├── hooks/                ← the family's two handoff hooks, the guide guard how-to-read-only.sh and their pair, copy-only shared, refreshed while unchanged (D80, D81, D84)
│   ├── rules/syntek-media/   ← template-owned rules, loaded at launch; NO pair here
│   └── skills/               ← pair (copy-only shared) + one folder per media skill (template-owned) + the seven family-shared skills (SKILL.md copy-only shared; media's MEDIA-<KIND>.md mode files template-owned)
├── brand/ · scripts/ · production/ · publishing/   ← production layers (docs/ src/ workflows/)
└── toolkit/                  ← supporting layer (flat): media.py, card.py, transcribe.py, scene.py, data/, templates/
```

In a combined project syntek-author's answers file, `Makefile`, `.claude/rules/syntek-author/` (with its project-owned `00-project.md`), its own skills, bridge, scripts and data-root map, `learning/`, `assets/`, layers and its own guide files under the three `syntek-author/` folders of `how-to/` sit beside these (syntek-author v0.6.0 or later, updated before media, D84), and the copy-only files, the family-shared ones included, are whichever template copied first: syntek-author's, on the intended path (D4); since 0.6.0 either template's update replaces a family-shared file only while it holds a version one of that template's own renders held, and keeps and reports one holding the other template's version (D81; syntek-author D51).

**`.claude/rules/syntek-media/`** (each ≤ 300 lines, about 1,000 together (D5), no `paths` frontmatter, H2s numbered `## N. Title` and separated by `---`, rules as `**Requirement.**` followed by `**Why this rule exists.**`). Each opens with the metadata header and this one-line blockquote, syntek-author's current wording adapted:
``> **Template-owned.** Shipped by syntek-media and replaced by every `copier update`: never edit it here. Where syntek-author's project settings file (00-project.md) is present, it outranks this file and says where project rules go; otherwise they go in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, under the heading 'Project-specific rules'.``

| File | Content (section numbers are cited by other files and are frozen) |
|---|---|
| `01-layout-and-routing.md` | 1 The brand (rendered from the answers: name, kind, description, audience test, owner and chat name, platforms, media kinds; "this is the brand brief in a combined project") · 2 Layers (`\| Layer \| Kind \| Purpose \|`; the router reads these four layers' workflow indexes) · 3 The kinds of layer (production, supporting, generated, and the shared reference folder `how-to/`, the user guide: read, never produced in, and read-only, its procedures run as their folder says, step by step with the checklist open, stopping for the owner at every step that changes the template's files or a settings file, and never routed by `run-media-workflow`, D84; media has no working folders of its own: `learning/` and `assets/` belong to syntek-author where it is applied, and `handoffs/` is the family's, its pair copy-only, D80) · 4 Sublayers of a production layer (syntek-author's table verbatim; workflows cited by full folder name, D32) · 5 The piece (D17; scripted and recorded, D46) · 6 The folder pair (rule verbatim; syntek-author's exceptions verbatim, then media's: each `generated/`, `renders/` and `raw/` (README only), and nothing in a folder inside one, such as a per-piece output folder (D42); a `handoffs/` folder that lacks the family's pair, where Claude never writes one (D35); every piece folder carries its pair) · 7 Routing frontmatter (verbatim; no `agent:` key) · 8 Who owns which files (the five classes; the copy-only shared files, the family-shared ones told apart from the rest: an update never renders a copy-only file, but D81's update script replaces a listed family-shared file (the working-practice skills, the hooks with their pair, the `handoffs/` pair and the shared how-to files) that the owner never changed, writes one the project never had, and keeps and reports one the owner edited or another template's version, and never refreshes the family-shared client files, D81; `settings.json`, `.mcp.json` and the MCP files of the clients the answers record, to which the copy and every update add the listed entries and servers once each, never one counted as offered (Section 10), D81; the files under `how-to/`, read-only and put back by every full update, D84; the family's template-owned scripts, D80; the seeds; the D76 migration) · 9 Read order · 10 Project settings and precedence (in a combined project syntek-author's 00-project.md, where present, outranks these rules: its Paths say where project rules and handoffs go, its Memory headings map `MEMORY.md`, its Workflow aliases may name a media workflow by full folder path, its Overrides may name a media rules section; standalone, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` Section 3; a departure from the guide in `how-to/` is recorded in the same places, Overrides or Workflow aliases where present and otherwise `.claude/CLAUDE.md` Section 3, D84). Two coexistence sentences: these layers sit beside any syntek-author lists; `README.md`, root `CONTEXT.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/skills/CONTEXT.md` may have been written by another template and not mention them — this file is the authority. About 210 lines. |
| `02-skills.md` | 1 Skills only ("no commands and no agents"; media's skills are template-owned by syntek-media even where `.claude/skills/CONTEXT.md` calls a skill outside another template's roster "the author's own"; never recreate a missing skill) · 2 Modes (the rendered sentence "The mode file in this project is `X.md`."; the paragraph; the four H2s; a mode file applies only when its `SKILL.md` carries the paragraph; in a family-shared skill's folder media work reads `MEDIA-X.md`, and another template's mode files beside it apply only to its work, D7) · 3 Media skills (`\| Skill \| What it does \| Mode \|`, the `narrate-audiobook` row gated) · 4 Family-shared skills and companions (the seven skills of Section 5.3 of this file, and a role table that gives each role they name its media path, D80; then the companions that stay syntek-author's, Section 5.2) · 5 Working with skills. About 185 lines. |
| `03-production-ethics.md` | 1 Who decides what (`\| Decision \| Who decides \| What the AI does \|`: script and transcript approval, voice choice, every credit-spending call, every cut approved, every publish — "Nothing the AI proposes takes effect until the author has seen it and said yes") · 2 The production loop (diagram + table: brief → script or recording → storyboard → voiceover/footage → master → cut-downs → captions → thumbnail → post → log; for a scene piece, the voice joined → its words timed and its mouths cued → the storyboard re-timed → stills, the mouths checked, and a preview render → a master per aspect, through M4's sub-checks, Section 6.1) · 3 The piece ladder (owns the requirement, D17: M1–M7 in `scripts/docs/reference/the-piece-ladder.md` bind every piece; a project guide of that name may add a check, never remove or weaken a gate; a one-line summary of each gate, and of M4's four sub-checks for a scene piece) · 4 Credits: never spend unasked (D21, D44, D52; no batch until `media.py check --setup` reports its ElevenLabs lines clean, D49) · 5 AI disclosure (D25: each platform's rule for its toggle; a description line always) · 6 Rights and consent (D40) · 7 Never fabricate · 8 The two flags (D37) · 9 Rules for this brand kind (gated blocks). About 200 lines. |
| `04-toolkit-pipeline.md` | 1 The pipeline (diagram: tokens → HTML → `card.py` → PNG; footage, cards and voice → `assemble` → master → `cut`/`encode` → deliverables → `captions burn`; `speak plan` → ElevenLabs → `generated/<piece>/takes/` → `take add`; approved takes → `voice join` → `transcribe` and `lipsync` → `cues`; a scene file → the kit's page → `scene.py stills`, then `scene.py render` → a master per aspect) · 2 The commands (table from Section 4.2; "where this file and `--help` disagree, `--help` is right") · 3 Rules (renders are generated and never hand-edited; probe the output before reporting it; never `-c copy` for a frame-accurate cut; standard library only except the declared `uv run` scripts (`card.py`, `transcribe.py`, `scene.py`), which `media.py` starts with a named interpreter (D20), and JavaScript only inside the page the scene kit writes (D70); author-edited data TOML, tool-written timing and scene data JSON with finite numbers; never write outside `renders/` or `generated/` without `-o`, and over a tracked file only through the named exceptions, a timing or scene file only while Git holds it committed and unchanged (D66); no network but `transcribe fetch`, which the author runs, and the approved `score run` and `voice remix run` calls (D74, D82), uv's first install of a script's packages aside (D20); nothing reads what Git ignores, D50) · 4 What the toolkit needs (`\| Tool \| For \|`: ffmpeg/ffprobe with libass, x264, mp3lame; Python 3.14 or later (D81); uv; Playwright Chromium; git-lfs for large exports; Rhubarb Lip Sync 1.14.0 (`lipsync`), WhisperX 3.8.6 on a Python from 3.10 to 3.13 kept beside 3.14, in uv's environment or at the interpreter path `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` names, never an environment the author activates, with its fetched files (`transcribe`, D67), espeak-ng, `systemd-run` and fontconfig's `fc-match` (`card.py --self-test`'s brand-font probe), each only for the commands that need it, a note when absent (D49); `check --setup` reports them all). "If a tool is missing, say which one and which command it blocks; never report a command as passed when it could not run." About 125 lines. |
| `05-model-allocation.md` | 1 The two tiers (scoped to media work; `**<%MODEL_MECHANICAL%>**` and `**opus**`; "No Haiku"; fallback to sonnet, never lower; "When in doubt, the work is substantive") · 2 How the tiers are named in files (checklist tags `· _opus_` / `· _sonnet_`, identical to syntek-author's) · 3 Why · 4 Other coding agents (only where `AGENT_CLIENTS` ticks a client: syntek-author's per-client table, one row per ticked client, its model from the family questions, D38, D80). About 85 lines. |
| `06-global-rules.md` | syntek-author's thirteen sections, in its numbers and briefly in its wording, so a combined session sees agreement: 1 Locale (D23, D29) · 2 Route; do not restate · 3 Never self-edit (and the user guide is read-only: nothing under `how-to/` is edited, written, moved or deleted but through a template's update, its recovery and the commands an update report prints for a file there, in syntek-author's words, D84) · 4 Never overwrite (masters, source media, exports, takes; a new version goes beside the old one, except a tracked timing or scene file, which only its own command replaces, through `-o` and while Git holds the old version, D66) · 5 One sentence per line, in `src/` only (and in the guide's chapters in `how-to/src/`, which the template writes so, D84) · 6 The 300-line cap (`src/` artefacts and `README.md` exempt, the guide's chapters in `how-to/src/` not, D84) · 7 Proofreading is supportive, and a report · 8 Design work opens with a grilling pass (a brief, a brand kit, the spoken voice: the `grill-with-docs` skill, family-shared since 0.5.0, D80; where its `SKILL.md` is absent, the same questions in rounds, each with a recommended answer) · 9 Internal notes for deliberate deviations (`<!-- INTERNAL NOTE: … -->` under the frontmatter; not a flag) · 10 Confidentiality (API keys at user scope only; never in `.mcp.json` or a committed file; voice IDs and account details only in the brand's own files) · 11 Answers change through Copier (`.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`, `-a`, Copier run as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier`, D81; `BRAND_KIND` never changes; removals delete files; a client ticked on update brings only its row of the model table, never its files, D80, and the update script offers the two MCP servers once to those of its files that already exist, D81) · 12 What Git ignores stays out of the session (D50; a still `scene.py stills` has just written is opened by the path it printed, to look at it, D71; and the data root, whatever the store, D80: media's map claims no folder, the `DATA_*` answers only keep the family's records in agreement, and a disagreement with syntek-author's stops the resolver's data commands) · 13 Web and code tools (media's own numbered heading over syntek-author's 'rules-web-tools' block, byte for byte between its two marker lines, D80). About 275 lines. |
| `07-session-boundaries.md` | 1 The rule (D35) · 2 The steps (the `handoff` skill, family-shared since 0.5.0, D80, where present; otherwise the file by hand, where syntek-author's 00-project.md Paths say, where present; never a pair in the handoffs folder) · 3 What a media handoff never drops · 4 How it is enforced (`autoCompactEnabled` and `idleCompaction` both `false`, so compaction starts neither as the window fills nor while a session sits idle; the family's two hooks, which the seed registers in four entries, D13: the context-threshold hook advises at half the window and insists at three quarters, and on a Python older than 3.14 measures nothing and warns; the PreCompact hook blocks automatic compaction and lets a deliberate `/compact` through, after which the `SessionStart` `compact` entry says that a handoff gives a cleaner break, syntek-author D29; to a settings file the project already had, the copy and every update add each key and entry once where it is absent, keep a value the author set and never add back one counted as offered (Section 10), D81; other clients run no hook and watch their own window, D80). About 80 lines. |
| `08-naming-and-memory.md` | 1 Naming conventions (Section 6.16, the guide's names among them: `TEMPLATE-GUIDE/`, the one upper-case folder, the shared chapters, and media's numbered chapters and procedures and its guides, D84) · 2 What goes in `MEMORY.md`, and what goes elsewhere (voice and model IDs → `voice.md`; publish status → the publish log; piece status → the brief; each heading as syntek-author's 00-project.md Memory headings map it, where present) · 3 The memory gate (syntek-author's three tests) · 4 Entries: dated, and superseded, never deleted. About 90 lines. |

Rules files name syntek-author's paths and its `00-project.md` **unbackticked** (doc-references check 6); a heading such as Paths is written in prose.

**`.claude/CLAUDE.md` (copy-only shared)**: syntek-author's skeleton — metadata header with `**Timezone**`, `@../CONTEXT.md`, the read-order blockquote naming `.claude/rules/syntek-media/` — then `## 1. Project` (the brand brief: name in its exact form, kind, description, audience test, platforms, media kinds, the owner's chat name, a near-term goal as `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM`), `## 2. Where the rules live` (the eight files; template-owned, never edit), `## 3. Project-specific rules` (empty). Standalone, the rules' blockquote points to Section 3; over syntek-author this file is syntek-author's, and its own project settings say where project rules go.
**`.claude/CONTEXT.md`**: syntek-author's shape; the tree shows `rules/syntek-media/` and quotes "Another template applied to this project would add its own folder beside it."; since 0.5.0 a `hooks/` row (the family's, D80) and rows for the bridge, the two family scripts and the map.
**`.claude/MEMORY.md`**: syntek-author's file with the same six H2s (`Facts`, `Decisions`, `Feedback`, `Status`, `Open questions`, `Sensitivities`) and entry format; the Facts prompt names `Release cadence:`, `Next publish date:` and `Audience test:`; the gate pointer cites `.claude/rules/syntek-media/08-naming-and-memory.md` Section 3.
**`.claude/settings.json`**: D13 exactly; valid JSON in every render (no blocks needed). To one the project already had, the copy's `baseline` task and every update add the entries and values `migrations/family-refresh.json` lists, once each, never one counted as offered (Section 10) and never the data-root guard, which is not listed (D81).
**`.claude/skills/` pair**: template-neutral — "the roster of each template lives in `.claude/rules/<template>/02-skills.md`"; the mode-file line names `BUSINESS.md`, `FICTION.md`, `NONFICTION.md`, and since 0.5.0 `MEDIA-BUSINESS.md`, `MEDIA-FICTION.md` and `MEDIA-NONFICTION.md` beside a family-shared `SKILL.md` (D7, D80).
**`README.md`**: syntek-author's H2 order adapted — Overview (brand, kind, platforms, media kinds) · How the repository is laid out (since 0.6.0 with the user guide, `how-to/`, read-only, its first chapter named by full path, D84) · The production loop · Producing (toolkit commands; requirements; `check --setup`) · Working with Claude Code · Conventions · Updating from the template (`-a .copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`, Copier run as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier`, D81, with Git's housekeeping off and a pointer to the recovery in syntek-media's own `README.md`; `BRAND_KIND` never changes; the MissingFileWarning is expected; since 0.6.0, that the update brings the listed family-shared files the owner never changed and adds the listed settings entries and MCP servers once, and that any other shared file or settings entry new in a later release arrives only by hand, from a fresh copy, D80, D81) · Removing a platform or a media kind deletes its files · Other coding agents (D73; since 0.5.0 the clients `AGENT_CLIENTS` ticks, the model table and the permissions script's `--write`, D80).
**Root `CONTEXT.md`**: syntek-author's three H2s; the tree lists the four layers, `toolkit/`, `.claude/rules/syntek-media/` and the answers file, and since 0.5.0 `.claude/hooks/`, `handoffs/` and each client's files, those rows gated on the client's atom, which a copy-only file may do because it renders once (Section 3.5, D80); since 0.6.0 also `how-to/`, the read-only user guide (D84).
**`.gitignore`**: syntek-author's skeleton minus TeX, SQLite and Word sections (Credentials, Claude Code local settings, OS, Editor, Python, Temporary). It ignores **no** media extension and no media folder — those rules are nested (D19) — and it matches no shipped path; since 0.5.0 it also ignores `/.code-review-graph/`, the local map of syntek-author D54's code-review-graph server (new copies only, D11, D80).
**`.mcp.json`**: since 0.5.0 syntek-author's family bytes (D80; syntek-author D54), byte-identical to `.agents/mcp_config.json`: `code-review-graph` and `scrapling`, pinned and keyless, code-review-graph's six cloud-embedding variables blank and `embed_graph_tool` withheld by `--tools`; no ElevenLabs server (D21). Before 0.5.0 it was exactly `{"mcpServers": {}}`, which an existing project keeps, and to which, since 0.6.0, the copy's `baseline` task (over an existing file) and every update add the two servers once each, never one counted as offered (D81, Section 10).
**`.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.py` and `.claude/syntek-media-permissions.py`** (template-owned, ungated, D80): syntek-author's family-shared resolver (syntek-author D50, D53) and permissions script (syntek-author D49), byte for byte under media's names, with no delimiter and no template name; Python 3.14 or later (syntek-author D18; D81): below it the resolver's commands and the permissions script do nothing and exit 2, and the resolver's guard lets the call through and warns; standard library only, no network. The resolver reads every `.claude/syntek-*-dataroot.json` and every `.copier-answers.syntek-*.yml`; the permissions script carries `settings.json`'s deny and ask rules into the client files that exist, and writes only when the owner runs `--write`.
**`.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.json`** (template-owned, ungated, token-free, media's own, D80): `"format": 1` and an empty `"folders"` list, nothing else. It must never name, contain or lie inside a folder another template's map names (syntek-author's: `library/src`, `research/src`, `proposal/src`, `assets`, `world/src`).
**The family-shared skills, hooks, `handoffs/` pair and `how-to/` files** (copy-only shared, D80, D84): syntek-author's bytes at its `v0.8.0` tag (D81), Sections 4.4 and 5.3, each listed in `migrations/family-refresh.json`, so every update replaces one the owner never changed, writes one the project never had, and keeps and reports one the owner edited or another template's version (D81); under `how-to/`, the restore puts each back where it differs, another template's version excepted (D84).

### 4.2 `toolkit/` (supporting, flat; template-owned)

```text
toolkit/
├── CONTEXT.md · CLAUDE.md
├── .gitignore            ← __pycache__/
├── media.py              ← the one CLI (python3, standard library); --self-test covers every module
├── media_common.py       ← TOML, timecodes, presets and brand overrides, paths, the ffmpeg runner
├── media_video.py        ← assemble, cut, encode, frame, still-video
├── media_audio.py        ← extract-audio, loudness, audiobook, take, speak plan, voice join, levels, lipsync; transcribe's launcher
├── media_captions.py     ← captions * (from-words included), script time, cues
├── media_repo.py         ← footage, tokens, flags, check, where, real
├── media_image.py        ← image deliverables, and the GIF pass of cut (D57)
├── media_feed.py         ← the podcast show register and its skeleton, the feed, chapters and the feed audio's ID3 tags (D58, D59)
├── media_score.py        ← title–thumbnail requests, Jev transport and offline result validation (D74)
├── media_voice.py        ← voice remix: the offline request, the one direct ElevenLabs call, the previews, their record and the credits-log row (D82)
├── media_scene.py        ← the scene kit (standard library): every frame's state and the page a scene file writes (D70)
├── card.py               ← HTML/CSS → PNG (uv run; PEP 723: playwright==1.63.0)
├── transcribe.py         ← word timing (PEP 723: whisperx==3.8.6, Python below 3.14; D67), started only by media.py transcribe
├── scene.py              ← a scene's stills and its masters, frame by frame (uv run; PEP 723: playwright==1.63.0; D71)
├── data/                 ← pair + platforms.toml · packaging-score.toml
└── templates/            ← pair + thumbnail.html · card.html (fallbacks for the brand's components, D18)
```

`--deliverable KEY` takes a `<platform>.<format>` key of `platforms.toml` (`youtube.short`, `podcast.apple_rss_audio`, `audiobook.acx`). Timecodes `HH:MM:SS.mmm`. Exit codes everywhere: **0** done and verified (or clean); **1** a finding — a check failed, or an output failed its post-render verification; **2** could not run — bad arguments, missing input, a missing tool (named, with its install hint), or the tool itself failed. Outputs go to a `renders/` or `generated/` folder by default, a piece's into its own folder there (names and folders in Section 6.16, D64); nothing is written elsewhere unless `-o` names a path, and nothing outside those folders is ever overwritten, with the exceptions below. **The commands that make tracked files — `captions align`, `retime`, `rewrap`, `vtt` and `transcript`, and `feed write` — write to `-o` or, without it, to standard output** (their files belong in the tracked `publishing/src/captions/` and `publishing/src/podcast/`, where the toolkit never chooses a path), and `--help` says so. **The timing and scene commands — `transcribe`, `lipsync`, `cues` and `real` — write a working copy to `production/src/renders/<piece>/timing/`**, and the tracked copy only where `-o` names it; `levels` writes only its working copy there (D66). **The exceptions**, each written only by the commands named for it: the registers and the manifest — `take add`, `footage add`, and for a show register `feed new`, `feed add` and `feed tag` (D59) — none of which ever rewrites a value the author set, except that `take add` resets a segment's `status` to `generated` and its `archived` to empty, because its new take is unheard and unarchived (a re-roll, Section 6.6), and `feed new --rekey` rewrites only the values it wrote, and only before anything is published; **a show's tracked feed `publishing/src/podcast/<show>.feed.xml`**, which only `feed write -o` overwrites, after its GUID comparison passes, through a temporary file renamed over it (D59); and **a piece's tracked timing and scene files** — `production/src/timing/<piece>.words.json`, `<piece>.words-check.md` and `<piece>.mouth.json`, and `production/src/scenes/<piece>.cues.json` and `<piece>.real.json` — which only the command that makes each overwrites, with `-o` naming it, only while Git reports no uncommitted change to it, through a temporary file renamed over it (D66). `extract-audio` writes `production/src/renders/<piece>/<stem>[.<in>-<out>].wav` by default, or at the folder's top where `<stem>` begins with no piece key, such as a footage file's (ruling T5).

| Command (`python3 toolkit/media.py …`) | Arguments | What it runs | Exit |
|---|---|---|---|
| `probe` | `FILE [--json]` | ffprobe: duration, size, every stream (codec, size, rate, sample rate, channels, pix_fmt); a `.pcm` take read with the format of `--output-format` | 0 · 2 |
| `score plan` / `score run` | `INPUT [--rubric TOML] [--model NAME] [--today DD/MM/YYYY] [-o JSON]`; run requires `--approve-call` | offline request preview / one approved TypeSafe request; validated scores, ranking and review findings; text concepts only, no automatic approval (D74) | 0 · 1 · 2 |
| `presets` | `[KEY] [--stale-after DAYS --today DD/MM/YYYY]` | prints deliverable tables with their `verify` keys and every brand override applied; with `--stale-after`, lists tables whose `checked` is older | 0 · 1 (stale, only with `--stale-after`) · 2 |
| `script time` | `PATH [--wpm N] [--write]` | counts spoken words (tags, braces and cue lines removed), adds `{pause}` seconds, per beat and in total, at the brief's `words_per_minute` (150 where it sets none; `--wpm` overrides both); reads each beat's `(target MM:SS)` as that beat's own duration, compares every beat with its own target and sums them; compares the total with the brief's `target_seconds` (±10%) and each deliverable's `max_seconds`; `--write` records `words` and `estimated_seconds` in the script's frontmatter (rulings T1, T2) | 0 · 1 · 2 |
| `assemble` | `EDL [-o OUT] [--memory-max SIZE]` | builds the master from `production/src/edits/<piece>.toml` (Section 6.4): video and audio clips cut frame-accurately (re-encode, never `-c copy`), stills and cards held for `seconds` with optional push-in, colour clips, fades; each still, card and colour clip framed once at the master's size, and runs of them with no push-in or fade between them read through one concat-demuxer input, one image at a time, rather than one decoder each; card PNGs rendered first where missing or stale (D47); overlays; the voice track from the segment register, joined as `voice join` joins it; `[[audio]]` ranges, fades and ducking under voice; loudness to the edit's target, then a picture master's sound held to its picture's length, sample for sample; ffprobe-verified, each stream's duration on its own; run under the memory cap where the author sets one (D72) | 0 · 1 · 2 |
| `cut` | `SRC --deliverable KEY --in TC --out TC [--cut cNN] [--frame crop\|pad] [--x PX] [--captions SRT] [--overlay PNG] [-o OUT]` | trims, reframes and encodes one deliverable in one pass (`-ss`/`-to` before `-i`; `-copyts` when burning captions); verifies size, codecs, duration ≤ `max_seconds`, moov first; a deliverable whose table has no `width` and `height` (an audio deliverable) is trimmed and encoded as audio only, so an audiobook's retail sample is `cut <piece>.chNN.mp3 --deliverable audiobook.acx --in … --out …`, within the table's `sample_max_seconds` (ruling T6). A table with `audio_tracks = 0` (`website.hero_loop`) gets no sound track, and its verification fails a file with one. **A table with `kind = "image"` and `formats = ["gif"]` (`newsletter.preview_gif`) is cut to an animated GIF** (D57; run by `thumbnail-brief` towards M7, never at M5): reframed, captions burned only where `--captions` is given, then ffmpeg `palettegen` and `paletteuse` at `colours_max` and at most `fps_max`; it plays `floor(max_seconds ÷ clip seconds)` times, written as `-loop` (plays − 1), or `-1` for one play; over `max_size` it is made again with `fps=fps/2` (every second frame kept, the duration unchanged), then a quarter, and otherwise exits 1 naming its size; a range longer than `max_seconds` renders nothing (exit 1); no audio stream. `--overlay PNG` (new) lays a transparent PNG of exactly the deliverable's size (any other size is exit 2), rendered with `card.py render --transparent`, over every frame of a video or GIF cut | 0 · 1 · 2 |
| `encode` | `SRC --deliverable KEY [--frame crop\|pad] [-o OUT]` | whole-file encode to a video or audio deliverable, loudness to its target (video, own sites included, the house target, D62; `podcast.feed_audio` the podcast target, measured again after the encode); an audio deliverable from a picture master takes its sound only (a talk as an episode, D58); `audio_tracks = 0` writes no sound track. `podcast.feed_audio` is encoded like any audio deliverable, untagged and needing no register: its tags are `feed tag`'s, at M7 (D59). After the loudness pass a video deliverable's sound is held to its source's picture, sample for sample, and padded with silence where it stops short; where the deliverable keeps its sound, a source whose sound runs more than a frame and 0.1 s past its picture is refused (exit 1, nothing rendered), naming `cut` and `still-video`. Each stream's duration is verified on its own | 0 · 1 · 2 |
