**`card.py`** (`uv run toolkit/card.py …`): `render HTML (--deliverable KEY | --size WxH) [--transparent] [-o OUT]` — Chromium through Playwright 1.63.0, viewport = size, device scale 1, the safe-zone variables set on `:root`, every http(s) request aborted, `document.fonts.ready` awaited, then a screenshot (`--transparent` keeps an overlay card's alpha; with `--deliverable`, `:root` carries `data-deliverable="<platform>.<format>"` and `data-platform="<platform>"`, so one layout can vary by deliverable, D57); `check HTML` — tokens linked, every required token resolves, the brand fonts loaded, line 1 `@dsCard` for files under `previews/`; `--self-test`. Exit 0 · 1 · 2; a missing browser is exit 2 with `uv run --with playwright==1.63.0 playwright install chromium`. `assemble` starts it with D20's interpreter.

**`transcribe.py`** (started only by `python3 toolkit/media.py transcribe`): PEP 723, `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.14"`, `dependencies = ["whisperx==3.8.6"]`; run by the interpreter path `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` names, which `media.py` runs directly, never an environment the author activates where Claude Code or `media.py` runs (whose `python3` the hooks and the family scripts need at 3.14 or later), or else by `uv run --offline` with D20's interpreter (without `--offline` only under `fetch`), never with a user-scope `UV_PYTHON` outside the script's `requires-python`, because D20 gives `UV_PYTHON` precedence over it and `uv run --quiet` hides the mismatch: it takes D20's `uv python find --system` within the range instead, and where none qualifies exits 2 naming `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` (D67, D81). Before any import it sets `PYANNOTE_METRICS_ENABLED=0` and, but for `fetch`, `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`; it imports torch first, refuses any WhisperX but 3.8.6 and any missing fetched file (exit 2), then aligns as D67 says. Its `--self-test` is standard-library only — the aligner-text rules, the word-sequence comparison, the words file's shape — and imports WhisperX for nothing, so `python3 toolkit/transcribe.py --self-test` runs anywhere.

**`scene.py`** (`uv run toolkit/scene.py …`, D71): `stills PIECE [--size WxH …]` — runs `production/src/scenes/<piece>.scene.py` by its path (never imported, so nothing lands in `scenes/`) to write `production/src/renders/<piece>/scene/index.html`, then, in one Chromium with the frame and safe-zone variables set before the first draw, captures each board's first and middle frame at every size (the edit decision list's `size` by default) to `production/src/renders/<piece>/stills/` with `<piece>.stills.json` (every text and sprite box; overlapping text, a box outside the safe zone, overflowing text and a position off the design-pixel grid are findings); `render PIECE [--size WxH] [--memory-max SIZE] [-o OUT]` — writes the page afresh, steps its clock frame by frame and pipes every frame to ffmpeg with the edit decision list's `[[audio]]` mix (the joined voice among it) at the target its `loudness` key selects, then probes the master: its frame count must be the scene's and its sound as long as its picture; by default `production/src/renders/<piece>/<piece>.master.mp4` at `[edit]`'s `size`, or `<piece>.master.<W>x<H>.mp4` at another `--size`, rendered natively; an edit decision list with `[[clip]]` or `[[overlay]]` rows is exit 2; `--self-test`. Exit 0 · 1 · 2; a missing browser is exit 2 with `card.py`'s install line, a missing sprite frame or timing file is exit 2 naming it, and neither command calls ElevenLabs or the network; an ignored file is opened only by a path it wrote, the joined voice's one path (the `vo:<piece>` row) or the manifest path of a footage image `<piece>.real.json` indexes (D50, D70), and `stills` prints every finding and the path of every still it wrote.

**`data/platforms.toml`**: schema in Section 6.8; template-owned, so `copier update` refreshes it; the brand's corrections are overrides (D26).
**`templates/`**: `thumbnail.html` (one layout adapting by aspect ratio to every thumbnail and cover deliverable) and `card.html` (title and end cards), both meeting the contract of Section 6.11. They are the fallbacks for the brand's own components (D18): a piece copies `brand/src/design-system/previews/thumbnail.html` (or this file where the brand's is absent) to `publishing/src/thumbnails/<piece>[--cNN].html`, and the brand's `card.html` (or this one) to `production/src/cards/<piece>.<card>.html`, fixing the relative tokens path.

### 4.3 Production layers

Every production layer: `<layer>/{CONTEXT.md, CLAUDE.md}`, `docs/{pair}`, `docs/reference/{pair, guides…}`, `docs/project/{pair}`, `src/{pair, …}`, `workflows/{pair}` (the "You want to… | Procedure" table in `workflows/CLAUDE.md`), `workflows/local/{pair}`, and `workflows/NN-name/{CONTEXT.md, CLAUDE.md, STEPS.md, CHECKLIST.md}` (syntek-author Section 4.4, verbatim). Guides follow syntek-author's format (54–82 lines; `type: guide`, `skills: […]`, `model: opus`; `**What it is.**`; two to five topic H2s; `## How we apply it here`; `## Who implements it`; `## Governing standard` last, naming the media rules file and section that owns the rule — syntek-author's own pronunciation guide names a rules file the same way). STEPS steps open ``> **Skill:** `x` · **Guide:** `path` `` (a companion unbackticked: `> **Skill:** fact-check (syntek-author), where present · …`; a family-shared skill backticked, ``> **Skill:** `grill-with-docs`, where present · …``, D10, D80) and end `_Substantive._` or `_Mechanical._`; CHECKLIST items end ` · _opus_` or ` · _sonnet_`; a `skills:` list names media skills only, never a companion or a family-shared skill, and on a shared file only skills that ship whenever it does. Phases: `plan`, `research`, `produce`, `review`, `publish`, `convert`, `author`.
**The gate-less See line** (D17) is used by `brand/workflows/01-` to `06-`, `production/workflows/01-log-source-media/`, `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/`, `publishing/workflows/01-plan-the-cut-downs/`, `publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/`, `publishing/workflows/07-refresh-the-platform-specs/` and every procedure under `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/` (Section 4.4, D84); every other CHECKLIST cites the gate it serves.

#### `brand/` (always)

```text
brand/src/
├── CONTEXT.md · CLAUDE.md          ← template-owned; index (gated platform rows)
├── design-register.md              ← seed: one row per design (Section 6.13)
├── design-system/                  ← template-owned pair
│   ├── tokens.css                  ← seed: the custom properties every layout imports (Section 6.11)
│   ├── fonts/                      ← pair-only: the brand's font files; licences in the rights register
│   └── previews/                   ← template-owned pair; seeds colors · type · spacing · brand · captions · thumbnail · card .html
├── exports/                        ← pair-only: small exported design assets, committed
│   └── large/                      ← pair-only + .gitattributes (template-owned): Git LFS
├── voice/                          ← pair-only + voice.md (seed): the spoken voice, narrators, pronunciations
└── platforms/                      ← pair-only, index pair + <platform>.md seed per selected platform + overrides.toml (seed)
```

| # | Workflow | Gate | Phase | Skill(s) | Moves the piece |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | `01-set-up-the-brand-kit` | always | author | — (opens with `grill-with-docs`, where present; `media.py tokens`, `card.py check`) | — |
| 02 | `02-sync-with-claude-design` | always | author | — (`/design-sync`, started by the author; fallback `/design import\|export`) | — |
| 03 | `03-record-a-design-export` | always | author | — (a revision is a new `-vN` file, and once the author approves it the references they agree move to it, D75; a podcast show's cover is one: encoded with `media.py image` to `podcast.cover` and `podcast.id3_cover`, D58; so is an image a partner site wants in its own branding, D61) | — |
| 04 | `04-set-up-a-platform` | always | author | — (cites the social media plan of syntek-author, where present, for each platform it covers: D48, D56; a website's or blog's Sites and a newsletter's Lists, with their agreements: D61) | — |
| 05 | `05-write-the-spoken-voice` | always | author | — (opens with `grill-with-docs`, where present) | — |
| 06 | `06-check-the-setup` | always | review | — (`media.py check --setup`, D49) | — |

#### `scripts/` (always)

```text
scripts/src/
├── CONTEXT.md · CLAUDE.md          ← template-owned: the piece and its files
├── piece-register.md               ← seed: numbers, kinds, origins, parents, retirements (no status: that is the brief's)
└── pieces/                         ← pair-only; its CLAUDE.md fences the skeleton of a piece's pair
    └── NNN-kebab-title/            ← one piece, with its own pair: brief.md · script.md or transcript.md · storyboard.md · shot-list.md
```

| # | Workflow | Gate | Phase | Skill(s) | Moves the piece |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | `01-brief-a-piece` | always | plan | — (`grill-with-docs`, where present; opens the piece folder and its pair) | idea → briefed (M1) |
| 02 | `02-write-a-script` | always | produce | `write-script` | briefed → scripted (M2, scripted pieces; with `M3: n/a — no picture` for a piece with `picture: false`; never an audiobook) |
| 03 | `03-storyboard-a-piece` | always | plan | `storyboard` | scripted → storyboarded (M3) |

#### `production/` (always)

```text
production/src/
├── CONTEXT.md · CLAUDE.md          ← template-owned; index (gated rows)
├── .gitignore                      ← template-owned: renders/, footage/raw/, voiceover/generated/, audiobook/{generated,renders}/
├── rights-register.md              ← seed
├── credits-log.md                  ← seed
├── renders/                        ← README.md only, tracked; everything else git-ignored, with no pair (D42)
│   ├── <piece>/                    ← one per piece (D64): the master (one per aspect, D71), <piece>.voice.wav, extracts; cards/ (card PNGs); timing/ (working copies of the timing and scene files, D66); scene/ (a scene's page) and stills/ (D70, D71)
│   └── proofs/<what>-DD-MM-YYYY/   ← brand-kit proofs, which no piece owns
├── footage/                        ← pair-only: every source file — camera, recorded audio, music beds, stock, archived takes — as manifest.toml (seed) rows; raw/README.md (the ignored local mirror)
├── assets/                         ← pair-only: small committed stills, logos, graphics and text captures (.txt, .ansi, .html; never .log or .out; home paths and host names removed), under 10 MB each (D69)
├── edits/                          ← pair-only: <piece>.toml edit decision lists
├── cards/                          ← pair-only: <piece>.<card>.html title and end cards (D47)
├── voiceover/                      ← pair-only: <piece>.toml segment registers + generated/README.md
│   └── generated/                  ← git-ignored: <piece>/takes/ (takes, D21) and <piece>/ (scratch tracks); voice-trials/<name>/ (no piece's; a remix's <name>.pNN.mp3 previews and <name>.remix.json, D82)
├── timing/                         ← pair-only (D64): <piece>.words.json · .words-check.md · .mouth.json, tool-written, reached only through -o (D66)
├── scenes/                         ← pair-only (D64): <piece>.cues.json · .real.json (tool-written, reached only through -o, D66) · <piece>.scene.py (the piece's scene as code, run by path and never imported, D70)
└── audiobook/                      ← 'audiobook' in MEDIA_KINDS: pair-only: <piece>.md chapter registers + generated/ + renders/ READMEs
```

| # | Workflow | Gate | Phase | Skill(s) | Moves the piece |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | `01-log-source-media` | always | produce | — (`media.py footage add\|verify`) | — |
| 02 | `02-make-a-voiceover` | always | produce | `voiceover` | towards M4; a scene piece's `M4.takes` (every take approved and archived, the voice joined) |
| 03 | `03-assemble-the-master` | always | produce | `cut-for-platform` | storyboarded → produced (M4); also `scripted` with M3 n/a (a recorded piece, or an audio master for a piece with `picture: false`); every piece but a scene piece, its `M4.*` sub-checks `n/a` |
| 04 | `04-master-a-podcast-episode` | `'podcast' in MEDIA_KINDS` | produce | `cut-for-platform` | → produced (M4; M3 n/a) |
| 05 | `05-narrate-an-audiobook` | `'audiobook' in MEDIA_KINDS` | produce | `narrate-audiobook` | briefed → scripted (M2, audiobook: its chapter register approved; M3 n/a), then towards M4 |
| 06 | `06-master-an-audiobook` | `'audiobook' in MEDIA_KINDS` | produce | `narrate-audiobook` | → produced (M4) → cut (M5; M6 n/a) |
| 07 | `07-clear-the-rights` | always | review | — | the rights part of M7 |
| 08 | `08-bring-in-a-recording` | always | produce | `captions` | briefed → scripted (M2, recorded pieces; M3 n/a) |
| 09 | `09-time-the-voice` | always | produce | `voiceover`, `captions`, `storyboard` | towards M4, a scene piece: `M4.words` (`transcribe` and the words check; `lipsync` beside it on the same joined voice, its mouths checked at `M4.stills`), then `M4.cues` (`cues`, and the storyboard re-timed from the words) |
| 10 | `10-animate-a-scene` | always | produce | `cut-for-platform` | storyboarded → produced, a scene piece: `M4.stills` (the scene file, `real`, `stills` with the mouths `lipsync` made, and a preview render, its timing notes going back through `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`), then M4 (`scene.py render`, natively per aspect) |
| 11 | `11-design-character-voices` | always | produce | `voiceover` | — (a character's voice, designed from the author's own clone through `voice remix plan\|run`, saved through MCP and recorded as a `character:<name>` row in `voice.md`, D82; a piece's takes in that voice are `production/workflows/02-make-a-voiceover/`'s) |

#### `publishing/` (always)

```text
publishing/src/
├── CONTEXT.md · CLAUDE.md          ← template-owned
├── .gitignore                      ← template-owned: renders/
├── schedule.md                     ← seed
├── publish-log.md                  ← seed
├── renders/                        ← README.md only, tracked; everything else git-ignored, with no pair (D42); at its top only what no piece owns: a feed's upload copy, a show's cover encodes
│   └── <piece>/                    ← one per piece (D64): deliverables, burned versions, thumbnail and image renders, GIFs, chapters JSON, a feed episode's audio
├── cut-downs/                      ← pair-only: <piece>.md cut-down plans
├── captions/                       ← pair-only: <piece>[--cNN|.<FID>][.<platform>-<format>].en-GB.srt and .vtt; <piece>[--cNN].transcript.en-GB.md published transcripts (D60)
├── thumbnails/                     ← pair-only: <piece>[--cNN].md brief + .html layout; flat option and score JSON (D74)
├── posts/                          ← pair-only: <piece>.md post package, one section per deliverable or placement
└── podcast/                        ← 'podcast' in PLATFORMS: pair-only: <show>.toml show registers + <show>.feed.xml, the feed as last published (D59)
```

| # | Workflow | Gate | Phase | Skill(s) | Moves the piece |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | `01-plan-the-cut-downs` | always | convert | `repurpose` | — |
| 02 | `02-cut-for-a-platform` | always | convert | `cut-for-platform` | produced → cut (M5; a feed episode's `podcast.feed_audio` encoded untagged, D59; never a GIF preview) |
| 03 | `03-caption-a-piece` | always | publish | `captions` | cut → captioned (M6; WebVTT and published transcripts for profile sites and feed episodes, D60) |
| 04 | `04-brief-a-thumbnail` | always | publish | `thumbnail-brief` | towards M7 (every image deliverable, the GIF preview with its overlay included, D57) |
| 05 | `05-prepare-a-post` | always | publish | `prepare-post` | captioned → scheduled (M7) |
| 06 | `06-record-a-publication` | always | publish | `prepare-post` | scheduled → published (for a feed episode also the register row `published` and the tracked feed, D59) |
| 07 | `07-refresh-the-platform-specs` | always | review | — (`media.py presets --stale-after 183`) | — |
| 08 | `08-publish-the-podcast-feed` | `'podcast' in PLATFORMS` | publish | `prepare-post`, `cut-for-platform` | a feed episode's part of M7 only (`M7 (captioned → scheduled)`; D58, D59): the show opened once, the episode's row, words and chapters, `feed tag`, `feed chapters`, `feed check`, the upload copy and the author's upload list; the encode is M5's and the transcript M6's |

| `publishing/…/podcast-feed.md` (D58, D59: the feed and its register, the two routes to an episode and the hosted-show route, hosting and the author's `curl` tests, the order and time of upload, the hand steps per episode, a corrected file, the GUID comparison, YouTube's two routes, the validators, one worked placement of an episode page on a second site; keys, never numbers) | `'podcast' in PLATFORMS` | `[prepare-post, cut-for-platform]` | publishing 08, 02, 03, 05, 06 | the podcast-episode workflow and guide (`'podcast' in MEDIA_KINDS`), the YouTube guide, the website profile and guide |

"production 02" in this table stands for the full folder name `production/workflows/02-make-a-voiceover/`, and "production 11" for `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/`, which is how a guide writes them (D32).

### 4.4 `how-to/` (always; D84)

The user guide: a shared reference folder, not a production layer, read and never produced in, and read-only in a project (D8, D84). It ships in every render, with no seed, no `docs/project/` and no `workflows/local/`; each media file in it is shared (Section 2), and its chapters hold no delimiter at all (Section 6.18). The files marked SHARED, with the guide guard `.claude/hooks/how-to-read-only.sh` (Section 4.1), are the family's fourteen: syntek-author's bytes at its `v0.7.0` tag (D81), each copy-only shared file by file (Section 3.6). Everything under a `syntek-media/` folder is media's own and template-owned; media ships no other path here, and syntek-author none under a `syntek-media/` folder (syntek-author D56). Every update that runs the family's update script in full puts each file the new render holds here back where it differs, another template's version of a shared one excepted (D84).

```text
how-to/                       ← SHARED pair
├── docs/                     ← SHARED pair
│   └── syntek-media/         ← pair + media's guides, kebab-title.md (Section 4.3's guide format, skills: [])
├── src/                      ← SHARED pair
│   └── TEMPLATE-GUIDE/       ← SHARED pair + agent-clients-and-mcp.md · the-data-root.md · updating-and-recovery.md (SHARED)
│       └── syntek-media/     ← pair (its reading order) + media's chapters, NN-kebab-title.md from 01-overview.md (Section 6.18)
└── workflows/                ← SHARED pair
    └── syntek-media/         ← pair (each procedure and its job) + media's procedures, NN-verb-first-name/ from 01
```

**Procedures** have Section 4.3's four files, with `skills: []` and the gate-less See line (D17), and are numbered from 01 in `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/`, frozen and append-only (D32). `run-media-workflow` never routes them (D9; **overrides syntek-author D56**, whose router reads its own template's procedures after the production layers): a session runs one as its folder says, step by step with its checklist open, stopping for the owner at every step that changes the template's files or a settings file (rules 01 Section 3). Each carries every command it needs, so it works where a shared chapter is missing. Among them are one each for updating, for recovering from an update that stopped and for enabling a coding-agent client, each fencing media's exact command (`-a .copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`, Copier run as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier`, with Git's housekeeping off), because the shared chapters route each of these jobs to the template's own procedure.
**Guides** sit in `how-to/docs/syntek-media/` as `kebab-title.md`, in Section 4.3's guide format with `skills: []`, each naming the procedure that carries out its practice.
**Chapters** follow Section 6.18. `01-overview.md`, where the guide starts, ships in every brand kind, because `_message_after_copy` names it (D13); and one chapter says that `toolkit/transcribe.py` needs a Python from 3.10 to 3.13, kept beside 3.14, and names `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON` (D81).

| # | Chapter (`how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/`) | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| 01 | `01-overview.md` | what media adds; the three brand kinds; the piece and its ladder; a toolkit, not an editor; never spending unasked; a companion, not a dependency |
| 02 | `02-prerequisites.md` | the tools and accounts; Python 3.14, and the older Python `transcribe` needs beside it (`MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON`); `check --setup`; clients and MCP routed to the shared chapter |
| 03 | `03-applying-over-syntek-author.md` | a clean branch, never `--overwrite`; syntek-author v0.6.0 or later, updated first; the expected 'conflict then skip' and 'identical' lines; a collision as a template bug; the `.agents` cases routed to the shared chapter |
| 04 | `04-a-standalone-project.md` | a copy into an empty folder; the shared files then media's; companions absent |
| 05 | `05-the-answers.md` | each media question; list answers; `BRAND_KIND` never changes; the family's questions routed to the shared chapters |
| 06 | `06-what-copier-writes.md` | the five ownership classes and what a copy and an update do to each: seeds, the copy-only and family-shared files, the entries and servers added once, the guide put back; the update itself routed to the shared chapter and `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/04-update-the-template/` |
| 07 | `07-a-tour-of-the-project.md` | the layers, the piece folder, the ignored output folders and `how-to/` itself; one line that media files nothing under a data root, whatever `DATA_STORE` records, since its map claims no folder (D80), which answers the route of `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/the-data-root.md` to the template's own procedure |

| 09 | `09-your-first-piece.md` | M1–M7 by name, each step a workflow by full folder name |

Beyond Section 6.18, a chapter has no frontmatter; its title line, `# Title — gloss`, is followed by a lede of one to three sentences and two to eight topic H2s; 'Where the rules live' names rules sections, guides and procedures by full path, and 'Next' gives 'reason → path' bullets. It carries no `VERIFY` or `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM` flag, no count of skills, workflows, questions or platforms, no platform number (D26) and no GitHub address: it cites `_src_path` in `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml` instead. The subjects of the three shared chapters are routed to them by full path, never restated. None of the files in these tables is gated, so no pair under `how-to/` is an index file (Section 3.5).

---

## 5. Skills

Conformance (syntek-author Section 5, adapted): frontmatter `name` equals the folder; only the keys `name`, `description`, `context`, `agent`, `background`, `model`, `metadata`; `description` (`>-`) ≤ 1,024 characters (aim ≤ 950 in `template/`), naming the job, its triggers in the author's quoted words and at least one ``Not … (`media-skill`)`` boundary, with companions named in prose ("Not proofreading (the spelling and grammar skills of syntek-author, where present)"); H1 `# Skill: <Name> (<%BRAND_NAME%>)`; locale line `Locale: en_GB · <%TIMEZONE%> · dates DD/MM/YYYY.`; `## Governing procedures (route here — do not restate at length)` with the local-override bullet ending ``(`run-media-workflow`, step 2)``; steps `N. **Title.** … *Complete when:* …`; `## Anti-patterns`; `## Cross-references` last; ≤ 300 lines with the longest mode file. Skills cite workflows by full folder name (D32). The seven family-shared skills of Section 5.3 are syntek-author's bytes and keep its forms where they differ: no token in the H1 (`# Skill: Grilling`) or the locale line (`Locale: en_GB · dates DD/MM/YYYY.`), and the family's neutral Mode paragraph (D80).
Every moded `SKILL.md` carries this paragraph verbatim before step 1:

> **Mode.** Before step 1, read the brand-kind mode file beside this one — exactly one of `BUSINESS.md`, `FICTION.md`, `NONFICTION.md` ships in this folder. The mode owns the domain (paths, unit, extra reads, domain rules, examples); this file owns the procedure. Where they disagree, the procedure wins and the disagreement is reported to the author.

Mode files: H1 `# BUSINESS.md — <skill>, business mode` (`FICTION.md — …, fiction mode`; `NONFICTION.md — …, non-fiction mode`); the four H2s and no other; no header, no frontmatter; invented examples only. Beside a family-shared `SKILL.md` the H1 reads `# MEDIA-BUSINESS.md — <skill>, media business mode` (`MEDIA-FICTION.md — …, media fiction mode`; `MEDIA-NONFICTION.md — …, media non-fiction mode`), with the same rules; its `## Additions to the steps` keys to the shared skill's own step numbers, and every section routes only (D7).
A skill that names a credit-spending ElevenLabs call (an `mcp__elevenlabs__` ask of D13, or the words `voice remix run`, D82) carries syntek-author's sentence verbatim: "If nothing was asked, stop: never generate unasked, including as a 'helpful' extra after another skill." One that names an `mcp__elevenlabs__` tool also loads deferred tools with ToolSearch (searching 'elevenlabs') and passes `output_directory` as an absolute path resolved with `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`; the remix run writes its own folder (D82) and needs neither. The unit in a mode file's `## Paths and unit` is one piece.

### 5.1 Media skills

| Skill | Gate | Moded | Job | Writes | Reports or edits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `run-media-workflow` | always | no | The router (D9): restate the request as one job; check syntek-author's `00-project.md` Workflow aliases where present, then the four layers' indexes, `workflows/local/` first, listing a layer's workflow folders directly when its index has no table; resolve to one procedure; resolve "next" from the briefs' `status` and `verified`, the newest handoff where the project keeps handoffs, and `MEMORY.md` Status (under the heading syntek-author's Memory headings map gives, where present); load the procedure whole; obey M-gates; work in order; hand back pointing at the next deliverable. Bias: **scaffolding is not progress** — a finished, approved deliverable brings a piece nearer. Steps 1–8 as syntek-author's `run-workflow`, but for its reading of a template's procedures under `how-to/workflows/`: media's are never routed (D9 unchanged, D84; **overrides syntek-author D56**, whose router reads its own template's procedures after the production layers, because D9 scopes media's router to media work). **Under `storyboarded`, a scene piece** (a shot typed `scene`, Section 6.3) is routed on its first undated `M4.*` sub-check (Section 6.1), whose dates it reads as progress, never as a date with no matching status: `M4.takes` to `production/workflows/02-make-a-voiceover/`, `M4.words` and `M4.cues` to `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`, `M4.stills` and then M4 to `production/workflows/10-animate-a-scene/`; every other piece goes on as before, to `production/workflows/03-assemble-the-master/`. Its scope includes media for the brand's own sites and newsletters and a podcast's feed (D53, D58). Boundaries: writing work, a blog post's or a newsletter's words included (the writing router of syntek-author, where present, D55); a social media plan, calendar or bios (the social-media-documents skill (syntek-author), where present). Its description names the new scope and boundary within 950 characters (Section 5). | — | — |
| `write-script` | always | yes | Brief → spoken script for one scripted piece (hook, beats, call to action) in Section 6.2's format; times it with `media.py script time`; adapts from written work (syntek-author's content layer, where present: read, never edited); revises on the author's notes; runs the companions as reports where present; records approval (M2), and `M3: n/a — no picture` beside it for a piece with `picture: false` (build ruling C2). Not a recorded piece's transcript (`captions`); never an audiobook, which has no script (D23). | `script.md`, the brief's `status`, `verified` and `last_updated` | edits (new file), then agreed revisions |
| `storyboard` | always | yes | Script → storyboard and shot list (Section 6.3): a picture for every spoken line, framing for every vertical deliverable, on-screen text, sound, a source for every shot (stills and cards included, D47); opens a `needed` rights row for every licensed or identifiable item (M3); types a code-animated scene's boards `scene`, framed `native` where an aspect is rendered from the scene, and records the four `M4.*` sub-checks `n/a` beside M3 for any other piece. For a scene piece, once its voice is timed, runs `media.py cues` (a working copy first, `<piece>.cues.json` through `-o` once the author accepts it, D66) and re-times the Time column and the shot list's Seconds from it and writes the author's Timing notes (`M4.cues`, `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`): a re-time keeps `approved:` and M3, while a note that replaces a shot or changes the words is a revision that clears them (Section 6.3). Refuses a recorded piece (M3 `n/a — recorded`) and a piece with `picture: false` (M3 `n/a — no picture`, recorded with its M2). | `storyboard.md`, `shot-list.md`, rights rows, `production/src/scenes/<piece>.cues.json` (through `-o`, D66), the brief's `status`, `verified`, `rights` and `last_updated` | edits |
| `repurpose` | always | yes | One long piece → a cut-down plan per selected platform (Section 6.10): the lines each cut carries, its In and Out on the master (for a recorded piece, from the master-timed captions of its transcript), hook in the first seconds, framing, caption width; refuses near-identical batches; hands to `cut-for-platform`. A silent loop (`website.hero_loop`) is a cut: a moment within its `max_seconds` with no words, so the whole-sentence and near-identical rules do not bind it (its Lines `—`); a GIF preview is not a cut but the thumbnail brief's (D57). Not a social media plan or content calendar (the social-media-documents skill (syntek-author), where present). | `publishing/src/cut-downs/<piece>.md` | proposal, then the agreed plan |
| `thumbnail-brief` | always | yes | Scores proposed title–thumbnail pairs through D74 before the author chooses; retains input/results beside the brief, and visual review after rendering.  Brief and HTML layout for a piece's thumbnails and covers, one per cut where cuts need their own (`--cNN`), copied from the brand's `previews/thumbnail.html` (`toolkit/templates/thumbnail.html` where absent); renders each thumbnail deliverable with `card.py`, at the video deliverable's size, flagged `VERIFY`, on a platform with no thumbnail table; checks legibility at small size, the safe zone and the grid crop of `platform.instagram.grid_aspect`. For a scene piece its picture may be a frame of the scene at a moment the author chooses, taken from the master into `production/src/assets/` with `media.py frame -o` like any other frame and recorded in `## Image` (Section 6.11). **Every image deliverable of a piece is this skill's** (D57): thumbnails and covers, a podcast episode's art, a website's posters and share images, a blog's featured and share images, a newsletter's preview image and its **GIF preview**, whose play button the layout shows for `data-deliverable` (an element carrying `data-play-button`: a solid disc with its own outline, so it survives an email client's dark mode). A poster is `media.py image` of its video's render at `--at`, the timecode recorded in the brief's `## Image`; every other format of a rendered PNG (JPEG, WebP, AVIF) is encoded with `media.py image`, never with ffmpeg by hand; the GIF is `card.py render --transparent` of the layout for its overlay, then `media.py cut … --overlay`, its In and Out on the master recorded in `## Image`. For a `newsletter.*` key it checks the piece's copied layout for the play-button hook and, in a 0.1.0 brand layout that lacks it, adds the element and its rules from `toolkit/templates/thumbnail.html` to that copy and tells the author (D63). A show's cover is a design export, never a piece's brief (D58). | `publishing/src/thumbnails/<piece>[--cNN].{md,html}`; PNGs, GIFs and their encodes in `renders/` | proposal, then agreed edits |
| `prepare-post` | always | yes | Scores proposed titles with the approved thumbnail concept through D74; changes to an approved image return to the thumbnail workflow. The post package per deliverable (Section 6.12): title, description, hashtags inside `platforms.toml` limits (warn above `platform.instagram.hashtags_max`), the disclosure plan (D25), a video's chapters from its beat starts where the platform takes them (Section 6.12), captions file, thumbnail, rights check; the schedule row, citing the content calendar entry where syntek-author's is present (D48) (M7). Never posts. When the author posts, gives each description in chat as paste-ready text (Section 6.12); afterwards writes the publish-log row with the URL the author gives. **Own-channel placements** (D61): one section per placement, `<platform>:<slug>`, naming the written piece it sits in (read, never edited, its status read at M7), with only the words media owns (D55): an embed or figure title, alt text, Links to, the structured-data values, the agreement for a site or list the brand does not own; its date taken from the written piece; an embed or player snippet only in chat, on request. **A podcast feed episode** (D59): its title, description and chapters are written in its show register, which the package cites; `feed tag`, `feed chapters` and `feed check` are its M7, through the podcast-feed workflow (named in prose in this always-shipping skill); on the author's report, `publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/` sets its row `published` and writes the tracked feed. Not text-only posts or bios (the social-media-documents skill (syntek-author), where present). Not the blog post or the newsletter itself, nor its standfirst, blurbs or link text (the writing router or the social-media-documents skill of syntek-author, where present, D55). Its description names placements, the feed and the written-side boundary within 950 characters (Section 5); the own-channel and feed detail lives in the gated guides, not the skill. | `publishing/src/posts/<piece>.md`, `schedule.md`, `publish-log.md`; a show register's episode words and status; the tracked feed, through `feed write -o` | proposal, then agreed edits |
| `voiceover` | always | yes | ElevenLabs text-to-speech, one segment per spoken sentence or beat (D21, D22, D51): confirm the request; check or choose the narrator with the author (record in `voice.md`); build request text with `media.py speak plan <piece>` (offline: segments, request text, characters and calls, never credits; pronunciations substituted, directions as audio tags where supported), which creates `generated/<piece>/takes/` before the first call; state the cost and wait; generate one call at a time into that folder; `take add` (a re-roll is the next take number, and resets its segment's `status` to `generated` and `archived` to empty); listen and log; on approval offer to archive the take (D52); for a scene piece, once every segment is approved and archived, `media.py voice join <piece>` writes the joined voice and the procedure dates `M4.takes`, which any later take clears with every sub-check after it and M4 (Section 6.1); then, in `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`, run `lipsync` on the joined voice beside `transcribe`, a working copy first and `<piece>.mouth.json` through `-o` once the author accepts the run, its mouths checked at `M4.stills` (D66, D68); espeak-ng scratch fallback. Through `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/` (D82), a character's voice: drafts its description from the manuscript or script with the author, remixes the author's own cleared clone (`voice remix plan`, its call and characters stated, then `voice remix run --approve-call` once the author says yes), has the author listen and choose, saves the chosen preview with `mcp__elevenlabs__create_voice_from_preview`, records the `character:<name>` row whose consent is the source clone's, and may try the saved voice on a few lines as a voice trial, never a take. | `production/src/voiceover/<piece>.toml`; audio in `generated/<piece>/takes/`; the joined voice in `production/src/renders/<piece>/`; `voice.md` the first time, and a `character:<name>` row per saved character voice; credits-log rows (through `take add`, and `voice remix run` for a remix); `production/src/timing/<piece>.mouth.json`, through `-o` | edits |
| `narrate-audiobook` | `'audiobook' in MEDIA_KINDS` | yes | Chapter by chapter from the promoted manuscript (syntek-author, where present, `final` units only unless the author says otherwise) or a provided text; self-contained: carries the call discipline of Section 5 itself. Plans the book (D23): the chapter register with its credit rows `ch00` and `ch99` and the credits' words, approved as the piece's M2 (with `M3: n/a — no picture`); choose the route per channel (D23) with the author, saying plainly that ACX needs a human narrator and that INaudio's AI route is ElevenLabs' own package, made outside this template; AI route: `audiobook text` → chunks and their pause sidecar → one call at a time → `take add`; human route: recording guidance and `footage add --kind audio`; master (pauses from the sidecar) and check with the toolkit; archive approved masters (D52; chunk takes only on the author's word); disclosure. | `production/src/audiobook/<piece>.md`, `<piece>.ch00.md` and `<piece>.ch99.md`; the brief's `status`, `verified` and `last_updated`; `voice.md` (the narration row, pronunciations); `generated/` and `renders/` audio; credits-log rows | edits |
| `captions` | always | no | Captions by the four routes of D22 — for a generated voice, route 4: word timing from `media.py transcribe`, which aligns the known script to the joined voice locally (no credits) and writes `<piece>.words.json` and the author's check `<piece>.words-check.md` through `-o` (`M4.words`, `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`), then `captions from-words` — and a recorded piece's transcript (D46, `production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/`); speech-to-text only when asked (credits; the base-path check, and on "outside of allowed directory" the exact re-add command with a base path it computes to contain `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`; `save_transcript_to_file: false` with `return_transcript_to_client_directly: true`, or an absolute `output_directory`); SRT and VTT to Section 6.9; per-cut alignment; `captions check`; burn-in or sidecar per platform (M6); the WebVTT sidecar for every video placed on a profile site, and the published transcript (`captions transcript`, `--lines` for a cut, then described by hand where the picture carries what the words do not) for every piece or cut placed on a profile site and every feed episode (D60). | `transcript.md`; `publishing/src/captions/*` (published transcripts included); `production/src/timing/<piece>.words.json` and `<piece>.words-check.md`, through `-o`; burned renders; credits-log rows | edits |
| `cut-for-platform` | always | no | Every ffmpeg render through the toolkit: writes the edit decision list with the author from the storyboard and shot list, or from a recorded piece's transcript (stills, cards, colour clips, fades and push-ins, D47); copies the brand's card component into `production/src/cards/`; `assemble` → master (M4); podcast masters cut from recordings; `cut` and `encode` per deliverable from the brief and the cut-down plan, burning captions in the same pass when they exist; `still-video`; loudness; verifies every output against its preset (M5); own-site video and the silent loop (D57); `podcast.feed_audio` encoded untagged at M5, from an audio master or, for a talk as an episode, from the picture master's sound (D58, D59), its tags left to `feed tag` at M7; never a GIF preview, which is the thumbnails'. **A scene piece** (Section 6.3): writes `production/src/scenes/<piece>.scene.py` with the author from the re-timed storyboard, `<piece>.cues.json`, `<piece>.mouth.json` and the index of the real sources it shows, which `media.py real` makes (a working copy first, `<piece>.real.json` through `-o` once the author accepts it, D66, D69, D70); `scene.py stills` at every master size, every still opened by the path `stills` prints and reported, its mouths among what the author checks, then a preview render (`scene.py render` at the edit decision list's `size`, the master itself) whose timing notes go on the storyboard and back through `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/` (`M4.stills`); `scene.py render` once per aspect, natively at its size, never cropped, under the mix of the edit decision list's `[[audio]]` rows (Section 6.4) → `<piece>.master.mp4` and each `<piece>.master.<W>x<H>.mp4` (M4, `production/workflows/10-animate-a-scene/`, D71); a `native` cut is taken from the master at its deliverable's aspect. Renders are git-ignored and regenerable. | `production/src/edits/<piece>.toml`; `production/src/cards/*`; `production/src/scenes/<piece>.scene.py`, and `<piece>.real.json` through `-o`; renders | edits |

A skill whose job ends at a gate (`write-script`, `storyboard`, `narrate-audiobook` at M2) records that gate in the brief itself, on the author's word; every other skill reports the gate's evidence and the procedure's step records the date (build reports, 04/10/2026: syntek-author's draft-section and grill-with-docs update their brief the same way).
The 0.2.0 work adds no skill (D57): the three own channels and the feed are worked by these ten, so the dev-isolation deny list is unchanged. The 0.3.0 work adds none either: a scene piece is worked by `voiceover`, `captions`, `storyboard` and `cut-for-platform`, through two new production procedures (Section 4.3), whose steps date the `M4.*` sub-checks as a procedure's step dates a gate. The 0.4.4 work adds none: a character's voice is designed through `voiceover`, in `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/` (D82), so the deny list is unchanged and no skill takes the name `character-voice`, which is syntek-author's (its written dialogue, not audio). The 0.5.0 work adds no media skill either (D80): the seven skills of Section 5.3 are the family's, never in this catalogue, and the development settings deny each of them as they deny these ten (Section 7). The 0.6.0 work adds none (D81, D84): the update script is a migration that never ships, and media's how-to procedures carry `skills: []` and run as their folders say, so the deny list is unchanged. No name here collides with any of syntek-author's 52 skills (Section 9); `skill-conformance.sh` check 20 reads them from `.github/scripts/syntek-author-names.txt`.

### 5.2 Companion skills from syntek-author (never shipped; used where present)

| Companion | Applies to | How media uses it |
|---|---|---|
| spelling, grammar | scripts, transcripts, post copy, captions text | a supportive report; fixes applied only when accepted (a transcript's words are what was said: only a mis-transcription is fixed); part of M2 where present |
| comprehension | scripts | a report read as the `AUDIENCE_TEST` viewer or listener (rules 01 Section 1) |
| flow | scripts | a report on order, rhythm and repetition across beats |
| fact-check | every factual claim in a script, transcript or post | verdicts; an unresolved claim stays `VERIFY`; M2 and M7 need none open (a recorded claim is cut, corrected in a caption or description, or accepted by the author) |
| improve-section | a script the author drafted | report, then the agreed edits only; no ledger entry, no status change, said so before it runs |
| adapt-section | a script revised from the author's notes | report-then-agreed-edit; no ledger entry; `write-script`'s revise step is the fallback |
| research | platform policies, facts behind a script | as practice; sources and dates recorded where the guide says |
| social-media-documents | social media plans, content calendars, bios, channel voice, text-only posts | owns them where present (business, D48); media's profiles, schedule and posts cite them and never duplicate them |
| run-workflow | — | a boundary only: writing work goes there, where present |
| pronounce | — | never run by media; its language's IPA (fiction, where present) is the source for constructed names in `voice.md` |

### 5.3 Family-shared skills (syntek-author D51; vendored, copy-only, D80)

| Skill | Media's mode files | Job (syntek-author's) | How media uses it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `grilling` | `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` | the interview engine: frontier rounds, each question with a recommended answer | loaded by `grill-me`, `grill-with-docs` and `wayfinder`; rules 06 Section 8 |
| `grill-with-docs` | `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` | grilling that records each settled decision in its home | opens the brief (`scripts/workflows/01-brief-a-piece/`), the brand kit and the spoken voice (brand 01, 05) and every design decision; decisions dated in `MEMORY.md` or the brief |
| `grill-me` | none | grilling that leaves no record | as practice, when the author wants the questions alone |
| `handoff` | `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` | a handoff at a session boundary, never a compaction | rules 07, carrying what its Section 3 lists |
| `wayfinder` | `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` | a decision map for a large body of work, worked in batches | a large series or a launch |
| `to-questionnaire` | `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` | a decision only someone else can answer, sent as a questionnaire | a partner, a narrator, a rights holder or a client; the file where rules 02 Section 4 says |
| `wait-what` | none | a re-pitch after a reply that missed | as practice |

Each `SKILL.md` is syntek-author's family-shared file at its `v0.8.0` tag (D81), byte for byte and never edited here, copy-only shared file by file (Section 3.6) and listed in `migrations/family-refresh.json`: every update replaces one the owner never changed and keeps and reports one the owner edited or another template's version (D81). It holds no delimiter, names no path only one template ships and names roles instead (the project's rules, the mode files, a piece of the work, the decision-maps folder, the questionnaires folder), which rules 02 Section 4 gives media's paths. Its Mode paragraph is the family's: it reads the mode files the project's rules route the work at hand to, and every one where they name none. Media's rules route media work to `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` (D7), so syntek-author's mode files beside them never steer media work, nor media's syntek-author's. These skills are not in Section 5.1's catalogue: `_common.sh` lists them in `SM_FAMILY_SHARED`, and the audits treat them as Section 7 says. `handoff` and the two handoff hooks rely on `autoCompactEnabled` and `idleCompaction` both `false` and on the seed's `hooks` key (D13, D35), which the copy and every update offer, once, to a settings file that lacks them (D81).

---

## 6. Formats

### 6.1 Piece brief and the ladder

`scripts/src/pieces/NNN-kebab-title/brief.md`:

```yaml
---
piece: 003-why-the-ferry-runs-late   # equals the folder name
title: "Why the ferry runs late"
kind: short-video          # short-video | long-video | podcast | audiobook | trailer | voiceover
origin: scripted           # scripted | recorded (D46)
picture: true              # false for audio-only pieces: M3 reads n/a
status: idea               # idea · briefed · scripted · storyboarded · produced · cut · captioned · scheduled · published
deliverables: [youtube.short, instagram.reel]   # full-length deliverables only: keys of toolkit/data/platforms.toml
source_media: []           # recorded: the recording's footage IDs, e.g. [F0001]
target_seconds: 45
words_per_minute: 150
parent: ""                 # the piece this was cut or adapted from
source: ""                 # written work it adapts (read, never edited)
synthetic_voice: none      # none | stock | designed | own-clone | other-clone
ai_visuals: none           # none | assisted | generated
music: none                # none | licensed | own | generated
rights: []                 # rights-register IDs, e.g. [RR0003]
verified: {}               # gate or M4 sub-check: DD/MM/YYYY | 'n/a — reason' | 'waived DD/MM/YYYY — reason'
last_updated: DD/MM/YYYY
---
```

H1 `# <Title> — brief` (as the script's is `— script`); body, eleven fixed H2s in this order: `## Purpose` · `## Audience` · `## Shape` (the piece's beats in order and what each does, after its kind's guide where one gives a shape) · `## Metaphor` (the one image or comparison the piece is built on) · `## Hook` · `## Key points` · `## Call to action` · `## Disclosure plan` · `## Rights needs` · `## Draws on` · `## Notes`. Shape and Metaphor are always answered, `n/a` being a valid answer and a blank never; a brief that passed M1 before they existed keeps its date until it is next re-briefed. Its skeleton is fenced in `scripts/src/pieces/CLAUDE.md`.
The brief lists only full-length deliverables; cut-downs live only in the cut-down plan (Section 6.10). An audiobook piece's deliverables are `audiobook.<store>` keys. Own-site video (`website.video`, `website.video_720`, `blog.video`) and a feed episode (`podcast.feed_audio`) are full-length deliverables; posters and share, featured and preview images and the GIF preview are the thumbnail brief's (Section 6.11); a silent loop is a cut (Section 6.10); and an embed of the YouTube upload is a placement of `youtube.long`, never a deliverable of its own (D61). **A feed episode's ladder** (D59): M5 encodes its `podcast.feed_audio` untagged (`publishing/workflows/02-cut-for-a-platform/`); M6 makes its master-timed WebVTT and published transcript (`publishing/workflows/03-caption-a-piece/`); M7 writes its words and chapters in the register, tags the file, checks the feed and writes the upload copy (the podcast-feed workflow, with `publishing/workflows/05-prepare-a-post/` for its other deliverables); the author's report then sets its row `published` and writes the tracked feed (`publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/`). A talk published as an episode is one piece whose deliverables include `youtube.long` and `podcast.feed_audio`; an episode of its own is a `kind: podcast` piece (D58).

| Gate | Moves | Passes when | n/a for |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | idea → briefed | Every frontmatter field set (`source_media: []` counts as set for a recorded piece briefed before `production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/` logs its recording); deliverables are keys of selected platforms, or `audiobook.<store>` keys for an audiobook; purpose, shape, metaphor, call to action, disclosure plan and rights needs written (shape and metaphor may read `n/a`, never blank); no flag in the brief; the author's word. | — |
| M2 | briefed → scripted | **Scripted:** the author approved `script.md` (`approved:` dated); zero flags in it; `script time` within 10% of `target_seconds` and inside every deliverable's `max_seconds`; companion reports run where present and every finding decided; every factual claim verified or cut. **Recorded (D46):** the author approved `transcript.md`; every beat anchored on the recording; zero flags in it; fact-check run where present and every finding decided. **Audiobook** (it has no script, D23): the author approved its chapter register (`approved:` dated), with a row for every chapter and the credit rows `ch00` and `ch99`, the credits' words written; every chapter's source `final` or a text the author provided; zero flags in the register. | — |
| M3 | scripted → storyboarded | Every spoken line sits in a storyboard row; every row has a shot with a source; framing noted for every vertical deliverable; a `needed` rights row for every licensed or identifiable item. | `picture: false`; `origin: recorded` |
| M4 | storyboarded → produced | The master exists and probes clean (for a scene piece, every master, one per aspect, D71); `footage verify` passes for every source it uses; every take it uses was heard, approved and archived (D52; for an audiobook, every mastered chapter, the credits included, approved and archived, its chunk takes optional); loudness within D24; the author has watched or heard it through. **A scene piece** (a shot typed `scene`, Section 6.3) has `M4.takes`, `M4.words`, `M4.cues` and `M4.stills` dated first, in that order (below); every other piece records them `n/a`. | — |
| M5 | produced → cut | Every deliverable of the brief and the cut-down plan is rendered and passes its verification; audiobook: `audiobook check` passes on every file; the author has seen or heard each. A feed episode's `podcast.feed_audio` is encoded here like any audio deliverable, untagged (its tags and chapters are M7's, D59); a GIF preview is made with the thumbnails towards M7, never here (D57). | — |
| M6 | cut → captioned | Every deliverable with speech and picture has captions that pass `captions check --script` (against the script, or a recorded piece's transcript), burned where the platform takes no sidecar or the brief asks, sidecar otherwise. Captions burned in the cutting pass let M5 and M6 pass together; both dates are recorded. **On a profile site, whoever owns it (D53, D60):** every `website.*` or `blog.*` video deliverable with speech has its WebVTT sidecar, never burned; every piece or cut with speech placed there has its published transcript `publishing/src/captions/<piece>[--cNN].transcript.en-GB.md`, one per distinct cut placed, made with `captions transcript` (`--lines` for a cut), described where the picture carries what the words do not, and approved by the author. **A feed episode** (`podcast.feed_audio`) has its published transcript and its master-timed WebVTT, made here (`publishing/workflows/03-caption-a-piece/`); every episode page carries that transcript. | audiobook; a podcast with no `podcast.feed_audio` deliverable and no placement on a profile site, unless the brief asks for a transcript; deliverables without speech (a silent loop, a GIF preview, an image: no table with `kind = "image"` or `audio_tracks = 0` takes captions, D57) |
| M7 | captioned → scheduled | Every rights row the piece uses is `cleared`; the disclosure is set per deliverable (D25); thumbnails approved where the platform takes one; the post package approved and inside every limit; zero flags in the piece's files (`media.py flags --piece`); a schedule row for every deliverable going to a platform and for every placement (D61: one per file a page or issue plays or shows first; its poster, share, featured or fallback image is a bullet of it, never a row; M7 counts placements, never image keys); the piece's images approved with its thumbnails, the GIF preview included. **The brand's own channels (D61):** every placement on a site or list the brand does not own has its profile row's dated agreement (a blog row citing the website row's); every placement names what it Links to, where it depends on another; for a placement inside a written piece, that piece's status is read and reported, a warning where it is not `final`, never a gate (D55). **A feed episode (D59):** its show's details approved; its row `ready`, with its words, chapters and `pub_date` approved; its M5 render tagged by `media.py feed tag` (the row's `render`, `bytes` and `seconds` written); `feed chapters` written; `feed check` exiting 0 against the show's tracked feed; and every episode page on a site other than the feed's a placement of its own. | — |
| — | scheduled → published | Not a gate: every schedule row for the piece is `posted` or `dropped`, and each posted row has its publish-log row (`publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/`, which for a feed episode also sets its register row `published` and writes the tracked feed, D59). | — |

A piece whose gate is `n/a` moves straight past that status (an audio-only piece goes scripted → produced with `M3: n/a — no picture`; a recorded talk with `M3: n/a — recorded`). **The procedure that passes M2 records M3's `n/a` beside it** **(build ruling C2)**: `write-script` (`scripts/workflows/02-write-a-script/`) for a scripted piece with `picture: false` — a podcast episode or an audio-only standalone voiceover, whose brief lists no picture deliverable — and says so; `production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/` for a recorded piece; the audiobook narration procedure for an audiobook. `storyboard` refuses such a piece, and a later procedure that finds the entry missing records it before it goes on; `production/workflows/03-assemble-the-master/` (an audio master) and the podcast procedure accept a piece at `scripted` with M3 `n/a`. A gate is waived only on the author's explicit word, recorded in `verified:` with the date and the reason. **A gate is reopened by change** (syntek-author's verification rule): a material change after a gate passed (a beat rewritten, a claim added, a shot replaced, a deliverable re-cut) clears that gate's date and every later one, and `status:` steps back to the rung the earliest cleared gate leads from; a corrected typing slip clears nothing. **A re-time is not material for M3**: re-writing the storyboard's Time column from the words, or adding a Timing note, clears `M4.cues`, `M4.stills`, M4 and every later gate, never M3; a note that replaces a shot or changes the words is a revision and still clears M3 (Section 6.3).
**M4's sub-checks** (D17). A **scene piece** — one whose shot list types a shot `scene` (Section 6.3), its master rendered by `scene.py render` from its scene file — dates four sub-checks in `verified`, each passing only after the one before, and a sub-check that does not apply is `n/a` with its reason, like a gate. **`M4.takes`**: every segment of its voiceover register heard by the author, `approved` and archived (D52), and the approved takes joined by `media.py voice join` (`production/workflows/02-make-a-voiceover/`). **`M4.words`**: `transcribe` has timed every word of the joined voice against the script, its `<piece>.words.json` and `<piece>.words-check.md` are written through `-o`, and the author has read the check and agreed that the voice says what the script says, a skipped cross-check named in it. **`M4.cues`**: `cues` has matched every board's lines, through `-o`, the storyboard's Time column is re-timed from `<piece>.cues.json`, and the author has read the re-timed board before anything is animated (`M4.words` and `M4.cues` both in `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`). **`M4.stills`**: `lipsync`, run on the joined voice in `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/` beside `transcribe`, has written `<piece>.mouth.json` through `-o`; `scene.py stills` has drawn every board at each master's size, its mouths from that file, which the author first sees here; Claude has opened every still by the path `stills` printed and reported; each finding of its checks is fixed or accepted by the author; and the author has watched a preview render — `scene.py render` at the edit decision list's `size`, which writes the master `<piece>.master.mp4` itself — has seen the mouths keep to the voice and has no timing note left to apply (`production/workflows/10-animate-a-scene/`, which then renders every other aspect towards M4). A timing note goes on the storyboard and clears `M4.cues`, `M4.stills` and M4 (Section 6.3), and the piece goes back through `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`; a note that replaces a shot or changes the words clears M3 as well and goes back through `scripts/workflows/03-storyboard-a-piece/`. Every other piece — footage, stills and cards through `assemble`, or sound only — records the four `n/a` with a reason (`'n/a — footage'`, `'n/a — no picture'`): the procedure that passes M3 writes them beside it; a piece whose M3 is `n/a` (a recorded piece, a piece with `picture: false`, an audiobook) has them written by the procedure that passes its M4 (`production/workflows/03-assemble-the-master/`, `production/workflows/04-master-a-podcast-episode/` or `production/workflows/06-master-an-audiobook/`), which also writes any it finds missing before it goes on (a piece boarded before they existed). They clear like gates: a material change clears that sub-check, every later one, M4 and every later gate, and `status:` steps back as above. A new or re-rolled take clears all four and M4, and `transcribe` and `lipsync` run again on the new joined voice, because `<piece>.words.json` and `<piece>.mouth.json` must be made again; a re-timed board clears `M4.cues`, `M4.stills` and M4 and needs no new `lipsync`, because the mouth cues depend only on the voice. `run-media-workflow` reads them under `storyboarded` as progress, never as dates with no matching status, and routes on the first undated one (Section 5.1).

### 6.2 Script and transcript

`scripts/src/pieces/<piece>/script.md` — frontmatter `piece`, `version`, `approved` (DD/MM/YYYY or empty), `words`, `estimated_seconds`; H1 `# <Title> — script`; one H2 per beat, `## N. <Beat name> (target MM:SS)`, the target being **that beat's own duration**, never a running time, so the beats' targets sum to the piece's (ruling T1; `script time` compares every beat with its own target); one sentence per line:

```markdown
## 1. Hook (target 00:03)

VO: {brisk} The ferry is late again.
TEXT: Late again?

## 2. The tide decides (target 00:20)

ON: Every crossing waits for the tide, not the timetable.
SFX: gulls, low
ON: {pause 0.6} So the timetable is a promise the sea never signed.
```

A spoken line opens with an upper-case speaker tag and a colon (`VO`, `ON`, `HOST`, `GUEST`, `NARRATOR`, a character's name); `TEXT`, `SFX`, `MUSIC` and `NOTE` are cues, never spoken. Directions sit in braces at the start of a line or before a phrase; `{pause S}` adds seconds. The script holds no IPA (pronunciations live in `voice.md`) and no stage direction outside braces or cue lines. **Delivery, effects and music remain in the script:** `{whispering}`, `{shouting}`, `{laughing}` or another direction records the intended performance, and `speak plan` converts it to an audio tag for a supporting model. `SFX:` describes a sound effect, and `MUSIC:` describes the music wanted and where it enters, changes or stops; neither is spoken text. A separately generated effect or music track is approved, logged and cleared through the existing footage and rights registers, then placed in the edit decision list as `[[audio]]` with `role = "effect"` or `role = "music"`. Its fades, gain and ducking stay in that list, which both master routes use. Music generation and commercial use depend on the author's eligible plan and current licence; the generation details and licence evidence are retained, and no workflow promises that a platform will never claim, mute or remove a track. The script's directions, effects and music also enter the cue index below, so animation and audio can refer to the same instructions and timing anchors. A line is cited as `beat.line` (`2.1`), counting spoken lines only. An audiobook has no `script.md`: its plan is its chapter register (Section 6.7), its credits' words are written beside it, and chapter text is read from its source by `media.py audiobook text` (D23) and never copied into a tracked file.
`scripts/src/pieces/<piece>/transcript.md` (a recorded piece, D46) — the same format, with frontmatter `piece`, `source` (the recording's footage ID), `made` (`speech-to-text DD/MM/YYYY` or `by hand`), `approved`; H1 `# <Title> — transcript`; beat H2 `## N. <Beat name> (at HH:MM:SS.mmm)`, the beat's start on the recording; spoken lines only, as said (no braces), with a word the transcript cannot settle written `[unclear]` and flagged `VERIFY`.

### 6.3 Storyboard and shot list

`storyboard.md` — frontmatter `piece`, `version`, `approved`; H1 `# <Title> — storyboard`; table `| # | Beat | Time | Picture | Spoken | On screen | Sound | Vertical framing | Timing notes |` — `#` B01…; Beat = the script's beat number; Time `MM:SS–MM:SS`, estimated from the beat targets at M3 and, for a scene piece, re-timed from `<piece>.cues.json` once its voice is timed (`M4.cues`, Section 6.1); Spoken cites script lines as `beat.line` (`2.1–2.3`); Vertical framing `centre`, `crop x=<px>`, `pad` or `native` (a scene rendered at the vertical size itself, never cropped: pixel art cropped or scaled leaves its grid); Timing notes the author's notes from the stills and the preview render, `—` where there are none. A board written before the column existed has eight columns, and `cues` reads it as having no notes, and a hyphen in Time or Spoken as the en dash. **A re-time is not a revision for M3**: re-writing Time (and the shot list's Seconds) from the words, or adding a Timing note that only moves time, raises `version` and keeps `approved:` and M3, clearing `M4.cues`, `M4.stills` and M4, and the piece goes back through `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`; a note that replaces a shot or changes the words is a revision, which clears `approved:` and M3 as before and goes back through `scripts/workflows/03-storyboard-a-piece/`.
`shot-list.md` — no frontmatter; H1 `# <Title> — shot list`; table `| Shot | Board | Type | Source | Framing | Seconds | Status | Rights |` — Shot S01…; Type `camera · screen · stock · still · card · colour · generated · scene`; Source a footage ID (`F0007`), an asset path, a card under `production/src/cards/`, a colour `#RRGGBB`, the piece's scene `production/src/scenes/<piece>.scene.py` (Type `scene`: a board the code-animated scene draws, which makes the piece a scene piece, Section 6.1; every board of a scene piece is one, because `scene.py render` draws the whole master, and the brief's `ai_visuals` agrees, D25; a scene piece's other shots name only the images and text captures its scene shows, which `media.py real` indexes, never a video: recorded video inside a scene is deferred, Section 11, and a piece that shows it is a footage piece, assembled, D47) or `to shoot`; Status `needed · captured · logged · cleared`; Rights a rights-register ID or `—`.

**The cue index** (`production/src/scenes/<piece>.cues.json`, written through `-o`, D66). The maintainer approved the separate-list schema and its delivery, effect and music events on 06/10/2026:

```text
{piece, beats: […], boards: […], lines: […], words: […], events: […]}
```

- `piece` is the piece key. Every beat, board and spoken line has its ID and exact `start` and `end` seconds on the joined voice; beats retain their titles, boards retain their Spoken references and Timing notes, and lines reference zero-based indices into `words`. Each word appears once, retains its alignment fields (`word`, `start`, `end`, `score`, `segment`) and adds its script-line ID. The index does not duplicate the words inside boards or beats.
- `events` preserves the script's delivery directions, sound-effect instructions and music instructions as `delivery`, `sfx` and `music`. IDs are `e01`, `e02` onwards in script order; regenerating after instructions change requires reviewing the links. Each records `instruction`, `beat`, `source_line` (one-based within the script body, after frontmatter), `script_line` (spoken line ID, or null) and `anchor`. Delivery anchors name a zero-based `word`, its `start` or `end` edge and exact `time`; directions before a phrase anchor to its next word, and trailing directions to the preceding word's end. They carry no inferred sound duration.
- Each SFX or music event connects to exactly one existing `[[audio]]` row through optional `cue = "e02"` (confirmed by the maintainer on 06/10/2026). That row supplies its footage `source`, `role = "effect"` or `"music"`, placement, source range, gain, fades and ducking. Its event copies these as `source` and `mix`, so the edit decision list remains the source of the actual mix settings. Its audio `anchor` identifies the zero-based row and `voice_offset`; exactly one `vo:<piece>` row locates the joined voice. Event `start` and `end` are exact seconds relative to that voice, potentially negative for an earlier music entrance; `mix.master_at` retains the master placement, with `source_in` and `source_out` retaining the source range. Unlinked, duplicate or incompatible rows are findings, never guessed placements.
- Anchors connect events to the timed words, lines, beats or boards. The original instruction remains available even when a generation model does not support it. Directions never become spoken words or caption text. A tag's position records intended direction; it does not prove that the generated audio obeyed it or establish the duration of a laugh or other non-word sound. The author reviews the performance, and separately generated effects and music have their own approved placement and duration.
- Unmatched rows retain null times and are reported with exit 1. Unresolved event anchors or unapproved sound placements are reported rather than assigned invented times. All numeric times are finite. `cues` prints its findings and the re-timed storyboard for review, and never rewrites the storyboard. Exact timings remain in JSON; the printed storyboard uses `MM:SS–MM:SS`, rounding its start down and its end up to whole seconds so the displayed range contains its spoken words, with exact seconds printed separately (confirmed by the maintainer on 06/10/2026).
- The toolkit builds this index offline from tracked sources. It never generates voice, effects or music itself; paid generation remains an author-agreed production action, and template development and audit fixtures make no paid calls.

### 6.4 Edit decision list

`production/src/edits/<piece>.toml`:

```toml
[edit]
piece = "003-why-the-ferry-runs-late"
version = 1
size = "1920x1080"          # the master's frame; "" for an audio master (.wav), which takes sound only from its clips
audio_rate = 48000
loudness = "social"         # social | podcast | none

[[clip]]                    # in timeline order
id = "c01"
source = "F0007"            # a footage ID · an asset under production/src/assets/ · a card under production/src/cards/
in = "00:00:05.250"         # video and audio sources: the range used
out = "00:00:12.500"
seconds = 0.0               # stills, cards and colour clips: how long it holds (no in/out)
colour = ""                 # "#RRGGBB" with no source: a blank clip of `seconds`
frame = "fit"               # fit | crop | pad
x = 0                       # crop offset in source pixels
mute = false                # true drops the clip's own sound
motion = "none"             # stills and cards: none | push-in (a slow zoom, ffmpeg zoompan)
transition = "cut"          # how this clip enters: cut | fade (ffmpeg xfade)
transition_seconds = 0.0

[[overlay]]                 # a card (rendered with alpha) over the picture
source = "production/src/cards/003-why-the-ferry-runs-late.title.html"
at = "00:00:00.000"
until = "00:00:02.500"

[[audio]]
source = "vo:003-why-the-ferry-runs-late"   # vo:<piece> | a footage ID (music, recorded sound) | an asset path
cue = ""                   # optional event ID (e02) on an effect/music row
at = "00:00:00.000"         # where it starts on the timeline
in = ""                     # optional source range, for a footage or asset source
out = ""
gain_db = 0.0
fade_in = 0.0               # seconds (ffmpeg afade)
fade_out = 0.0
role = "voice"              # voice | music | effect
duck = false                # music: true ducks it under voice
```

`media.py assemble` builds the voice track by joining the approved segments of `production/src/voiceover/<piece>.toml` in order with their `pause_after`, and renders each card it names first (D47). In a picture master every clip has a picture (a sound-only source goes in `[[audio]]`); an audio master takes sound only from every clip.
**The frame rate is never a key**, as no `platforms.toml` table has `fps` (Section 6.8): `assemble` takes it from the first video clip that is not an animated GIF or WebP, else from the first animated one rounded to a whole rate, and otherwise uses 30, snapping it to a standard rate.
**Every clip boundary falls on the master's frames** (in an audio master, on its samples): each clip starts and ends on the frame or sample nearest its running total on the timeline, never rounded clip by clip, so the master ends on the one nearest the edit's total however many clips it has. An `[[overlay]]` shows from the frame its `at` rounds to up to, not including, the frame its `until` rounds to, so one timed to a clip's start or end starts or ends with that clip. `assemble` refuses (exit 2) a clip shorter than one frame (or sample) of the master where it falls, its start and end rounding to the same one, and an overlay whose `at` rounds to the master's end or later, or that would show on no frame.
**A scene piece's sound comes from this file too** (Section 6.3, D71): its list holds `[edit]` and `[[audio]]` rows and no `[[clip]]` or `[[overlay]]`, `size` being its first master's frame (each further aspect is rendered with `scene.py render --size`, Section 6.16). `scene.py render` lays the scene's frames over the mix of those rows — the `vo:<piece>` voice (the join `media.py voice join` writes), music and effects, with their ranges, fades, ducking and `loudness` — made by the one function `assemble` also uses, so the same rows sound the same under either master; the `vo:<piece>` row's `at` is the voice's offset, by which the scene places its mouth and words (D70); `assemble` refuses such a list, as it refuses any with no `[[clip]]`.

### 6.5 Footage manifest

`production/src/footage/manifest.toml` (seed: header comments only):

```toml
[[file]]
id = "F0001"                 # permanent, never reused
path = "raw/talk-cam-a.mp4"  # relative to production/src/footage/; footage add copies the file here
kind = "video"               # video | audio | music | stock | image | generated
sha256 = ""
bytes = 0
duration = 0.0               # seconds; 0.0 for an image
location = ""                # where the master copy lives: a label, never a secret URL
recorded = ""                # DD/MM/YYYY
rights = ""                  # RRNNNN when licensed or showing people
notes = ""                   # generated: the take or chapter it archives (D52)
```

### 6.6 Voiceover segment register

`production/src/voiceover/<piece>.toml`: `[voiceover]` `piece`, `voice_use` (a row of `voice.md`), `model_id`, `output_format`, `per_cue` (`false`; `true` only for kinetic-caption shorts); `[[segment]]` `id` (`s01`…), `script_lines` (`beat.line` or a range: one sentence or one beat), `text` (as spoken, braces removed — the caption text), `request` (as sent), `take` (an integer: the `N` of `tN`), `file` (relative to `production/src/voiceover/`: `generated/<piece>/takes/<name>`; a row written before the per-piece layout keeps its flat `generated/<name>`, and every reader opens the path the row names, D65), `characters`, `pause_after` (seconds), `status` (`generated · approved · rejected`), `archived` (the footage ID once archived, D52). **A re-roll is a new take**: `take add` writes `take` and `file`, numbering after the segment's highest take in either the flat `generated/` or the piece's `generated/<piece>/takes/`, and resets `status` to `generated` and `archived` to empty, because the new take is unheard and unarchived; an earlier take, once archived, stays in the footage manifest (D52).

### 6.7 Audiobook chapter register

`production/src/audiobook/<piece>.md` — the audiobook's plan (it has no script, D23) — frontmatter `piece`, `approved` (DD/MM/YYYY or empty: the author's approval is the piece's M2), `route` (`human` | `ai` | `external`), `channels` (each the store part of an `[audiobook.<store>]` key of `toolkit/data/platforms.toml`: `acx`, `google_play`, `spotify_authors`, `inaudio`), `voice_use`, `model_id`, `output_format` (as `voice.md` records it; the toolkit reads `model_id` for the chunk limit and `output_format` to name and read a take, D21); table `| Ch | Title | Source | Route | Takes | Master | Duration | Check | Status |`; rows `ch00` (opening credits), `ch01`… in reading order, `ch99` (closing credits) **(ruling T4)**; Source the chapter's file or 'provided' with its path, and for `ch00` and `ch99` the credits' own `<piece>.ch00.md` and `<piece>.ch99.md` beside the register (their spoken words, one sentence per line; the only text the folder holds); Status `planned · generated · recorded · mastered · checked · packaged`; for the `external` route, Master names the export and where it is kept, and Check reads `external`. Human recordings and approved masters are source media (Section 6.5; D52).

### 6.8 `toolkit/data/platforms.toml`

Header comment in syntek-author's data-file style (purpose, "Read by …", "Template-owned: `copier update` may refresh it", re-check every `source` at least every six months, a `# Fields:` line). Tables: `[house]` (decisions, not platform facts: `social_loudness_lufs = -14.0`, `social_true_peak_db = -1.0`, `basis`, `checked`, `source = "DESIGN.md D24"`); `[platform.<name>]` (platform-wide limits: title, description, caption and post characters, hashtag rules, grid aspect, the AI-flag field; for the brand's own channels the embed limits, the minimum image sizes of search and share previews, the email clipping size and the accessibility markers, D53; for the podcast, the feed's limits, D58); `[platform.<name>.<format>]` (one deliverable); `[audiobook.<store>]` (a mastering and delivery target). Deliverable fields: `kind` (video | image | audio) · `width` · `height` · `aspect` ("W:H") · `min_seconds` · `max_seconds` · `fps_min` · `fps_max` · `container` · `video_codec` · `video_bitrate` · `audio_codec` · `audio_bitrate` · `audio_sample_rate` · `loudness_lufs` · `true_peak_db` · `safe_zone {top, bottom, left, right}` · `caption_formats` · `max_size` · `checked` (DD/MM/YYYY string) · `source` · `extra_sources` · `verify` · `chosen` · `notes`; and `max_size_mobile` (a warning above it: `card.py` since 0.1.0, `image` since 0.2.0); and, read by the toolkit since 0.2.0, `formats` (an image deliverable's file formats, the first being `image`'s default;
