`["gif"]` on a `kind = "image"` table marks an animated GIF, which `cut` makes, D57) · `alpha` (`false`: no transparency; `image` flattens it, `feed check` refuses it on a cover or episode art) · `min_width` (`feed check`, for the cover and episode art) · `audio_tracks` (`0`: no sound track, D57) · `colours_max` (a GIF's palette) · `cbr` · `enclosure_type` (the feed's type for the enclosed audio) · `id3_version` (the ID3 version `feed tag` writes, D59) · and in `[platform.podcast]` the keys `feed tag` and `feed check` test: `tag_max_chars`, `show_description_max_bytes`, `episode_description_max_chars`, `chapters_min`, `chapter_min_seconds`, `chapters_per_hour_max`, `chapter_title_max_chars`, `chapter_title_max_words`, `explicit_values`, `episode_types`, `show_types`. **Documentary keys** are read by people, guides and checklists, never by the toolkit, and the `# Fields:` header says which they are: `pix_fmt` (the toolkit always writes and checks `yuv420p` for video), `min_height`, `max_width`, `audio_codec_alt`, `audio_bitrate_max`, `loudness_tolerance_db` (the toolkit's tolerance is ±1 LU), the own channels' platform-wide keys (`embed_*`, `autoplay_muted_allowed`, `captions_required`, `featured_min_width` and the other structured-data minimums, `display_width`, `html_clip_kb`, `html_target_kb`, `image_formats`, `video_tag_supported`), and the rest of `[platform.podcast]` (`display_max_chars`, `episodes_shown_max`, `max_seconds`, `chapters_auto_min_seconds`, `transcript_formats`, `transcript_max_size_spotify`, `feed_move_min_days`, `artwork_dpi`), and `[platform.youtube]`'s `chapters_min` and `chapter_min_seconds`, against which `prepare-post` checks a video's chapters (Section 6.12). Rules for readers: a key in `verify` is unconfirmed and a checklist flags it; an absent key means no published value (keep the source's rate; skip that check); `max_size` is copied as stated; `safe_zone` is in pixels at the table's size, `0` meaning none published; `caption_formats = []` means burn captions in, and a table with `kind = "image"` or `audio_tracks = 0` carries no `caption_formats` and takes no captions (D57); `chosen` marks a house choice inside an official range; no `fps` key anywhere, so no preset passes `-r` unless the source exceeds `fps_max`. A platform with no thumbnail or cover table (LinkedIn, Facebook and TikTok at 03/10/2026) takes the video deliverable's size, flagged `VERIFY` in its guide; `website`, `blog` and `newsletter` have image tables of their own, house choices in `chosen` (D53). Video for the brand's own sites takes `[house]`, whose `basis` says so (D62). Podcast loudness comes from `[platform.podcast.apple_rss_audio]`, `podcast.feed_audio` included; the ACX targets from `[audiobook.acx]`. Overrides (D26) are read from `brand/src/platforms/overrides.toml`.

### 6.9 Captions

SRT is the master format; VTT is generated from it. House limits: **≤ 42 characters per line** for landscape and square deliverables, **≤ 32** for 9:16; **≤ 2 lines** per cue; **≤ 17 characters per second**; a cue lasts 1.0–7.0 s; ≥ 0.08 s between cues. Break at sentence or clause boundaries, never inside a name. A speaker change inside one cue opens each line with `- `. Non-speech sounds in square brackets, lower case, only when they matter. No audio tags or braced directions. Words as the script or transcript spells them, en_GB.
Naming: `<piece>.en-GB.srt` (timed to the master) · `<piece>--cNN.en-GB.srt` (timed to a cut) · `<piece>.<FID>.en-GB.srt` (timed to a recording, D46: `003-….F0001.en-GB.srt`) · a `.<platform>-<format>` qualifier (the key's dot and every underscore as hyphens, Section 6.16) before `.en-GB` when a deliverable needs its own line width (`003-why-the-ferry-runs-late--c01.youtube-short.en-GB.srt`) · a `.vtt` beside each `.srt`.
A generated voice: `captions from-segments` on its register (D22 route 1) or, once its words are timed, `captions from-words production/src/timing/<piece>.words.json` (route 4), each with `--deliverable` and with `--offset` the voice track's start on the master → the master-timed SRT, then `captions retime --in --out` per cut, as for any master-timed SRT.
A recorded piece: `captions align transcript.md <recording audio> --anchors` → the recording-timed SRT (`production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/`) → `captions retime --edl` → the master-timed SRT (`production/workflows/03-assemble-the-master/`, or `publishing/workflows/01-plan-the-cut-downs/` where it is still missing), whose cue times `repurpose` uses for each cut's In and Out → `captions retime --in --out` per cut, or `extract-audio --in --out` and `captions align --lines` where the author sees drift. Every caption file is written with `-o` (Section 4.2).
Burn-in: an ASS file with `PlayResX`/`PlayResY` = output size; style from the `--caption-*` tokens; `Alignment=2`; `MarginV` = the preset's `safe_zone.bottom`, or 8% of the height where none is published (a house value); `MarginL`/`MarginR` = its side margins; the fonts folder passed as `fontsdir`.
**On a profile site** (D60) the `.vtt` is the sidecar, loaded by `<track kind="captions" srclang="en-GB">` from the same origin or with CORS, and never burned in; an embed keeps the captions uploaded with its `youtube.long` deliverable. **The published transcript** `<piece>[--cNN].transcript.en-GB.md` (beside the captions, tracked; one per distinct cut placed, the whole piece's without `--cNN`) is made with `captions transcript TEXT [--lines B.L-B.L] -o …` from the script or transcript, then described by hand where the picture carries what the words do not; one sentence per line (D30), a paragraph per beat, speaker names only where two or more people speak, a recorded piece's line 'As recorded on DD/MM/YYYY'. The words are what was said: the spelling and grammar companions may report on it and fix only a mis-transcription. A feed episode's `podcast:transcript` is its master-timed `.vtt`.

### 6.10 Cut-down plan

`publishing/src/cut-downs/<piece>.md` — frontmatter `piece`, `master` (render name; for a piece with a master per aspect, the list of them, `<piece>.master.mp4` first), `approved`; H1 `# <Title> — cut-down plan`; table `| Cut | Deliverables | Lines | In | Out | Frame | Hook | Status |` — Cut `c01`…; Deliverables are keys (the cut-downs of the piece live only here, never in the brief); Lines the script or transcript lines the cut carries (`3.2–3.9`); In and Out `HH:MM:SS.mmm` on the master; Frame `centre`, `crop x=<px>`, `pad` or `native` (cut from the master at the deliverable's own aspect, which the skill passes to `cut` as its source, with no reframing); Status `planned · approved · rendered · checked`; then `## cNN — <hook>` notes per cut (opening words, on-screen title, caption width, thumbnail if it needs its own, why this moment stands alone). A silent loop (`website.hero_loop`) is a cut (D57): In and Out within its `max_seconds`; its Lines are `—`, because it carries no words, so the rules that a cut stands alone in whole sentences and differs from every other cut do not bind it, and it may share frames with a cut it was taken from, which its note names. A GIF preview is not a cut: it is the thumbnail brief's (Section 6.11). **A master per aspect** (Section 6.16): a scene piece whose deliverables or cuts need a second aspect renders it natively from its scene (`scene.py render --size`), never by cropping or scaling the first, because pixel art cropped or scaled leaves its grid and a native render lays the scene out for that frame and its safe zones. Every master of a piece shares one timeline — the same frames of one scene at another size, under the same sound — so a cut's In and Out hold on each, and a `native` cut is taken from the master at its deliverable's aspect.

### 6.11 Thumbnails, cards and the token contract

**Option scoring (D74):** `publishing/src/thumbnails/<piece>[--cNN].<platform>-<surface>.options.json` and matching `.scores.json` retain the exact candidates and evaluation; new evaluations take a new suffix rather than overwrite a result. They are author-owned, created in use, never seeded. `## Variants` records candidate IDs and the author's choice; `## Checks` cites the result, rubric/model versions, guidance sources/dates and visual checks. Title-only evaluations may use empty image words and concept; inapplicable image questions are omitted, not scored zero. The default rubric is editorial practice, not platform facts; platform questions may be filtered by `platforms` and `surfaces`. Every supplied guidance row has `version`, `source`, `checked`, `basis` (`published` or `hypothesis`) and `summary`. Update project guidance and rubric copies on the author's word, preserving previous evaluations.

`publishing/src/thumbnails/<piece>[--cNN].md` — frontmatter `piece`, `cut` (`cNN` or empty), `deliverables` (`youtube.thumbnail`, `instagram.reel_cover`, `podcast.episode_art`, `website.poster`, `website.og_image`, `blog.featured_image`, `newsletter.preview_image`, `newsletter.preview_gif`, or a video key on a platform with no thumbnail table, …), `html`, `approved`; H1 `# <Title> — thumbnail brief`; body `## Promise` · `## Words on the image` · `## Image` · `## Variants` · `## Checks`. **The image is a small committed still in `production/src/assets/`** (a frame is extracted there with `media.py frame -o`), set on the frame as `--picture`, never a git-ignored render, so a layout rebuilds from tracked files (build ruling C3). **`## Image` also records every moment taken from a render**, so a regenerated image equals the approved one: each poster's `--at` timecode on its video's render, and the GIF preview's In and Out on the master and what its first frame shows (D57). One layout serves one design per aspect: deliverables of one aspect in one brief share its words, and where two need different words (art that takes none beside a thumbnail that takes a title) the author chooses, or a cut gets its own `--cNN` brief; an image a partner site wants in its own branding is a design export, not a brief (D61). A show's cover and its ID3 copy belong to a show, not a piece: they are a design export (`brand/workflows/03-record-a-design-export/`), encoded with `media.py image` to `podcast.cover` and `podcast.id3_cover` (D58).
**HTML layout contract** (`toolkit/templates/*.html`, the brand's `previews/thumbnail.html` and `previews/card.html`, every piece's thumbnail and card, and the page a scene file writes, on which `scene.py` sets the same variables before its first draw, D70, D71): `<!doctype html>` and `<html lang="en-GB">`; a `<link rel="stylesheet">` to `brand/src/design-system/tokens.css` by relative path and nothing over the network (`card.py` aborts every http(s) request and fails); `html, body { margin: 0; width: 100vw; height: 100vh; overflow: hidden }`; one file serves every aspect through `aspect-ratio` media queries; text stays inside `--safe-top`, `--safe-right`, `--safe-bottom` and `--safe-left` (px), which `card.py` sets on `:root` from the preset with `--frame-width` and `--frame-height`, and, rendering for a deliverable, `data-deliverable` and `data-platform` (D57), so a layout may show an element for one deliverable only, such as a newsletter preview's play button (an element carrying `data-play-button`, hidden except under `:root[data-deliverable^="newsletter."]`; under `data-transparent` the layout keeps only it and the words, which is the GIF's overlay); images by relative path; an overlay card leaves its background transparent (`data-transparent`). A rendered PNG reaches its deliverable's other formats through `media.py image`, and a poster is `media.py image` of the deliverable's own render at `--at`, with no layout. Previews open with `<!-- @dsCard group="Colors|Type|Spacing|Brand|Components" -->` on line 1 and link `../tokens.css`; a copy fixes the path.
**Required tokens** in `tokens.css` (`media.py tokens` and `card.py check` fail on any missing): `--color-bg`, `--color-surface`, `--color-text`, `--color-text-muted`, `--color-accent`, `--color-on-accent` · `--font-display`, `--font-body`, `--weight-display`, `--weight-body` · `--space-unit`, `--radius` · `--caption-font`, `--caption-weight`, `--caption-size` (in `vh`), `--caption-text`, `--caption-outline` (both `#RRGGBB`), `--caption-outline-width` (in `vh`). Fonts are `@font-face` rules in `tokens.css` with `src: url("fonts/…")`; `--caption-font` names a family in `fonts/` or installed.

### 6.12 Post package

**Title selection (D74):** propose several titles, score them against the content, audience, surface and the current thumbnail concept, then have the author choose. The package carries the selected Title only; option and score JSON stay beside the thumbnail briefs even for title-only evaluations. Scores never date `approved`, change a schedule or pass M7. Feed episode words remain in their show register, and placement titles remain limited to media-owned words (D55, D59).

`publishing/src/posts/<piece>.md` — frontmatter `piece`, `approved`; H1 `# <Title> — post package`; one H2 per deliverable, `## <platform>.<format>[ — cNN]`, with bold-led bullets: **Title**, **Description** (a fenced block, **one sentence per line** like every `src/` artefact, D30, a blank line between paragraphs), **Chapters** (only where the platform takes them, below), **Hashtags**, **Disclosure** (the toggle and its setting, the description line, the spoken or on-screen line), **Captions** (file, burned or sidecar), **Thumbnail** (render name), **Render**, **Calendar** (the content calendar entry, where syntek-author's is present, D48), **Scheduled** (DD/MM/YYYY HH:MM, `<%TIMEZONE%>`), **Limits** (each count against its `platforms.toml` key). **At posting time `prepare-post` gives the author each title and description in chat as paste-ready text**, the fenced lines joined into paragraphs, a video's chapter lines after them, and never writes that joined text to a file (build ruling C4). **A video's chapters** (the **Chapters** bullet) go under a deliverable whose platform table has chapter keys — `youtube.long` only, at 0.3.0 (`chapters_min` and `chapter_min_seconds` in `[platform.youtube]`, D26) — as a fenced block of `MM:SS <title>` lines, the first at `00:00` and each later one a beat's start on the master, rounded down to the second (Section 6.16): its start in `<piece>.cues.json` plus the `at` of the edit decision list's `vo:<piece>` row, or, for a piece with no cues file, its first master-timed caption, all of them tracked files, so nothing is read from a render (D50); each title is the beat's name unless the author chooses another. `prepare-post` proposes them, and the author approves them with the package (M7). A video's chapters are lines of its package, never a file of their own, and never a feed episode's, which live in its show register and `<piece>.chapters.json` (D59); WebVTT chapter tracks for video on the brand's own sites are deferred (Section 11).
**A placement on the brand's own channels** (D61) is the one file a page of a profile site or an issue of a list plays or shows first — the video, the embed, the GIF, or a still only where there is no GIF — and has its own H2, `## <platform>.<format>[ — cNN] — <platform>:<slug>` (an embed: `## youtube.long — website:<slug>`). It carries only the words media owns (D55), in these bullets in place of Title, Description and Hashtags: **Placement** (the site or list slug, the page, post or issue as the author names it, and the route: self-hosted, or an embed of `youtube.long`), **Written piece** (the piece it sits in: where syntek-author is present, the unit in its library layer, by path in prose, read and never edited, its status never recorded here but read at M7; standalone, 'the author's, outside media'; `none` for a page that is the media's own, such as an episode page), **Title** (an embed's or figure's title, never the post's), **Alt text** (one line for every image the placement shows, naming what it shows; for a newsletter image, naming the video and saying that it opens it), **Links to** (the placement it depends on, and its publish-log URL once reported), **Structured data** (the VideoObject values the site's code emits: name, description, thumbnail, duration, upload date, content or embed URL, transcript), **Agreement** (`own`, or the profile row's dated record for a site or list the brand does not own; a blog placement cites the website row's); then **Disclosure** (no platform label: the house line beside the media, D25), **Captions** (the `.vtt` and the published transcript of exactly what the page plays), **Images** (the poster, share, featured or fallback renders that go with it: bullets, never placements or schedule rows), **Render** (or `n/a — embed`; for a self-hosted file, the hosting tests of the channel's guide, run by the author), **Calendar**, **Scheduled** (the written piece's own publish date or send time, cited, never set here; DD/MM/YYYY HH:MM, `<%TIMEZONE%>`) and **Limits**, as above. Standfirsts, figure captions, link text and issue blurbs are the written piece's, drafted on the written side (D55). **A feed episode** (D59) has `## podcast.feed_audio`: its **Title**, **Description** and chapters are cited from its show register, never repeated; it adds **Episode page** (the feed site's page that plays it, the register's `page`, with the block the site shows: the player, the show notes, the chapter list, the published transcript and the subscribe links the author has reported), **Feed** (the `feed check` result, the upload copy and the order and time of upload) and **On YouTube** (the `youtube.long` deliverable that carries the episode in the show's podcast playlist: this piece's own, or for an episode cut from a talk, its parent's, by publish-log URL; or `none`). **Every episode page on another site** is a placement, `## podcast.feed_audio — website:<slug>` (or `blog:<slug>`), with Placement, Written piece, Links to (the feed episode), Agreement, Captions (the same published transcript) and Scheduled, playing the same enclosure URL (D61). A `youtube.long` that carries a feed episode adds **Playlist** (the show's podcast playlist, which the author adds it to by hand, D58).

### 6.13 Registers and logs (seeds; banner; empty tables)

| File | Columns | Vocabularies |
|---|---|---|
| `brand/src/design-register.md` | `\| Design \| Claude Design link \| Export path \| Storage \| Exported \| Notes \|` | Storage `git · lfs`; Exported DD/MM/YYYY |
| `production/src/rights-register.md` | `\| ID \| Item \| Kind \| Pieces \| Holder \| Licence or permission \| Evidence \| Expires \| Status \| Notes \|` | ID `RR0001`… permanent; Kind `music · stock · footage-release · location · likeness · voice-consent · quotation · scripture · font · artwork · generated`; Status `needed · requested · cleared · refused · expired` |
| `production/src/credits-log.md` | `\| Date \| Piece \| Tool \| Calls \| Characters or minutes \| Voice \| Model \| Output \| Notes \|` | Tool = the MCP tool name without its prefix, or `voice remix` for the one call the toolkit makes directly (D82), whose row `voice remix run` writes, with the source voice's ID as Voice and the trial folder as Output |
| `scripts/src/piece-register.md` | `\| No. \| Piece \| Kind \| Origin \| Parent \| Opened \| Retired \| Notes \|` | Origin `scripted · recorded`; a retired number keeps its row |
| `publishing/src/schedule.md` | `\| Date \| Time \| Platform \| Deliverable \| Piece \| Package \| Status \| Notes \|` | Status `planned · ready · posted · moved · dropped`: `planned` when the package is written, `ready` at M7, `posted` or `dropped` on the author's report; `moved` takes the new date, keeps the old one in Notes and stays open; media deliverables only, Notes citing the content calendar entry where syntek-author's is present (D48); Platform is `<platform>`, or `<platform>:<slug>` for a placement on the brand's own channels (D61), and Deliverable the key of the one file the placement plays or shows first (the video, `youtube.long` for an embed, the GIF, or a still only where there is no GIF; its other images are bullets of its package section, never rows); a placement inside a written piece takes its Date and Time from that piece's publish date or send time, cited in Notes, and is `moved` when the written side moves it (D55); a feed episode is one row, Platform `podcast`, Deliverable `podcast.feed_audio`, covering the feed and the feed site's episode page; every episode page on another site is a placement row of its own, Platform `website:<slug>` (or `blog:<slug>`), Deliverable `podcast.feed_audio` |
| `publishing/src/publish-log.md` | `\| Date \| Platform \| Deliverable \| Piece \| URL \| Disclosure set \| Captions \| Notes \|` | one row per upload or placement the author reports; never deleted; Platform as in the schedule; a feed episode's URL is its episode page on the feed's site, a placement's the page or issue it went into, and the show's directory listings belong in the podcast profile; written by `publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/` only |

### 6.14 `brand/src/voice/voice.md`

`## Spoken style` (pace in words a minute, register, how the brand name is said, words never said aloud) · `## Written voice` (one line: the written voice is syntek-author's voice notes or brand voice, where present, and is not restated) · `## Narrators` `| Use | Service | Voice | Voice ID | Model ID | Stability | Similarity | Style | Speed | Output format | Consent | Chosen |` (Use `voiceover · narration · podcast-host · character:<name>`; Consent a rights-register ID for any clone, and for a voice remixed from a clone the source clone's, D82) · `## Pronunciations` `| Word | IPA | Respelling | Source | Notes |`. Ships with `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM` slots and no rows.

### 6.15 Platform profile and overrides

`brand/src/platforms/<platform>.md`: `## Account` (handle and URL as `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM`) · `## The social plan` (one line: where syntek-author's social-media documents are present, whichever of them sets a value for this platform — the plan, the calendar or an operating procedure — owns it, and the four sections below hold only what none sets, D48, D56) · `## Cadence` · `## Tone on this platform` · `## Call to action` · `## Hashtag sets` · `## Deliverables used` (keys) · `## Overrides` (one line pointing to `overrides.toml`).
**The brand's own channels** (D53, D61): `website.md` and `blog.md` hold `## Sites` and `newsletter.md` holds `## Lists` in place of `## Account`, each a table that ships with its header, an `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM` flag and no rows. Sites: `| Site | Domain | Owner | CMS or hosting | Player | Captions | Carries | Agreement | Notes |` — Site a kebab slug, frozen once a schedule row uses it (the blog reuses the website's slugs where both ship); Domain as the author gives it; Owner `brand` or who owns it; Player `self-hosted`, `youtube-embed` or `both` (the site's usual route; a package may choose per placement); Captions `track` or `youtube`; Carries what the site takes (pages, posts, episode pages, a podcast feed by its show slug); Agreement `own`, or the dated record for a site the brand does not own, recorded once: a blog row for a site the website profile also lists reads 'see website', and holds the record itself only where the website platform is not chosen (D61). Lists: `| List | Sender | Service | Owner | Images hosted at | Agreement | Notes |`, never a subscriber, a count or an account ID (D36). Their `## The social plan` line reads 'where a social-media document of syntek-author is present and covers this platform (names it with a cadence), it owns what it sets' (D56), and `## Hashtag sets` says the channel takes none. The podcast profile's `## Account` names each show by its register's slug, with the show's directory listings as the author reports them, and, where the website platform is not chosen, the web server that serves each self-hosted feed; the feed's own values live in the register, never here (D59).
`brand/src/platforms/overrides.toml` (seed: header comments only; always shipped, so removing a platform never deletes a confirmed correction):

```toml
[[override]]
key = "youtube.short.max_seconds"   # <platform>.<format>.<field> of platforms.toml
value = 60                          # a TOML value of the field's own type
why = ""                            # what the platform now says
source = ""                         # where it says it
checked = ""                        # DD/MM/YYYY
```

### 6.16 Names

| Pattern | Example | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `NNN-kebab-title` | `003-why-the-ferry-runs-late` | a piece (frozen) |
| `<piece>/` | `003-why-the-ferry-runs-late/` | a piece's output folder inside `production/src/renders/`, `production/src/voiceover/generated/` and `publishing/src/renders/`: git-ignored, never holding a tracked file, with no pair and no README (D42, D64) |
| `proofs/<what>-DD-MM-YYYY/` | `proofs/brand-kit-05-10-2026/` | brand-kit proofs, which no piece owns, in `production/src/renders/` |
| `voice-trials/<name>/` | `voice-trials/warm-narrator/` | voice trials, which no piece owns, in `production/src/voiceover/generated/`, made by `speak plan --trial <name>` or `voice remix run`: never a take, never in a register (D21) |
| `<name>.pNN.mp3` · `<name>.remix.json` | `gruff-ferryman.p01.mp3` · `gruff-ferryman.remix.json` | a voice remix's previews, numbered from `p01` in the order returned, and its record (the date, the source voice, the description, the text, and each preview's `generated_voice_id`, media type and duration), in its `voice-trials/<name>/` folder, written by `voice remix run` (D82) |
| `<platform>.<format>` / `<platform>-<format>` | `youtube.short` / `youtube-short` | a deliverable key / its form in a file name |
| `<piece>.master.mp4` (`.wav`) | `003-….master.mp4` | the master, `production/src/renders/<piece>/` |
| `<piece>.master.<W>x<H>.mp4` | `003-….master.1080x1920.mp4` | a further master at another aspect, beside `<piece>.master.mp4` (which keeps the `[edit]` size): a scene rendered natively at that size by `scene.py render --size`, never cropped or scaled from the first (Section 6.10, D71) |
| `<piece>.<card>.html` / `<piece>.<card>.<W>x<H>[.transparent].png` | `003-….title.html` / `003-….title.1920x1080.png` | a card in `production/src/cards/` / its render in `production/src/renders/<piece>/cards/`, with `.transparent` for an overlay's, so a card used as a clip and as an overlay at one size keeps both renders (D47) |
| `<piece>[--cNN].<platform>-<format>[.burned].<ext>` | `003-…--c01.youtube-short.burned.mp4` | a deliverable, `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/` |
| `<piece>[--cNN].md` / `.html` → `<html-stem>.<platform>-<format>.png` | `003-…--c02.youtube-short.png` | a thumbnail brief and layout → its render, `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/` |
| `<piece>.sNN.tN.mp3` (`.pcm`) | `003-….s04.t2.mp3` | a voiceover take, `production/src/voiceover/generated/<piece>/takes/` (D21) |
| `<piece>.voice.wav` | `003-….voice.wav` | the piece's approved takes joined in register order with their pauses, mono, by `voice join`, `production/src/renders/<piece>/` |
| `<piece>.words.json` / `<piece>.words-check.md` | `003-….words.json` | the joined voice's word timings / the check the author signs, tracked in `production/src/timing/` |
| `<piece>.mouth.json` | `003-….mouth.json` | the joined voice's mouth shapes, tracked in `production/src/timing/` (D68); `levels` writes `<piece>.levels.json` only as a working copy, in `production/src/renders/<piece>/timing/` (D66) |
| `<piece>.cues.json` / `<piece>.real.json` | `003-….cues.json` | the piece's cue index / the index of the real sources its scene shows (D69), tracked in `production/src/scenes/` |
| `<piece>.scene.py` / `scene/index.html` | `003-….scene.py` | a piece's scene as code, tracked in `production/src/scenes/` and run by path / the page it writes, in `production/src/renders/<piece>/` (D70) |
| `<piece>.bNN[.mid].<W>x<H>.png` / `<piece>.stills.json` | `003-….b02.mid.1080x1920.png` | a board's first (or middle) frame, `bNN` its storyboard number in lower case / every box and flag of the piece's stills; both in `production/src/renders/<piece>/stills/` (D71) |
| `<sprite>-<pose>-mouth-<shape>` · `<sprite>-<pose>` with `<sprite>-mouth-<shape>` · `<sprite>-<pose>-blink` | `host-point-mouth-d.svg` | a character's whole frame · a mouthless pose and its mouth layer · its blink layer: design exports in `brand/src/exports/`, read by the scene kit's sprite slot; `<sprite>` and `<pose>` kebab slugs, `<shape>` `a`–`h` or `x` (D70) |
| `<design>-vN.<ext>` · `<sprite>-<pose>-vN` | `harbour-lane-logo-v2.svg` · `host-point-v2-mouth-d.svg` | a revised design export beside the original it supersedes, `N` the next unused number from 2; a sprite revision is filed whole: a revised pose becomes `<pose>-vN` in every name built on it, and a revised mouth layer, which names no pose, renames the sprite (`<sprite>-vN`); the scene names it (D75) |
| `<piece>.chNN.pNN.txt` / `<piece>.chNN.pNN.tN.mp3` / `<piece>.chNN.mp3` | `012-….ch03.p02.t1.mp3` | an audiobook text chunk / its take / a mastered chapter |
| `<piece>.chNN.chunks.toml` | `012-….ch03.chunks.toml` | a chapter's chunk sidecar in `generated/`: the chunks in order, each with `pause_after` (D23) |
| `<piece>.ch00.md` / `<piece>.ch99.md` | `012-….ch00.md` | an audiobook's opening / closing credits, beside its chapter register (`ch00`, `ch99` are the credit rows) |
| `<piece>.sNN.scratch.wav` / `<piece>.chNN.pNN.scratch.wav` | `003-….s04.scratch.wav` | an espeak-ng scratch track, a voiceover's in `production/src/voiceover/generated/<piece>/` (never in `takes/`), a chapter's in the audiobook's `generated/`: approximate, never a take, never entered in a register |
| `<stem>[.<in>-<out>].wav` | `talk-cam-a.wav` | audio extracted for speech-to-text or alignment: by default in `production/src/renders/<piece>/`, or at the folder's top for a stem with no piece key, such as a footage file's; `production/workflows/08-bring-in-a-recording/` passes `-o` into the recorded piece's `production/src/renders/<piece>/` (D64) |
| `<html-stem>.<platform>-<format>.<ext>` / `<stem>.<platform>-<format>.<ext>` | `003-….blog-featured-image.avif` | an image deliverable `media.py image` encoded (`jpg`, `png`, `webp`, `avif`), `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/` (a stem with no piece key, such as a show cover's export, at the folder's top); `<stem>` the source's name up to its first `.` (D57) |
| `<piece>[--cNN].newsletter-preview-gif.gif` | `003-…--c03.newsletter-preview-gif.gif` | a GIF preview, made by `cut` from its thumbnail brief (D57), `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/` |
| `<piece>[--cNN].transcript.en-GB.md` | `003-…--c01.transcript.en-GB.md` | a published transcript of the whole piece or of one cut, `publishing/src/captions/` (D60) |
| `<show>` | `harbour-lane-talks` | a podcast show's kebab slug, frozen: names its register and its feed |
| `<show>.toml` / `<show>.feed.xml` | `harbour-lane-talks.feed.xml` | a show register / the feed as last published, both tracked in `publishing/src/podcast/`; the same `<show>.feed.xml` at the top of `publishing/src/renders/`, which no piece owns, is the upload copy (D59) |
| `<piece>.podcast-feed-audio.mp3` | `003-….podcast-feed-audio.mp3` | a feed episode's audio, `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/`: encoded at M5, tagged in place by `feed tag` at M7 (D59) |
| `<piece>.chapters.json` | `003-….chapters.json` | an episode's JSON chapters, `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/` (D59) |
| `MM:SS <title>` | `00:12 The tide decides` | a video's chapter line, under its post package's **Chapters** (Section 6.12): a beat's start on the master, rounded down to the second, and its name, the first line at `00:00`; a line of the package, never a file of its own, and never a feed episode's `<piece>.chapters.json` |
| `<platform>:<slug>` | `website:studio` | a placement on the brand's own channels: the schedule's and the publish log's Platform cell, and a package H2's qualifier (D61) |
| `transcript.md` | — | a recorded piece's transcript, in its piece folder |
| `F0001` · `RR0001` · `cNN` · `sNN` · `chNN` · `pNN` | — | footage, rights, cut, segment, chapter and part IDs: permanent (`ch00` and `ch99` are always the credits) |
| `TEMPLATE-GUIDE/` | `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/` | the folder of the guide's chapters: the family's name, frozen, and the one upper-case folder in a project (D84) |
| `kebab-title.md` | `updating-and-recovery.md` | a shared chapter, unnumbered, directly in `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/` (syntek-author's bytes), or one of media's guides, in `how-to/docs/syntek-media/` (Section 4.4) |
| `NN-kebab-title.md` | `01-overview.md` | one of media's chapters, in `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/`, numbered from 01 there, frozen and append-only (Section 6.18) |
| `NN-verb-first-name/` | — | one of media's procedures, in `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/`, numbered from 01 there, frozen and append-only (D32, Section 4.4) |

In a file name, the deliverable key's dot and every underscore become hyphens (`linkedin.video_vertical` → `linkedin-video-vertical`), in a render, a thumbnail and a caption qualifier alike.
**Folders** (D64): in an output folder a name that begins with a piece's key lands in that piece's folder — a take in its `takes/`, a card PNG in its `cards/`, the working copy of a timing or scene file in its `timing/`, a scene's page in its `scene/` and its stills in `stills/` (D70, D71), anything else in `<piece>/` itself — and a name with no piece key stays at the folder's top; the audiobook folder's `generated/` and `renders/` stay flat. A tracked file never sits in a per-piece folder: it stays flat in its tracked folder under its `<piece>.` name.
No tracked media file ends in `.log`, `.out`, `.aux`, `.toc`, `.tmp`, `.bak` or `.orig`: syntek-author's root `.gitignore` ignores them.
A published file's name follows Apple's rule for feeds and URLs: lower-case ASCII letters and digits with `-`, `.` and `/`, no spaces (whether Apple accepts hyphens literally is `VERIFY`); every published name above already does. A corrected episode file is published under a new name (a `-v2` before its extension) while its render name stays (D58).

### 6.17 Podcast show register and feed

`publishing/src/podcast/<show>.toml` (D59) — written by `media.py feed new` from the skeleton `toolkit/media_feed.py` holds, never a seed; one file per show; every value the feed carries, and nothing else. The folder's `CLAUDE.md` fences the same skeleton with no live flag: `feed new` adds one `# AUTHOR TO CONFIRM:` comment, on `owner_email`, which the author removes on confirming it, and `toolkit-smoke.sh` check 18 holds the fenced copy and the module's equal, flags and computed values aside:

```toml
[show]
show = "<show>"              # kebab slug, frozen: names this file and its feed
title = ""                    # no episode numbers; within platform.podcast.tag_max_chars
site = ""                     # the website profile's slug of the site serving the feed, where that platform is chosen; else "" and the podcast profile names the server
feed_url = ""                 # written by feed new from --feed-url: the feed's permanent https URL, for life (D58); feed new --rekey alone changes it, before anything is published
new_feed_url = ""             # only while moving the feed: a redirect too, for platform.podcast.feed_move_min_days
link = ""                     # the show's page
media_base = ""               # the URL folder the audio, art, captions and chapters are published under
language = "en-gb"
author = ""
owner_name = ""
owner_email = ""              # public: the directories send their ownership codes here; a role address (feed new flags it)
copyright = ""
category = ""                 # Apple's category, exactly as Apple spells it
subcategory = ""
explicit = false
type = "episodic"             # episodic | serial (serial: every episode numbered)
cover = ""                    # the published name of the podcast.cover render; a new cover gets a new name
id3_cover = ""                # the podcast.id3_cover render encode embeds in every episode; "" = none
podcast_guid = ""             # written once by feed new; never recomputed (feed new --rekey only while nothing is published)
locked = true                 # no other host may import the feed
complete = false
block = false
youtube_route = "upload"      # upload | rss | none (D58: never upload and rss for one show)
description = """
"""                           # plain text, one sentence per line; within platform.podcast.show_description_max_bytes; the disclosure sentence where any episode uses a synthetic voice (D25)
approved = ""                 # DD/MM/YYYY: the author approved the show's details
last_updated = ""

[[episode]]                   # one row per episode, in any order; never deleted
piece = ""                    # the piece (D17)
guid = ""                     # written once by feed add; NEVER changes, even when the title or the URL does
title = ""                    # no episode or season number, no show name
number = 0                    # 0 = left out (serial needs it)
season = 0
type = "full"                 # full | trailer | bonus
explicit = false
pub_date = ""                 # DD/MM/YYYY HH:MM, the project's timezone: <pubDate>; the schedule row's time
render = ""                   # written by feed tag: the podcast.feed_audio render's name
audio_url = ""                # "" = media_base + the render's name; a corrected file gets a NEW url, the guid stays
bytes = 0                     # written by feed tag; equals the served Content-Length
seconds = 0.0                 # written by feed tag: the render's probed duration
art = ""                      # the published name of a podcast.episode_art render; "" = the show's cover
page = ""                     # the episode page on the feed's site (pages on other sites are placements, D61)
transcript = ""               # the published name of the master-timed VTT; "" = none
description = """
"""                           # plain text (no < or >), one sentence per line; within platform.podcast.episode_description_max_chars; the disclosure sentence (D25)
status = "planned"            # planned (feed add) · ready (M7, the feed workflow) · published (the author's report, publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/) · withdrawn (kept here, left out of the feed)
notes = ""

[[episode.chapter]]           # optional; at least platform.podcast.chapters_min, the first at 00:00:00.000
start = "00:00:00.000"        # on the master (D29)
title = ""                    # within platform.podcast.chapter_title_max_chars; title case
```

**Derived files and the order of work.** At M5 `encode --deliverable podcast.feed_audio` writes `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/<piece>.podcast-feed-audio.mp3`, untagged. At M7, in the feed workflow: the row's words and chapters approved; `feed tag` tags that render in place and writes `render`, `bytes` and `seconds`; `feed chapters` writes `publishing/src/renders/<piece>/<piece>.chapters.json`; `feed check` (against the tracked feed) exits 0; the row is `ready`; and `feed write SHOW --as-of '<the episode's pub_date>' -o publishing/src/renders/<show>.feed.xml` writes the upload copy, refused while any GUID of the tracked feed has vanished or changed. **The order and time of upload** is the files first (audio, art, captions, chapters), then the upload copy of the feed, at the episode's `pub_date` and not before, because a static feed has no clock and Spotify downloads every file the feed names; where another episode of the show becomes `ready` before then, the upload copy is written again. **After the author reports the feed live**, `publishing/workflows/06-record-a-publication/` sets the row `published` and runs `feed write SHOW --as-of '<the same time>' -o publishing/src/podcast/<show>.feed.xml`, which gives the bytes uploaded (the workflow compares it with the upload copy and reports any difference: the register changed after the upload) and replaces the tracked copy, so a diff shows exactly what the directories read. **The author's tests**, printed by the feed workflow and never run by the toolkit: the audio host answers HEAD with the enclosure's length and type and honours byte ranges; the cover answers HEAD with `Last-Modified`; the feed is served as `application/rss+xml`; captions and chapters carry CORS. **The author's hand steps per episode**, in the workflow's hand-back: upload the files, then the feed, at the time; upload the transcript in Spotify for Creators (whether Spotify reads `podcast:transcript` is `VERIFY`); add the YouTube upload, where there is one, to the show's podcast playlist. A feed made by a site's own code from these values is checked with `feed check --feed`.

### 6.18 Template-guide chapters (D84)

Media's chapters are `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/NN-kebab-title.md`, numbered from 01 in that folder, frozen and append-only; a shared chapter is `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/kebab-title.md`, unnumbered, syntek-author's bytes and never written here (syntek-author D56). A chapter is instructional, not an artefact: plain English for the person the project belongs to, at most 300 lines (D31) and one sentence per line (D30). It opens with its title and a short gloss and ends with 'Where the rules live' and then 'Next', as the shared `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/CLAUDE.md` says; it carries no metadata header and no delimiter, so it is the same bytes in every render (Section 4.4), and it names a gated path, a client file or a gated workflow in prose (Section 3.5). Nothing in it is reported by syntek-author's `make lint` or `make flags` with `SCOPE=how-to/src`, in any of its document types (syntek-author D56): no line holds two sentences, no spelling is en_US, no em dash stands outside a heading, and every flag form is backticked; `coexist-test.sh` check 24 holds it. It explains and routes, never restates (rules 06 Section 2): where it and a rule differ, the rule decides. `01-overview.md` ships ungated in every brand kind, since `_message_after_copy` names it by its full path (D13).

---

## 7. The template repository root

```text
syntek-media/
├── copier.yml · DESIGN.md · README.md · CONTEXT.md · CHANGELOG.md · VERSION
├── family-shared.json    ← media's record of the family files and blocks it vendors from one syntek-author tag (D80), never shipped
├── LICENSE · CONTRIBUTING.md · SECURITY.md · CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md   ← community files (D83), never shipped: MIT; changes to the template; private vulnerability reports; Contributor Covenant 2.1
├── AGENTS.md · GEMINI.md ← development entrypoints for Codex and Antigravity; route to the development manual (D73)
├── .mcp.json             ← development MCP: code-review-graph only (Section 3.7)
├── .gitignore            ← development only: the code-review-graph database and the handoff files
├── .agents/              ← a real folder holding only its pair (D76)
├── .codex/               ← pair + config.toml (development Codex settings: no fallback, no skill rule)
├── .claude/              ← development manual + settings.json (dev isolation)
├── .github/              ← pair; scripts/ (audits, tests, syntek-author-names.txt + pair); workflows/ (audit-template.yml + pair); CODEOWNERS; PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md; ISSUE_TEMPLATE/ (GitHub's YAML files only, no pair, D83)
├── examples/             ← pair + business / author-fiction / author-nonfiction .answers.yml (invented)
├── handoffs/             ← pair; the handoffs themselves stay on the maintainer's machine (D27)
├── migrations/           ← pair + the update-time scripts, never shipped (D76): media's own v0.4.2-agents-folder.py and v0.6.0-how-to.py (D76, D84); agents-outside-link.py and family-update.py, vendored from syntek-author (D85, D81); and family-refresh.json, media's own refresh data, which no manifest lists (D81)
└── template/             ← everything that ships
```

D73 adds development agent entrypoints and configuration; the root `.agents/` is a real folder (D76); no root `CLAUDE.md` (the development manual is `.claude/CLAUDE.md`), a root `.gitignore` (development only: the code-review-graph database and the handoff files), no `adopt/`, a root `migrations/` (with its pair) since 0.4.2 (D76), and a root `family-shared.json` since 0.5.0 (D80): media's record of what it vendors, in the family's format (syntek-author D48) — `format` 1, `owner` `syntek-media`, `release` `v` + `VERSION` at a release and `unreleased` between, each vendored file under its family path (`<template>` kept) with its source, kind, size, SHA-256 and `mode` 755 for the hooks and the two family migration scripts (the update script since 0.6.0, D81), or a link's target, `"scope": "repository"` for those two scripts, and each block with its marker lines, size and SHA-256, 'rules-web-tools' with its `destination` — so that syntek-author's `family-shared.sh --sibling` can compare media's copies. Only `shipped-seeds.sh --write-manifest` writes it, after a tag is vendored; the audits hold the tree to it, and `coexist-test.sh` holds it to the `--author` tree's own manifest. The three example answers files together turn on every platform and every media kind. Metadata headers appear on `CHANGELOG.md`, this file and `.claude/CLAUDE.md` only, never on the community files (D83). The development manual's release recipe says: never tag a release while `coexist-test.sh` reports SKIP, which, since 0.6.0, `run-all.sh`'s summary lists by name and counts (D81), and name the syntek-author baseline in the `CHANGELOG.md` entry (D27).
**Dev isolation** (root `.claude/settings.json`): `permissions.deny` lists `Skill(<name>)` for every skill under `template/.claude/skills/` (the ten of Section 5.1, in that order, then the seven of Section 5.3, D80); `skillOverrides` sets each of the same seventeen `"off"`, because a deny refuses a skill but leaves it listed in the session (approved 14/10/2026; the seven family lines approved with D80); the user-level Codex recipe in `.codex/CLAUDE.md` names the same seventeen; `"claudeMdExcludes": ["**/template/**/CLAUDE.md", "**/template/.claude/**"]`. The development manual adds: never call an `mcp__elevenlabs__` tool from here; tests use runtime-generated tones.
**`.github/scripts/syntek-author-names.txt`** (D27): a header comment (date, syntek-author commit and "plus working tree"), then one line per name — `top <entry>` for each entry of syntek-author's `template/` plus `top build`, `claude <entry>` for each entry of its `template/.claude/`, `skill <name>` for each skill folder (52 at the baseline `dfe9528`, syntek-author `v0.7.0`, D81), `rules <file>` for each file in its rules folder — sorted. Written only by `coexist-test.sh --refresh-names`. The family-shared names stay in it, `top how-to`, the guide's family folder, among them since 0.6.0 (D84); `SM_FAMILY_SHARED` excepts them where they are checked (D27, D80).

**Audits** (`.github/scripts/`, each with syntek-author's header: job, why, numbered checks, "Numbers are stable identifiers. Append, never renumber.", what it cannot check, `--self-test` with fixtures written at run time and one finding per mutation, requirements, usage, exit codes 0 clean / 1 findings / 2 script error). `_common.sh` transcribes this file: the skill catalogue (`name gate modes`, modes `B F N`), the path catalogue, seeds, examples, the copy-only shared paths (nineteen, D76; thirty-six since D80; fifty since D84), the spine set, the identity tokens, the companion list, the D13 allow, ask and deny lists, the `_message_after_copy` lines and the D83 community files with the private-reporting URL, and loads syntek-author's names from `syntek-author-names.txt`; helpers `answer_list`, `load_answers`, `gate_true` (atoms `always business fiction nonfiction author p:<x> k:<x> seed`), `negate_gate` with list membership, `kind_of`/`profile_of` (`--`), `owned_paths` (reads `<render>.owned`), and `build_sets` telling the copy-only class (`_copier_operation == 'update'`) from examples. Prefix `SM_`/`sm_`; scratch `syntek-media-audit.XXXXXX`; git identity `syntek-media audit`. The 0.2.0 work adds to `_common.sh`: `website`, `blog` and `newsletter` in `SM_PLATFORMS` (so the gate atoms `p:website`, `p:blog`, `p:newsletter`, the profile seeds and the index-row vocabulary follow; the default platforms are unchanged, D54), a catalogue row for every path Section 3.5 adds (`p:podcast` for the feed folder, workflow and guide), `publishing/src/podcast` in the hard-coded `SM_PAIR_ONLY_GLOBS` (so `shipped-seeds.sh` check 11 polices it: a register or feed left there by a toolkit run inside `template/` would otherwise ship), the seeds comment's count (twenty-six), the two new toolkit modules `toolkit/media_image.py` and `toolkit/media_feed.py`, and `removable_value` choosing the gated value of the answer with the most catalogue paths (the first such on a tie), so that `update-test.sh` check 13 and `coexist-test.sh` check 18 take `podcast` away where a project has it, the one removal that deletes a gated folder holding author files. **One `minimal` render ships `blog` without `website`**: `business--minimal` becomes `PLATFORMS=[blog] MEDIA_KINDS=[long-video]`, so `doc-references.sh` check 1 catches `blog.md`, the blog profile or a shared file backticking a path only `website` opens (D28's one platform and one media kind still hold). The 0.3.0 work adds to `_common.sh`: catalogue rows, gated `always`, for `production/src/timing/` and `production/src/scenes/` (both also in `SM_PAIR_ONLY_GLOBS`, so a timing or scene file a toolkit run leaves there inside `template/` is caught), `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/` and `production/workflows/10-animate-a-scene/`, the guide `production/docs/reference/scenes-as-code.md`, and the toolkit's three new files, `toolkit/transcribe.py`, `toolkit/scene.py` and `toolkit/media_scene.py` (D64, D67, D70, D71); no gate atom, platform or media kind changes. The 0.4.4 work adds to `_common.sh`: the two `voice remix run` asks in the D13 ask list (eleven entries, which the seed check, the `_message_after_copy` lines and the fixtures read), `character-voice` in the companion list, and catalogue rows, gated `always`, for `production/workflows/11-design-character-voices/`, `production/docs/reference/character-voices.md` and `toolkit/media_voice.py` (D82); check 21 of `skill-conformance.sh` asks the guard sentence of a skill naming either kind of entry, and `output_directory` and `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` only of one naming an `mcp__` tool, because the remix ask names a command that writes its own folder, not a tool that takes an `output_directory`. The 0.4.5 work adds to `_common.sh` (D85): the vendored outside-link script's release tag, SHA-256 and Git mode (`SM_OUTSIDE_RELEASE`, `SM_OUTSIDE_SHA256`, `SM_OUTSIDE_MODE`) and the family-shared migrations block's marker lines and SHA-256 (`SM_FAMILY_MIGRATIONS_BEGIN`, `SM_FAMILY_MIGRATIONS_END`, `SM_FAMILY_MIGRATIONS_SHA256`), each transcribed by hand from syntek-author's `v0.5.0` and its `family-shared.json`, which `shipped-seeds.sh` checks 21 and 22 read; `sm_family_migrations_block`, which prints the block, markers included, for `sm_fixture_template`'s `_migrations` and for the self-test fixture of `shipped-seeds.sh`; `sm_sha256`; the hand fix's first step, `SM_OUTSIDE_CHECK`, which `update-test.sh` check 21 looks for, and `SM_CRASH_VERSION`, the scratch release of its check 23; and `_min_copier_version` 9.12.0 in both fixture templates (D2). The 0.5.0 work adds to `_common.sh` (D80): `SM_CLIENTS` and four gate atoms for the client gates — `agents` (`AGENT_CLIENTS | length > 0`), `agents-md` (Codex or OpenCode), `gemini-md` (Gemini CLI or Antigravity) and `cl-<client>` — which `gate_true`, `SM_GATE_EXPR` and `SM_SHIPPING_ATOMS` know, with `A_CLIENTS` read from a render's answers (none where the record predates the question); catalogue rows for the client files under those atoms and, gated `always`, for the `.claude/hooks/` and `handoffs/` pairs, the two hooks, the two family scripts and the map; the family skills' catalogue, `SM_FAMILY_SKILLS_CAT` (`name gate modes`, their mode files `MEDIA-<KIND>.md`), which gives each its gate and modes only; the seventeen new shared paths and the two folder gates, `SM_SHARED_DIRS`; **`SM_FAMILY_SHARED`**, each vendored file's family path, source and scope, with `SM_FAMILY_LINKS`, `SM_FAMILY_BLOCKS` (name, source, destination and marker lines), `SM_FAMILY_DEFERRED` (`family-update.py` and the 'update' block, D81), `SM_FAMILY_TEMPLATE_OWNED`, `SM_FAMILY_RENDERED` (the three client files that carry the shared keys), `SM_FAMILY_RELEASE` and `SM_DATAROOT_MAP`, and `is_family_path`, `is_family_entry` and `is_family_skill` (its names, `SM_FAMILY_SKILLS`, read from the `SKILL.md` paths `SM_FAMILY_SHARED` holds), which every name and form check's exemption reads, so one list drives them all (D27); a reader and writer of `family-shared.json`; the family's eight asks, its deny and its `hooks` object for the seed check, `hooks` leaving the banned keys; the `--write` line among the `_message_after_copy` lines; and the companion list, `SM_AUTHOR_NEVER` and the backticked syntek-author path heads without the four former companions, `.claude/hooks` and `handoffs` (`exempt_dir` no longer exempts `handoffs/`, which now carries the family's pair, D42). It drops the values 0.4.5 transcribed by hand, `SM_OUTSIDE_RELEASE`, `SM_OUTSIDE_SHA256`, `SM_OUTSIDE_MODE` and `SM_FAMILY_MIGRATIONS_SHA256`: `shipped-seeds.sh` checks 21 and 22 read `family-shared.json` instead. The 0.6.0 work adds to `_common.sh` (D81, D84): `SM_FAMILY_RELEASE` `v0.7.0`; the fifteen new family files in `SM_FAMILY_SHARED`, `migrations/family-update.py` (scope `repository`) and D84's fourteen, so that `how-to` is a family entry for every name and form check (`shipped-brands.sh` check 11, `coexist-test.sh` check 13) with no case of its own; the 'update' block in `SM_FAMILY_BLOCKS`, between 'migrations' and 'rules-web-tools' as the manifest orders them; `SM_FAMILY_DEFERRED` empty, so `coexist-test.sh` check 22 defers nothing; the fourteen among the copy-only shared paths, and so among the `_message_after_copy` lines, with the guide line and the update command run as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier`; for the seed check, `idleCompaction`, syntek-author's `v0.7.0` `hooks` object and `Edit(/how-to/**)`; the `baseline` task's lines, transcribed from syntek-author's `copier.yml` at that tag, and the lists `migrations/family-refresh.json` must hold (the twenty-seven refresh paths, the restore, the offered settings entries and the `mcp` lane), which `shipped-seeds.sh` checks 30 and 31 read; catalogue rows, gated `always`, for the fourteen and for media's chapters, procedures and guides under its three `syntek-media/` folders, the procedures' being the catalogue `shipped-brands.sh` check 7 holds `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/` to; `how-to/*/syntek-author/` among the backticked syntek-author paths, which `doc-references.sh` check 6 reads; and the Python pin: before any Copier runs, `_common.sh` sets `UV_PYTHON` to `python3` where it is unset and refuses (exit 2) a `python3` or a `UV_PYTHON` older than 3.14, because uv prefers a managed interpreter it already has, so an unpinned `uvx` may run Copier, and the family scripts inside it, on an older Python (syntek-author's `_common.sh` does the same), the minimum `docs-pairing.sh` check 20 also reads.

**Over-author scope.** On an `<kind>--over-author` tree every per-tree audit checks only **media-owned paths**: the paths media's copy added to the committed syntek-author render, which `generate-all.sh` records beside the tree as `<render>.owned` (the shared paths syntek-author's render already holds are never among them, because the copy skipped them). Consequences: `shipped-seeds.sh` skips checks 5, 6, 10, 12, 13 and, since 0.5.0, 29 (the shared files are syntek-author's; `coexist-test.sh` check 12 proves them unchanged, but for the entries media's `baseline` task adds to `.claude/settings.json` once each, D81), and its check 27 reads only the family files media's copy wrote (those syntek-author wrote first are its own, D80); `doc-references.sh` checks only media-owned files, resolving their backticked paths against the whole composite; `docs-pairing.sh` checks only media-owned folders, with syntek-author's exemptions and media's (syntek-author's own folders are its audit's business, and its `exempt_dir` does not know media's README-only folders); `shipped-brands.sh` skips checks 5 and 8 (syntek-author's paths are there by design; `coexist-test.sh` check 6 proves ownership) and runs 3, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15 and, since 0.6.0, 16 on media-owned paths; `skill-conformance.sh` checks media's catalogue only; `line-cap.sh` checks media-owned files; `byte-identity.sh` runs on standalone trees only.

Since 0.5.0 each `minimal` render also shuts a different client gate (D28, D80): `business--minimal` ticks no client, `author-fiction--minimal` only OpenCode and `author-nonfiction--minimal` only Antigravity, so `doc-references.sh` check 1 catches a file backticking a client path with a slash (`.codex/config.toml`, `.agents/mcp_config.json`) where that client is off; it tests no bare name (`AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `opencode.json`, `.codex`, `.gemini`, `.agents`), which a template-owned file therefore names in prose (Section 3.5); `defaults` and `all` tick all four, as a fresh copy does, and every render's recorded `AGENT_CLIENTS` and `DATA_STORE` are held to that (check 10 of `shipped-brands.sh`). |

a missing file is exit 2, never a SKIP) · 12 no file but its `README.md` in any `generated/`, `renders/` or `raw/` folder, at any depth, so no per-piece output folder or anything in one ships (D19, D42, D64; on an over-author tree, media-owned paths only) · 13 media-supplied `.agents` is a real folder whose `skills` entry is the relative link `../.claude/skills`, and the client configurations media supplies (`.codex/config.toml`, `.agents/mcp_config.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `opencode.json`) parse as TOML or JSON, their content the family's since 0.5.0 (the two servers, the model answers), whose bytes are `shipped-seeds.sh` checks 25 and 27's (D73, D76, D80; existing shared paths are outside media's scope) · 14 media-supplied `AGENTS.md` and `GEMINI.md` route to every `.claude/syntek-*-agents.md` bridge, and the template-owned `.claude/syntek-media-agents.md` reads every other one present (D73, D76) · 15 media's copy supplies no `.claude/mcp_config.json`: Antigravity's workspace file is `.agents/mcp_config.json`, and the old path keeps its `copier.yml` lines but ships no more (D76; over syntek-author, a copy syntek-author wrote is its own) · 16 every path media ships under `how-to/` is one of the thirteen family paths there or lies under `how-to/docs/syntek-media/`, `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/` or `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/`, so a stray file, or a folder named for another template, fails (D84; syntek-author D56, whose own audit sees a sibling's placement only with `--sibling`, which no CI passes; on an over-author tree, media-owned paths only). |

check 4 leaves GIF tables to this check · 17 a table with `audio_tracks = 0` (`website.hero_loop`) renders with no audio stream, and a mutation that keeps the sound fails its verification; `website.video` encodes with its index at the front · 18 the podcast feed end to end on a fixture show, offline (SKIP by name where the render has no podcast folder): the fenced skeleton of `publishing/src/podcast/CLAUDE.md` equals `media_feed.py`'s, flags and computed values aside; then ID3v2.3 title, author, album and chapter frames and the cover where named, the audio stream unchanged, `render`, `bytes` and `seconds` written back), `feed write --as-of` twice (well-formed XML with every required tag, the two outputs identical; no `--as-of` is exit 2), `feed write -o` the tracked copy then again after a register change (replaced), `feed chapters` (valid JSON), `feed check` exit 0, and exit 1 on each mutation: a repeated GUID, a GUID vanished from the tracked copy while its row is not `withdrawn` (`feed write` then writes nothing), an enclosure length changed under the same URL, a `<` in a title, `bytes` that differ from the render, an `AUTHOR TO CONFIRM` flag left in the register; `feed new --rekey` succeeds before any row is `published` and is exit 2 after · 19 `captions transcript` on a fixture script drops cue lines other than `TEXT:`, braced directions and a lone speaker's tags, keeps on-screen text as `[On screen: …]`, takes only the given lines with `--lines`, and writes only with `-o` · 20 the voice tools on tone takes: `speak plan` creates the piece's takes folder, writes nothing else and states characters and calls, never credits, and `--trial` creates only the trial's folder; a second `take add` for one segment numbers its take after the first across the flat and per-piece folders and resets `status` and `archived`; `voice join` writes a mono WAV as long as the takes and their pauses, and is exit 1 while a segment is not approved; `levels` writes finite numbers only, a silent stretch at the floor, to its working copy alone · 21 `lipsync` on an espeak-ng voice (a tone where espeak-ng is absent) writes `mouthCues` with a repository-relative `soundFile`, and a stand-in `rhubarb` that exits 1 makes it exit 2 (SKIP by name without Rhubarb) · 22 `transcribe` without WhisperX: a digit or symbol in the aligner text is exit 1 before anything runs, hyphens become spaces and a respelling's parts map back to its word, a fixture alignment whose word sequence differs or whose segment has no words is exit 1, and a missing fetched file is exit 2 naming `transcribe fetch`; with the user-scope interpreter already set, a fixture voice is aligned and every word timed (SKIP by name otherwise); `captions from-words` on a fixture words file writes cues within the deliverable's limits, each from its first word's start to its last word's end · 23 in a `git init`ed copy, a timing file written with `-o` and committed is replaced by a second `-o` run, while an uncommitted change to it, an untracked copy and a copy outside Git are each refused (exit 2) with nothing changed, and `-o` over any other tracked path is still refused · 24 `cues` on a fixture storyboard (an en dash and a hyphen in Spoken; boards with and without Timing notes) and a fixture words file writes every board's times and prints the table, and a board whose lines match no words is exit 1; `real` indexes an asset and a footage image with their hashes and is exit 1 for a missing source and for a video; `where` prints the piece's tracked files and names its ignored folders without listing them · 25 a run of stills whose `seconds` are not whole frames (0.16 s at 30 fps) assembles to the frame nearest the edit's total, each still starting on the frame nearest its running total (Section 6.4); an overlay that starts on a clip boundary is on that clip's first frame and off the frame `until` falls on; `encode` of a stills master gives audio no longer than its video and passes its own duration check · 26 `check --setup` with a stand-in `rhubarb` linked from a folder lacking `res/sphinx/cmudict-en-us.dict` reports a FAIL naming the file, and with none a note naming `lipsync`; with no WhisperX interpreter, a note naming `transcribe fetch` · 27 `assemble` with a memory limit set runs under `systemd-run` where it and a user service manager exist (SKIP by name otherwise), and with no limit says it runs uncapped · 28 a scene end to end, offline, on fixtures written at run time (D71; SKIP by name without Chromium, and `render` also without ffmpeg): a two-board cue file with its words and mouth cues, a placeholder sprite drawn in CSS with an anchor on a typed line, and a voiceover register whose fixture takes are an espeak-ng voice (tones where espeak-ng is absent, as check 9's are); `stills` at a landscape and a vertical size writes both frames of each board and `<piece>.stills.json`, each still's sprite showing the shape whose cue holds that frame's middle, and is exit 1 on each mutation (overlapping text, a box outside the safe zone, overflowing text, a position off the design-pixel grid); a missing sprite frame is exit 2; `render` at both sizes writes `<piece>.master.mp4` and `<piece>.master.<W>x<H>.mp4`, each with exactly the scene's frame count, its sound as long as its picture and within ±1 LU of the target its `loudness` key selects; one frame captured twice is identical; an edit decision list with a `[[clip]]` row is exit 2 · 29 `media.py`'s interpreter choice for `transcribe.py` never takes a user-scope `UV_PYTHON` outside its `requires-python`: with `UV_PYTHON` naming Python 3.14 and no `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON`, the choice is an interpreter `uv python find --system` returns for that range or, where none meets it, the exit 2 that names `MEDIA_TRANSCRIBE_PYTHON`, and no environment is built (D20, D67, D81). Never calls ElevenLabs, never touches the network, and never builds the WhisperX environment: no audit runs `transcribe fetch` or `uv run toolkit/transcribe.py`, because uv builds one environment per script path and every temporary copy would build several gigabytes of its own; for the same reason every audit that runs `card.py` or `scene.py` (here checks 1, 4 and 28, and `assemble`'s card PNGs in check 9) runs it from one fixed path outside the temporary renders, the same on every run, so uv builds one environment for each script, never one per render. `--require-ffmpeg`, `--skip-thumbnails`. |
| `run-all.sh` | Self-tests (every audit's, and those of the vendored family scripts: `migrations/agents-outside-link.py --self-test`, D85, since 0.5.0 `template/.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.py` and `syntek-media-permissions.py`, D80, and since 0.6.0 `migrations/family-update.py --self-test`, D81, all four with `SYNTEK_DATA_ROOT` unset, so no self-test reaches a real data root, and on Python 3.14, below which each exits 1 or 2), then static audits, then `generate-all`, then per-tree audits (byte-identity on standalone trees only; on over-author trees each audit reads `<render>.owned`), then the integration tests; a summary table with `PASS`, `FINDINGS`, `ERROR` and `SKIP` kept apart, where a step that skipped anything is listed as SKIP, never PASS, with each named SKIP on a row of its own; since 0.6.0 those rows are recorded in `run-all.sh`'s own shell, never in a `$(…)` subshell that loses them, and the summary counts every status that begins `SKIP`, so a SKIP is never read as none and D27's rule has a count to read (D81), which the self-test checks with the count line and one named row; three checks on the run itself: 1 an audit or test reported findings · 2 one could not run (exit 2, or any other failure) · 3 a self-test no longer separates good input from bad; exit 2 on any error, 1 on findings, else 0. |

**CI** (`.github/workflows/audit-template.yml`, `name: Audit — Template Integrity`): on every push to any branch, pull requests and `workflow_dispatch`, no path filter; a newer run on the same branch or pull request cancels the one it supersedes, never on `main`; `permissions: contents: read`, git identity `syntek-media audit` / `audit@example.com`; jobs "[1/3] Template source", "[2/3] Renders" (installs ffmpeg; `--require-ffmpeg`; thumbnails SKIP without Chromium, lip sync without Rhubarb and word timing without a WhisperX interpreter, each by name; it never installs WhisperX), "[3/3] Update and coexistence", and a fourth, "syntek-author main (advisory)". Jobs 2 and 3 first run a plain shell step that clones `https://github.com/Syntek-Dev/syntek-author` at the tag named once in the workflow's `SYNTEK_AUTHOR_TAG` (the release media vendors from, now `v0.7.0`; bumped at each adoption, with `syntek-author-names.txt`, in one commit, D81, D85) into the runner's temporary folder and exports `SYNTEK_AUTHOR_DIR` through the environment file, or prints a warning; the scripts then run or SKIP. The four jobs' `setup-python` steps install Python 3.14 and the workflow's `env` sets `UV_PYTHON` to it, because `setup-python` alone does not choose the Python `uvx` runs Copier on, and below 3.14 the vendored scripts' self-tests fail and the outside-link stages do nothing, so `update-test.sh` check 20 stops with `ForbiddenPathError` (D81). The advisory job clones syntek-author's default branch and runs `coexist-test.sh` against it, reporting what it finds as warnings and never failing the run, so a syntek-author merge is seen in media's CI without turning it red. Every script runs `--self-test` before its real run, and job 1's loop step also runs the vendored `migrations/agents-outside-link.py --self-test` (D85), since 0.6.0 `migrations/family-update.py --self-test` (D81), and, with `SYNTEK_DATA_ROOT` unset, `template/.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.py --self-test` and `template/.claude/syntek-media-permissions.py --self-test` (D80), with no step or condition of their own; failures accumulate as `::error::` lines. The dollar-brace-brace sequence appears only in the `concurrency` block (`group` and `cancel-in-progress`); no `run:` step, matrix or secret uses it.
