## 8. Applying syntek-media

**Over a syntek-author project (the intended path).** The project's syntek-author must be `v0.6.0` or later, updated to it before media is applied or updated (D84; syntek-author D56): an older one calls every `src/` the author's, lints `how-to/src/` by default and restores nothing, and a copy-only task, non-fatal, warns where `.copier-answers.syntek-author.yml` stands without syntek-author's guide files, reading no answer (D14). On a clean, committed branch of the project, `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier copy --trust gh:Syntek-Dev/syntek-media .` (quoted, on Python 3.14, as D3 asks of every shown copy and update command, because the copy's `baseline` task runs `family-update.py` on the Python that runs Copier, D81; or `--data-file` an edited copy of an `examples/` file, with `--data SEED_EXAMPLES=false` for a project already in use, deleting, since 0.6.0, its client and model lines as well as its `DATA_*` lines, so that those questions take syntek-author's recorded answers, as `README.md` step 2 and the examples' comment say, D81). **Never pass `--overwrite`.** The shared paths syntek-author renders already exist and are skipped; any other existing path is a collision, and a non-interactive copy stops with "Interactive session required" before writing it — report it, it is a template bug; interactively Copier asks "Overwrite …?" with default yes, so answer no and report it. (An update gives no such stop: Section 10.) Review `git diff`, make the hand edits below, and commit, including `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`. There are no adoption scripts: every media folder is new, so no move has exactly one right destination, and loose media already in a repository is the author's to sort. The data-root, client and model questions take syntek-author's recorded answers as their defaults (D14, D80, D81): a first data-root answer must equal syntek-author's, and a first copy records syntek-author's clients and models where its answers file records them (all four clients otherwise), the OpenCode address, model and context only from a record with the same provider (syntek-author D47 and its Section 9 rule 7), so it writes no client file syntek-author's answers leave out; the update that first asks `AGENT_CLIENTS` beside syntek-author records them too, writes no client file, and its update script offers the two servers once to those clients' existing files. The family-shared files syntek-author already holds are skipped, whatever its release, so its bytes stay (D11); the copy's `baseline` task, on Python 3.14, then adds to `.claude/settings.json` and the MCP files, once each, the entries and servers media's refresh data lists that they lack (D81).
**Standalone.** `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier copy --trust gh:Syntek-Dev/syntek-media my-channel` (or `"$(realpath ../syntek-media)"` with `--vcs-ref=HEAD` for a local clone; never a relative path). The shared files are written from media's copies, the family-shared ones syntek-author's bytes (D80), and the project works without the writing companions, as D4 describes. A fresh copy ticks all four agent clients and keeps binary files under the data root (`DATA_STORE` `external`) by default; media files nothing there either way (D80).
**The hand edits after a copy over syntek-author** (printed by `_message_after_copy`): (1) `.claude/settings.json` — add the four `Bash(…)` allows of D13, which no lane offers (syntek-author's seed has none of them, so every toolkit call would prompt); the copy's `baseline` task, run on Python 3.14, has already added, once each, every other entry media's refresh data lists that the file lacked: the nine ElevenLabs asks and the two `voice remix run` asks (D82), without which no credit call would prompt (once the allows are added, not even a voice remix), `Edit(**/generated/**)` and `Edit(**/renders/**)`, without which nothing would keep hand edits out of the output folders, and the family's entries, `Edit(/how-to/**)` and the guide guard among them (D81, D84); only where it did not run (a Python older than 3.14, which its framed message names, or `--skip-tasks`) add those by hand as the message gives them; and, where syntek-author `v0.5.0` or later did not seed the file, the data-root guard, which neither template's copy or update ever offers (D13, D80, D81); then run `python3 .claude/syntek-media-permissions.py --write` (`--check` shows the plan first), so that they reach the Codex, Gemini CLI and OpenCode files that exist (D80; syntek-author D49); (2) optionally, a row in `README.md`'s layout table and the four layers in root `CONTEXT.md`'s tree, and a line in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` naming `.claude/rules/syntek-media/` — optional because the rules load anyway; (3) nothing in `.gitignore` or `.mcp.json` (the ignore rules are nested; the server is user-scoped); (4) `git lfs install` once, if `brand/src/exports/large/` will be used — after it the repository-local `filter.lfs.required` the tripwire may have set is harmless, since git-lfs sets the same value; (5) run `python3 toolkit/media.py check --setup` (`brand/workflows/06-check-the-setup/`) and clear what it reports before the first credit-spending call. The message ends with where the user guide starts, `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/01-overview.md` (D13 as D84 amends it); the guide is read-only (D84).
**ElevenLabs setup.** The server is user-scoped, named `elevenlabs`, and **its base path must contain the project**, or speech-to-text rejects every project file ("outside of allowed directory"; the path is resolved before the test, so a symlink does not help). Use `$HOME` when every project lives under it; otherwise the nearest folder that holds them all, such as the disk the repositories live on. An existing server is removed and re-added:

```bash
claude mcp remove elevenlabs --scope user
claude mcp add --env ELEVENLABS_API_KEY="$ELEVENLABS_API_KEY" --env ELEVENLABS_MCP_BASE_PATH="$HOME" \
  --transport stdio --scope user elevenlabs -- uvx elevenlabs-mcp
```

Replace `"$HOME"` with that folder where projects live elsewhere; the `captions` skill prints the exact command, with a base path it has computed to contain `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, when the error appears. Export the key in the shell first; never type it on the command line or commit it.
**Updating.** Each template updates separately from a clean tree, committing between, syntek-author first in a combined project (D84): `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier update --trust -a .copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`, the quoted request making `uvx` run Copier, and the update and outside-link scripts inside it, on Python 3.14 (D3, D81; syntek-author D3), with Git's automatic housekeeping off for the run (the five `GIT_CONFIG_*` variables `README.md` 'Updating a project' puts in front of it; Section 10), review, commit; syntek-author's own update uses its own `-a`. A list answer passed on update replaces the whole list. `BRAND_KIND` never changes; a different kind is a new project. The MissingFileWarning about `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml` is expected on a fresh copy and on every update (Copier's temporary renders read it too), and since 0.5.0 a second, about `.copier-answers.syntek-author.yml`, wherever syntek-author is absent (D14). **The update into 0.5.0** asked the fifteen family questions, whose defaults kept the project as it was (Section 2), and brought the rules, the mode files, the resolver, the permissions script and the map, but no family-shared file and none of the seed's new entries, because Copier renders no copy-only path on update (D11, D80). **The update into 0.6.0** brings them (D81, D84). Its render brings the rules, the resolver's and the permissions script's new bytes and media's how-to chapters, procedures and guides; media's `v0.6.0` crossing report prints, once, where the user guide starts, that it is read-only and, where the project holds syntek-author, syntek-author's guide beside it or that syntek-author must first be `v0.6.0` or later (D84); and the update script, last, prints media's update report ('▸ syntek-media update report, 0.5.0 → 0.6.0'), writes each of the twenty-seven listed family-shared files the project never had (the seven skills, the hooks with their pair and the `handoffs/` pair for a project made before 0.5.0; the shared how-to files and the guide guard for every project), refreshes each the owner never changed, keeping and reporting an edited one, puts the guide back, and adds once each the listed settings entries, top-level values and MCP servers the files lack (D81). It writes no client file. On a Python older than 3.14 the update and outside-link scripts print a framed message that they did NOT run and exit 0 (an update over an outside `.agents` link then stops with `ForbiddenPathError`), and `SYNTEK_UPDATE_REPORT=off`, `--pretend` or a failed render skips the report, the refresh, the offers and the restore; the next full update delivers them, or, where the script cannot run, a fresh copy into an empty folder shows them to take by hand. Neither template ever offers the data-root guard (syntek-author D17, D50): a settings file seeded before it shipped gains it only by hand. `update-test.sh` check 24 proves the update from 0.5.0, standalone and over syntek-author. `README.md` 'Updating a project' and `_message_after_update` say what to do where no update report, or a framed message that the script did NOT run, appears (D81; syntek-author D52).

---

## 9. Coexistence rules

syntek-author Section 9, and how media keeps each rule:

1. **Each template sets its own `_answers_file`** — media's is `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml` (D3).
2. **Each template's instructions live in `.claude/rules/<template>/`; no template updates `.claude/CLAUDE.md`** — media's eight files are in `.claude/rules/syntek-media/`, and the brand brief is rules 01 Section 1 (D5). syntek-author's project settings file, where present, outranks both templates' rules (D5).
3. **Shared root files are seed-if-missing** — media's fifty shared paths are copy-only (D11, D80, D84): Copier renders none of them on update. Since 0.6.0 media's update script does what syntek-author's does (syntek-author D51, D52, D56): it refreshes the twenty-seven family-shared files its refresh data lists (the working-practice skills, the hooks with their pair, the `handoffs/` pair and the shared how-to files with the guide guard) where the owner never changed them, puts `how-to/` back on every full update, and, on copy and on update, adds to `.claude/settings.json` and the five MCP files the listed entries and the two MCP servers once each, the servers only to the files of the clients the answers record, never to `.claude/mcp_config.json`, whichever template's script reaches them first (D81). No other shared path, the client entrypoints included, is ever touched by an update.
4. **Skill and layer names never collide; the shared writing skills ship only from `syntek-author`** — media's ten skills and five folders are new names, the companions are cited, never copied (D8, D10), and the frozen name list proves it on every run (D27). **For the family-shared set, syntek-author D51 replaces 'only from syntek-author'** (D80): the seven working-practice skills, the hooks with their pair, the `handoffs/` pair, the client files and, since 0.6.0, the shared how-to files with the guide guard ship from both templates, byte for byte from one syntek-author tag and copy-only in both, so the template that copies second skips them and Copier's update of either renders none of them; each template's update script refreshes the listed ones the owner never changed and keeps and reports one holding the other template's version, older or newer, never downgrading it, its report attributing that file to the other template and its records kept under its own `refs/<template>/` (syntek-author D51, D52; D81); `SM_FAMILY_SHARED` excepts exactly these names from the name checks (D27). D80's template-owned files take media's own names (`.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.py`, `.claude/syntek-media-permissions.py`, `.claude/syntek-media-dataroot.json` and the `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` mode files), so no file is owned by both templates. The 0.2.0 work adds no top-level folder, no `.claude/` path, no skill and no rules file: its new paths (`publishing/src/podcast/`, `publishing/workflows/08-publish-the-podcast-feed/`, three profiles and four guides) sit inside media's own layers, where the name list does not reach. The per-video part of 0.3.0 adds no such names: `production/src/timing/`, `production/src/scenes/`, the per-piece output folders (D64), `production/workflows/09-time-the-voice/`, `production/workflows/10-animate-a-scene/`, `production/docs/reference/scenes-as-code.md` and the toolkit's new files sit inside media's own layers and its `toolkit/`, and it adds no skill (Section 5.1). The 0.6.0 work adds one top-level name, `how-to`, a family folder shared file by file: media takes only the thirteen family paths there and its own `how-to/docs/syntek-media/`, `how-to/src/TEMPLATE-GUIDE/syntek-media/` and `how-to/workflows/syntek-media/`, syntek-author ships nothing under a `syntek-media/` folder, and each template's update restores its own and the shared files there, never another template's folder (syntek-author D56; D84); it adds no skill and no rules file. Agent support adds the shared paths and the namespaced bridge of D73; check those against the frozen names and preserve existing agent configuration.
5. **Large binaries never sit in plain Git** — nested ignore rules for renders and generated audio; footage and approved takes external; LFS only for large design exports (D19, D52).
6. **One project, one data-root layout** — media's data-root questions are the family's block, so a first answer defaults to syntek-author's recorded one and must equal it, a later difference in the store or the layout is reported after the update, and both resolvers refuse their data commands while two records that both hold a store disagree; `DATA_PROVIDER` follows syntek-author's but is never refused (D14, D80; syntek-author D50, D53).
7. **One project, one set of agent clients and models** — since 0.6.0 media carries syntek-author's `v0.8.0` 'questions' block, so the client and model keys default to the project's own record, then syntek-author's, taking only values media offers, and the OpenCode address, model and context only from a record with the same provider (syntek-author D47; D81): a first copy over syntek-author, and the update that first asks `AGENT_CLIENTS` beside it, record syntek-author's clients and models. The client files are copy-only and written by whichever template comes first, so media applied second writes no client file syntek-author left out, both rules 05 model tables follow the same answers, and media's update script may add each family server once to those clients' existing files where it never stood (D80, D81).

Media specifics: whichever template is applied first writes the shared files, so with syntek-author first they never mention media — rules 01, 02 and 04 carry everything instead. With media first, the shared files are media's, the family-shared ones syntek-author's own bytes, and the seed carries its hooks, MCP asks and deny (D80); but syntek-author's `make` and `tooling/` allows and its build, SQLite, Word and TeX ignore rules are absent until the author adds them by hand, as the user guide lists, while its copy's `baseline` task adds its `Edit` denies (PDF, `build/` and, outside business, `.base/`) to media's settings file, once, as it adds every entry its refresh data lists (syntek-author D51). Rules 02 claims media's skills as template-owned by syntek-media, because the shared `.claude/skills/CONTEXT.md` calls any skill outside its roster the author's own. syntek-author's `run-workflow` never sees media's layers or its how-to procedures (it reads only `how-to/workflows/syntek-author/`, syntek-author D56); `run-media-workflow` routes media work and yields writing work in prose. In a business project syntek-author's social-media family owns the written social plan and media cites it (D48). syntek-author's `make flags` and `make lint` scan media's `src/` Markdown (D30); from its `v0.6.0` they read `how-to/src/` only under `SCOPE=how-to/src` (D84; syntek-author D56), under which media's chapters report nothing (Section 6.18; `coexist-test.sh` check 24). syntek-author's `make clean` removes `build/`, which media never uses. Media never backticks a syntek-author path or skill (doc-references checks 5 and 6), so syntek-author's `doc-references.sh` stays clean on a combined tree (`coexist-test.sh` check 21). syntek-author's `skill-conformance.sh`, `shipped-variants.sh` and `docs-pairing.sh` are run only on syntek-author-only renders: on a combined tree they flag media's skills by design (check 19, uncatalogued; check 16, a mode file on a skill they treat as unmoded), and `docs-pairing.sh`'s `exempt_dir` does not know media's README-only `generated/`, `renders/` and `raw/` folders (D42). Media reads from syntek-author's answers only the family keys its shared questions and `_message_after_update` read (D14, D80), the client and model keys among them since 0.6.0 (D81), and never its `_commit`: the copy-time guide check looks for syntek-author's guide files, never at its answers (D84). Both templates' data-root resolvers are the same bytes and read every map; media's claims no folder, so it never contends with one of syntek-author's (D80). Coexistence is proved against syntek-author `v0.7.0` only, the tag CI clones (D81, D85).
**Media never ships**: `Makefile`; anything in `.claude/hooks/` but the family's three hooks (the two handoff hooks and the guide guard) and pair (D80, D84); `.claude/rules/syntek-author/**`; root `CLAUDE.md`; root `.gitattributes`; anything in `handoffs/` but the family's pair (D80); anything under `how-to/` but the thirteen family paths and media's three `syntek-media/` folders, and any folder there named for another template (D84); `learning/`, `assets/`, `build/` at the root; `.github/**` in `template/`; `LICENSE`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` at the root of `template/` (D83); `.claude/agents/`, `.claude/commands/`; `manuscript/`, `library/`, `planning/`, `research/`, `proposal/`, `typeset/`, `world/`, `standards/`, `tooling/`; any folder named as one of syntek-author's 52 skills at the baseline `dfe9528` (syntek-author `v0.7.0`, D81) (accounting-documents, adapt-section, add-reference, add-word, approach-a-reader, argument-audit, build, build-language, business-documents, category-check, causality, character-voice, chart-character-arc, clause-consistency, comprehension, continuity, create-creature, create-name, design-quest, design-script, draft-section, email-documents, fact-check, flow, grammar, grill-me, grill-with-docs, grilling, handoff, improve-section, learn-voice, legal-documents, msp-scp-documents, obligation-check, pacing, promote-section, pronounce, prototype, research, run-workflow, sensitivity-pass, social-media-documents, spelling, steelman, structure-review, teach, to-questionnaire, tone, tradition-check, typeset, wait-what, wayfinder), except the seven family-shared skills (grill-me, grill-with-docs, grilling, handoff, to-questionnaire, wait-what, wayfinder), whose `SKILL.md` media ships byte for byte and beside which it writes only its `MEDIA-<KIND>.md` mode files (D80). `syntek-author-names.txt` is the authority for this list (D27); `coexist-test.sh` checks 6, 13 and 20, `shipped-brands.sh` checks 5, 11 and, under `how-to/`, 16 (D84) and `skill-conformance.sh` check 20 prove it; and `shipped-brands.sh` check 8 proves, on every standalone render, that nothing `SM_NEVER` lists in `_common.sh` ships: the root `CLAUDE.md` and `.gitattributes`, `.github/`, `.claude/agents/`, `.claude/commands/` and the four root files of D83 among them. **A release is never tagged while `coexist-test.sh` reports SKIP** (D27), which, since 0.6.0, `run-all.sh`'s summary lists by name and counts (D81).

---

## 10. Hazards

**Never template:** real brand or person names, channel handles, URLs and account IDs, ElevenLabs voice IDs, API keys, Claude Design links, follower or view counts, prices, and any binary (`template/` ships no audio, video, image or font). **Ship empty:** every seed, register, log and manifest.
**An update does not stop on a collision.** A copy stops on a template-owned path that already exists; an update does not: Copier forces overwrite on update, so a later media release that adds a path syntek-author renders exits 0 and leaves three-way conflict markers inside syntek-author's file (mechanics review probe `upd.sh`, Copier 9.18.2). Every new top-level folder, `.claude/` path, skill folder or rules file is checked against `syntek-author-names.txt` before release, `coexist-test.sh` must PASS (never SKIP) for a tag, and the name list is refreshed whenever syntek-author releases.
**Never delete a shared file's update-gated `_exclude` line**: without it the file becomes seed-if-missing, and a shared file the author deleted is recreated from media's copy on the next update — inside syntek-author's project. **Never delete its `_skip_if_exists` line**: without it a copy over an existing file aborts. An update renders the old release with the old release's `_exclude` as well as the new one, so a project's copy is kept when a release stops shipping the file (probe `gate.sh`: file and both lines dropped, the author's file kept); the file is still never removed or renamed in a later release (R5), because a renamed shared file is a second shared path neither template seeds. A retired shared path keeps its file and both lines and gains an unconditional `_exclude` line (`/.claude/mcp_config.json`, D76).
**`how-to/` is shared file by file** (D84; syntek-author D56): each of its thirteen family paths, and the guide guard, is a copy-only shared path with its own two `copier.yml` lines, twenty-eight in all and permanent, never a line naming `/how-to` or a folder under it, which would freeze or drop media's own files there; a family path is never template-owned in either template, or the other template's update writes conflict markers into it (exit 0) and its second copy stops (exit 1); and media's own files lie only under its three `syntek-media/` folders (`shipped-brands.sh` check 16). **`how-to` must be a real folder**: a file there stops an update with `FileExistsError` or `NotADirectoryError` (exit 1) after Copier has written every path that sorts before it and moved the answers file forward, which is discarded as any stopped update is; a link outside the project stops it (exit 1); a link inside the project makes Copier write and delete the guide through it, and the update script restores nothing beyond it; on a case-insensitive disk a folder named in another case receives the files. Media's `v0.6.0` crossing report names a link, a case variant and an ignored `how-to`. **The guide is read-only, and every full update puts it back** (D84): the deny and the guide guard stop Claude Code's file tools (a session started in a subfolder escapes the anchored deny, never the guard, which runs on macOS, Linux and WSL), OpenCode is held by its deny once `--write` adds it and every other client by the rules alone; the restore replaces an owner's edit, which stays in Git (`HEAD`, `refs/syntek-media/yours`), never writes over another template's version of a shared file while it stands unchanged, and runs only on a full update, so a skipped run (`SYNTEK_UPDATE_REPORT=off`, `--pretend`, a failed render, a Python older than 3.14) restores nothing until the next; an untracked file at a guide path is committed or moved first, since Copier may write over it before any script runs where `status.showUntrackedFiles=no` hides it. A departure from the guide is recorded where rules 01 Section 10 says, never in the guide. **An older syntek-author beside media's guide** calls every `src/` the author's, lints `how-to/src/` by default and restores nothing (syntek-author Section 10), so a combined project runs syntek-author `v0.6.0` or later, updated before media, and a copy-only task warns where its guide files are absent (D84).
**Never ship a link a sandbox mounts read-only onto a writable folder**: Codex mounts `.agents` read-only and refuses it as a link to `.claude`, so `.agents` is a real folder and only `.agents/skills` is a link (D76). **A shared path changes in an existing project only through a migration the maintainer approved as a stated exception** (D11): the `v0.4.2` migration acts on the exact `.agents -> .claude` link, moves `.claude/mcp_config.json` only where its destination is absent, and reports everything else; the unversioned `agents-outside-link.py` pair renames only an `.agents` link that leaves the project, aside and back, never its target; and, since 0.6.0, the family update script, D11's third exception, writes a listed family-shared file only where the project never had it, replaces one only where it equals a revision of the project's render history, puts back what `how-to/` should hold, and adds each listed settings entry, top-level value and MCP server once, never removing, reordering or weakening one and never adding back one it counts as offered, as below (D81, D84). **An update that stops between the pair's stages leaves `.agents` held** as `.agents.syntek-held`: the next update refuses the changed tree until the link is put back by hand, in the order the hold stage prints before the render and `README.md` 'Migrations' gives (D76, D85). A bare `mv .agents.syntek-held .agents` while `.agents` stands would put the held link inside it, and so into the folder outside the project: media's own update never puts `.agents` back (D76), but a discard (`git reset --hard`) restores a committed link, and a stopped syntek-author update puts it back.
**An update that Copier's own cleanup stopped is never committed** (syntek-author Section 10). Where Copier's cleanup fails (`OSError: [Errno 39] Directory not empty` under the `.git/objects` of a temporary `copier._main.old_copy.…` or `copier._main.new_copy.…` folder), the update has written the new files and recorded the new version in `.copier-answers.syntek-media.yml`, but has run no after-stage migration: D76's `restore`, so an `.agents` link outside the project stays held; crossing 0.4.2, the folder migration and its report; crossing 0.6.0, media's how-to report (D84); and, last, the update script, so the update leaves no update report, no refresh of the shared files, no settings entry or MCP server added and no restore of the guide (D81). Nor does it print the message after the update. An update run over that state takes the new version as already reached and skips the release's migrations. The cause is Git's automatic maintenance, which a commit in Copier's own temporary render starts in the background and which may still be writing when Copier deletes the folder (intermittent; most likely with Git 2.54 or 2.55, and with several updates at once); it is Copier's to fix. `README.md` 'Updating a project' gives the update command, Copier run as `uvx --python '>=3.14' copier` (D81), with `gc.auto` and `maintenance.auto` off for that run (five `GIT_CONFIG_*` variables in front of it, for POSIX shells) and the recovery: put a held link back, discard the stopped update after a `git clean -n -d` dry run, and run it again. `_message_before_update` ends with a pointer to it, because it prints once the command has been typed and so cannot carry the variables. The update script's own renders, and every Git the audits start, set both off (syntek-author Section 10; `sm_git_quiet`, `_common.sh`).
**Never stop shipping a seed** without a migration that preserves it (probe h2: a filled seed was deleted silently). **Never add a root `.gitattributes`** or use `git lfs track`, which writes one: it would be a shared file neither template seeds. **The LFS tripwire writes the repository's `.git/config`** (D19): it is copy-only, never undone by media, and said in `_message_after_copy`. **Never negate a path back in under a gated directory** in `_exclude`, except the `README.md` of a README-only output folder (D42), whose negation is listed above every gated line, so a shut gate, matched last, still wins: Copier obeys the last pattern that matches, so a negation listed after a gate brings its path back with the gate shut (D19). **Never read `_external_data` anywhere but a validator, the family questions block's own defaults and `_message_after_update`** (D14, D80; probes c2c, c3), and never compare with `_external_data.prev` in `_message_after_update`: Copier re-reads it after writing the new answers, so it holds them. **Never let the root `.gitignore` seed match a shipped path**: it applies to `template/` in this repository too, so the snapshot would drop the file. **Never name a tracked file with an extension syntek-author's root `.gitignore` ignores** (Section 6.16). **Never put a tracked file in a per-piece folder**: each sits inside an ignored output folder, so a file there is never committed and no session reads it (D50); a tracked file stays flat in its tracked folder under its `<piece>.` name (D64). **A text capture can carry a home path or a host name**, and `scrub.sh` never runs in an author's project: the assets pair has them removed before the capture is committed (D69). **Never name an example path in a template-owned file** (D43). **The companions' own procedures expect syntek-author's section drafts and write ledger entries**: media's rules 02 says to run improve-section and adapt-section as report-then-agreed-edit with no ledger step, and `write-script`'s revise step is the fallback when a companion refuses a non-section file. **The first `/design-sync` on a plain HTML kit is unproven** (`VERIFY`; D18). **A permission added to `.claude/settings.json` reaches an existing project only through the update script** (D81): Copier never renders the copy-only file, so a rule, hook or top-level value arrives once, on an update that runs the script in full on Python 3.14, and only where `migrations/family-refresh.json` lists it and the new seed holds it; one the owner allows is reported, not added, and one the owner removed is never added back once media's record holds it or, where the record does not decide, once a committed version of the file held it (below). Until such an update, a project made before 0.4.4 lacks the two `voice remix run` asks, and its allow lets a remix spend credits unprompted; `check --setup` reports either one missing as a FAIL (D49, D82). The six allows, which no lane offers, and the data-root guard, which is never listed and reaches new copies only (syntek-author D17, D50), are added by hand (Section 8). **Approved generated audio is unrepeatable**: a `git clean -fdx` or a lost disk destroys paid-for takes that were never archived (D52). **The ElevenLabs base path must contain the project**: `$HOME` does not cover a repository kept on another disk (Section 8). **syntek-author is still moving**: its working tree was mid-v0.2.0 at the baseline, so every builder re-reads it, and `coexist-test.sh` check 20 catches name drift.
**Never edit a vendored family file or block here** (D80, D85): each comes from one syntek-author tag, and media's audits hold every byte to `family-shared.json`; a change goes to syntek-author first and arrives with its next tag, re-vendored with every other family file. That includes `opencode.json`'s edit denies: three on paths media never ships (`*.pdf`, `build/*`, `typeset/src/units/.base/*`), syntek-author D48's one exception, inert where the path is absent, and, since `v0.6.0`, a fourth, `how-to/*`, the guide's deny, which names a path media ships (D84). **A family-shared file reaches an existing project only through the update script** (D81): it writes a listed file the project never had and refreshes one the owner never changed, so until an update runs it in full on Python 3.14, a project made before 0.5.0 lacks the seven skills, the hooks and the two pairs, and every project made before 0.6.0 the shared how-to files and the guide guard; an edited one stays as edited and is reported, a sibling's version is kept and attributed to it, a deleted one stays deleted (outside `how-to/`, which the restore puts back, D84), and no client file ever arrives by update. So every media rule that routes to a family skill keeps a by-hand fallback (D10, D35). **One project, one data root**: the first data-root answers must equal syntek-author's, and a later difference in the store or the layout makes every data command of both templates refuse until the two agree (syntek-author D50), which `_message_after_update` reports (D80). **A data-root map that claims a folder another map names stops every data command that touches it** (exit 2): media's claims none, and a later claim (footage, approved takes) is a decision of its own, which must also settle what rules 06 Section 12 lets the resolver's `status` list (D50). **A hook's executable bit is part of its bytes**: Copier compares it, so a vendored hook without mode 755 is a conflict in a combined project; the manifest records the mode and the copy task sets it (D80).
**Media's update script needs Python 3.14** (D81; syntek-author D18): it and the outside-link script run on the Python that runs Copier and, below 3.14, print a framed message that they did NOT run and exit 0, so a `uvx` without `--python '>=3.14'` may give an update with no report, refresh, offer or restore and, over an outside `.agents` link, one that stops with `ForbiddenPathError`; the recovery is the one for any stopped update, run again with the flag. **'Once' is counted per template, from media's own record** (`refs/syntek-media/template`, kept in the clone); syntek-author's record is never read for it. The first update into 0.6.0 finds no such record, so the committed history of `.claude/settings.json` decides: `HEAD`'s, as `git log HEAD -- .claude/settings.json` walks it (Git's default history simplification, at most the 200 latest commits that touched the file), so a version held only on a side branch whose merge left the file as it was does not count. An entry a committed version held counts as offered, so one the owner committed and later removed stays removed, while one removed before it was ever committed is added. That update adds each listed hook, rule and value that the new render's `settings.json` also holds and the file never held, except that a hook is added only where every script it names is in `.claude/hooks/` (otherwise it is reported and offered again by the next update), a hook that names no script there, the data-root guard's among them, is never offered (syntek-author's `v0.7.0`), and a deny or ask rule the owner allows is reported, not added, and recorded as offered. From then on media's record decides for hooks and permission rules: one a later media release adds is added even where syntek-author offered it first and the owner removed it. A top-level value is different: where media's record does not list it, the committed history still decides, so a value a later release adds stays removed wherever a committed version held it; an MCP server media's record does not list is counted the same way, from the committed history of each MCP file (syntek-author D51). A clone without the record (a fresh clone: the records travel only with a mirror, below) falls back to the committed history for every entry. The update report says nothing about an entry it counts as offered because history held it; only the record shows it (`git log -1 --format=%B refs/syntek-media/template`). So media's lists stay as 0.6.0 sets them, and a top-level value a later release adds reaches only projects whose history never held it. **Never list the data-root guard in `migrations/family-refresh.json`**: the guard reaches new copies only (syntek-author D17, D50), and since syntek-author's `v0.7.0` the script never offers, records or reports a hook entry that names no script in `.claude/hooks/`, as the guard's does (syntek-author D51), so media's never listing it is a second defence, which does not rest on the script; `shipped-seeds.sh` check 31 refuses it, and holds every listed hook and rule to the seed, without which it is never offered. **The update script's records stay in the clone**: `refs/syntek-media/template` and `refs/syntek-media/yours` live inside `.git`, where a plain push, fetch or clone never carries them and a mirror push, `git clone --mirror` or `git bundle create --all` does; `yours` keeps the owner's `HEAD` at the last update reachable, so a file since removed from history stays in the clone, and both refs are deleted (`git update-ref -d`) before history is rewritten to remove a secret (syntek-author D52 and its Section 10). **An update counts as not made until it is committed**: a discarded one is safely run again and offers again what it offered, so an entry removed from its uncommitted result comes back; and the record is kept per clone, not per branch, so updates run on one line of history and other branches take them by merge. **The two MCP servers reach `opencode.json` and `.gemini/settings.json` only once each holds every ask form the permissions script derives**, because OpenCode lets the last matching rule win; until then the report gives `python3 .claude/syntek-media-permissions.py --write`, and the next update adds them (syntek-author D51). **Offer-once never delivers a pin bump**: a later server pin reaches an existing project by hand until a release refreshes an unedited server by its recorded hash (Section 11).
**A show's feed URL and every GUID are for life** (D58, D59): a feed that moves needs a redirect and `new_feed_url` for at least `platform.podcast.feed_move_min_days`, and a GUID recomputed or retyped makes every directory list the episode twice; `feed new` and `feed add` write them once, `feed new --rekey` is refused once anything is published, and `feed write` and `feed check` refuse a GUID that vanished from the tracked feed. **The tracked feed is written only after the author reports the feed live**, and only by `feed write -o` after that comparison: a feed written first and checked after would compare itself with itself. **A corrected episode file gets a new URL**, never the old one overwritten: Spotify fetches a file again only when its path changes, and YouTube never replaces ingested audio; `feed write` and `feed check` refuse a changed length under an old URL, and `feed tag` on a published row says so. **One feed per show, on a site the brand owns**: another site may carry an episode page that plays the same audio, as a placement with its own agreement (D61), never a second feed. **The feed is uploaded last**, after every file it names, and at the episode's publication time, never before: a static feed has no clock. **Never put a real site, list, show, sender or address in `template/` or `examples/`** (D36): the template's examples use Harbour Lane Studio and `example.com`. **Media never edits a website repository or sends an email** (D55): a snippet goes to the author in chat.

## 11. Deferred (not in v0.1, v0.2 or v0.3)

Direct-API SRT, ElevenLabs forced alignment and text-to-speech with timestamps (each needs a direct API call, and the voice remix is the one D21 allows, D22, D82); local speech-to-text of a recording (`transcribe` aligns words already known, and its cross-check only compares them, D67); an ElevenLabs music and sound-effects skill (`compose_music`, `text_to_sound_effects`); transitions beyond a cross-fade, motion beyond a slow push-in, and multi-track picture in `assemble` (motion drawn as code is a scene's and no longer deferred: D70); a React component kit for `/design-sync`; Git LFS for source footage (external storage is the default); dubbing and other caption languages (en-GB only); posting through platform APIs (TikTok's `is_aigc` field and the rest; D41); ACX's voice-replica route for an author's own cloned voice (`VERIFY`); making INaudio's LPF package from the template (it comes from ElevenLabs' own product, D23); researched thumbnail sizes for LinkedIn, Facebook and TikTok (until then the video deliverable's size, `VERIFY`); a toolkit count of a post package's fields against their `platforms.toml` keys (`media.py post check`; until then `prepare-post` counts exactly, by hand, never by eye); adoption scripts and any migration beyond D76's `v0.4.2` that moves author work (none needed until a folder that can hold author work moves; the per-piece output layout moved only what Git ignores, D65; media's `v0.6.0` report and the family 'update' entry move nothing, D81, D84); a media PreCompact hook of its own; a project settings file of media's own (syntek-author's `00-project.md` serves a combined project, D5).
**Deferred by the 0.2.0 work:** a WebM (AV1 or VP9) source offered before the MP4, and `container_alt`/`video_codec_alt` keys to drive it (D57); a value that differs per site (an override with a site field, D53); audio-described versions of video for WCAG 2.2 SC 1.2.5 (D60); WebVTT voice spans (`<v Name>`) for Apple's speaker labels; the 4:3 and 1:1 crops Google's Article structured data also asks for; video in the self-hosted feed (Apple's HLS route, `podcast:alternateEnclosure`) and video on Spotify; download measurement; `podcast:person`, `podroll` and `value`; transcripts and captions in other languages; a second Apple category (whether Apple reads one from RSS is `VERIFY`); an online `feed check` (HEAD, ranges, CORS), which the guide gives as `curl` commands instead; a featured image for a text-only post (a Claude Design export through `brand/workflows/03-record-a-design-export/` until then); a thumbnail brief per placement, for an image in a partner site's own branding (a design export until then, D61); a register field linking an episode to its YouTube upload (the package's On YouTube bullet until then, D58); a blog-post or newsletter-issue document type, which is syntek-author's to add; a house weight budget for blog images, which is the brand's override where it wants one.
**Deferred by the 0.3.0 work:** recorded video inside a scene (the page loads only the images and text captures `<piece>.real.json` indexes, D70; a piece that shows recorded video is a footage piece, assembled, D47); WebVTT chapter tracks for video on the brand's own sites (a video's chapters are lines of its post package, `youtube.long` only, Section 6.12).
**Deferred by the 0.4.4 work (D82):** multi-voice takes in one piece (a voice per segment, and Voice Changer takes made from a recording with `speech_to_speech`), so a segment register keeps one `voice_use`; the remix's iteration parameters (a remix session, `prompt_strength`, `seed`, `guidance_scale`, `loudness`), which `voice remix run` never sends; and saving a preview through the API, which stays MCP's `create_voice_from_preview`.
**Deferred by the community-files work (D83):** a third-party notices file (none is needed while `template/` holds no file under a licence of its own; a file that arrives with one keeps it, and the licence paragraph names it, as syntek-author's does); DCO sign-off (the inbound = outbound paragraph of `CONTRIBUTING.md` and the pull-request checkbox stand instead); a trademark sentence or policy, left to the family so that every family repository says the same; and a custom form for private vulnerability reports, `.github/VULNERABILITY_REPORT.yml`, which is outside the family's file set (`SECURITY.md` lists what a report should include).
**Deferred by the 0.5.0 work (D80)**, of which D81 delivers the first three in 0.6.0 (`migrations/family-update.py` with its 'update' block and `baseline` task and media's own `migrations/family-refresh.json`; syntek-author's later bytes, from its `v0.7.0` tag; Python 3.14 for media's own tools, with `transcribe.py` the exception), so that only these stay deferred: filing anything on the data root (footage and approved takes stay on `manifest.toml`, D19, D52); a `check --setup` report of the family's asks, deny and hooks (D49); mode files for `grill-me` and `wait-what`, for which syntek-author writes none.
**Deferred by the 0.6.0 work (D81):** the hooks finding the project root through `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, for every hook entry, with syntek-author, whose `v0.7.0` did not take it; refreshing an offered MCP server the owner never changed, by its recorded hash, so that a pin bump reaches existing projects (syntek-author D54's deferral).
**Deferred by D84:** a coexist case that copies media over a real syntek-author older than `v0.6.0` and runs the whole flow, for which `coexist-test.sh` check 23's render without its guide stands in; an opt-out for the guide; folder overrides (`docs/project/`, `workflows/local/`) inside `how-to/`; a restore at copy time; reporting on every update, not only on the crossing, the files under `how-to/` that no template ships; routing the how-to procedures through `run-media-workflow`; more shared chapters; a guide guard for native Windows (syntek-author D56 defers the same).
